# riscv-small Core Specification

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Status | draft |
| Version | v0.8.8 |
| Date | 2026-11-21 |
| Owner | designer — spec and ISA gaps; verification lead — DV gaps; every target 2026-12-31 (`A-09`, `Q-12`) |
| Last reviewed | never — designer review requested |
| Baseline | commit `bb789bb` (v0.1, as-built); v0.2 to v0.7 add intent only, no RTL change |
| Source | elicitation + reconstruction |
| Normative ISA | RISC-V ISA **v20260120**, volume I (unprivileged) and volume II (privileged); the debug rows cite the RISC-V **Debug Specification v1.0** (ratified 2025-02-21), a separate document (§0.7) |

> **Provenance.** Two kinds of row live in this document, and the status tells them apart.
> Rows marked `implemented`, `partial` or `verified` are **reverse-engineered** from `rtl/` and `tb/`; they cite
> `file:line` and record today's behaviour, not anyone's intent. Rows marked `deferred`, the decision table
> in §0.1, and the target ISA table in §7.1 come from the **2026-10-28 elicitation rounds**; they record the
> target, not the RTL. Where the two disagree, the difference is a gap (`G-xx`), never an edit to either
> side. Nothing became `implemented` in the intent-only revisions (v0.2 to v0.7): no RTL changed there.
>
> The four status values are shared with [verification.md](verification.md) and mean the same in both files:
> `implemented` = the RTL does what the clause says and no gap contradicts it; `partial` = the clause executes
> in the RTL but a named gap shows it wrong for part of its domain, and the gap is cited in the row;
> `verified` = a test in this repository observes it; `deferred` = the capability is absent and the clause
> states the target.
>
> **Where a claim comes from, since v0.4.** A row the standard decides cites the normative text by volume and
> section. A row the standard leaves open says so and records a **project decision** with its reason. The two
> are never mixed silently: `x0` (volume I §1.1.1) and misaligned support (volume I §1.1.6) are cited rules,
> while the reset style is a project decision (`D-09`, `A-06`) precisely because the ISA says nothing about it.

## Change log

Line pins inside a change-log row are as of the revision that row describes — the tree as it stood then, never
re-pinned, because a dated row that claimed to describe a later file would be the ambiguity this convention
exists to remove; the pins in the normative sections (§3 to §9) are as of the current revision, the one in this
document's header.

Adds seven requirements — `FR-43` (`M`), `FR-44` (`C`), `FR-45` (`MRET`), `FR-46` (`WFI`), `FR-47` (`Zicsr`), `FR-48` (interrupt entry, masking and priority), `FR-49` (`Zicntr`) — and the ten obligations they and the existing rows were missing (`mepc` WARL and `IALIGN`, `ECALL`/`EBREAK` causes 11 and 3, `misa.MXL`, `mhartid` zero, `SC` semantics, the reset vector, `FENCE`'s device ordering and reserved configurations, the `rv32um`/`rv32uc`/`rv32mi` signoff suites, and the `(base & ~3) + 4` second beat). `A-17` and `A-18` confirmed (`Q-04` and `Q-27` answered by the standard); `Q-30`..`Q-35` opened and cited where a clause depends on them. Adds §0.7, the citation list. No requirement status promoted | spec-author (conformance) | designer — conformance review |
| 2026-10-28 | v0.7 | **WP1 — machine mode for an RTOS, and `misa`.** The round-7 answers are applied, not re-derived (`Q-30`..`Q-35`): `misa` is read-only and a write is ignored rather than trapped (`FR-37`, `Q-30`); `WFI` is a no-operation (`FR-46`, `Q-31`); `mtval` is read-only zero (`FR-52`, `Q-32`); the reset vector is the `RESET_VECTOR` parameter with default `0x8000_0000` (`FR-41`, §5.2, `Q-33`); the product claims no profile (`A-19`, `Q-34`); and a trap request carries its cause on a bus beside three separate level-sensitive interrupt inputs (`FR-22`, `FR-39`, `FR-40`, §6, `Q-35`). Adds three requirements — `FR-50` (the machine-mode CSR file and its address map), `FR-51` (`mscratch`) and `FR-52` (`mtval`) — plus assumption `A-19` (machine-mode-only scope, `confirmed`) and constraint `C-15` (what that scope removes from the CSR and trap surface). Extends `FR-22`, `FR-37`, `FR-39`, `FR-40`, `FR-41`, `FR-46` and `FR-48`; §1 names machine-mode completeness a phase-1 deliverable, §5.2 gains the `RESET_VECTOR` row, §6 gains the trap-request rows and §7.1 names the requirement behind every machine-mode row. Adds §0.8 and three rows to §0.7. `FR-50` carries the whole address map — trap and status, the machine counters and their read-only views, the four identification registers the standard makes readable or mandatory, and the performance counters that are legally read-only zero — and states the write rule by address rather than by register: a write to a `csr[11:10] = 2'b11` address traps, while a write to a read/write address whose bits are read-only (`mtval` 0x343, `mstatush` 0x310, `misa`) is accepted and ignored. That reading, and the one it replaced, are recorded in §0.8. No status changed: the RTL has no CSR file (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:351-353` decodes the system group as a no-operation, `rtl/control.sv:297-311` ties the vector to a constant and the cause to zero), so nothing is presented as built and `FR-50`..`FR-52` arrive `deferred`. **WP2 — debug, appended in the same version and not a second bump.** Applies `Q-36`..`Q-42` rather than re-deriving them: an external Debug Module behind a JTAG DTM with the hart side in the core (`C-13`, `D-10`), a `dbg_auth` strap that gates debug on the hart side as an addition to the authentication the Debug Specification places in the DM (`C-14`, `D-11`), `NUM_TRIGGERS` of 0, 2 or 4 with `mcontrol6` (`FR-57`), `resethaltreq` (`FR-54`) and a dedicated `mtime` input for the `Zicntr` views (`FR-49`, `Q-42`). Adds seven requirements — `FR-53` (debug mode and the core debug registers), `FR-54` (run-control handshake, hart side), `FR-55` (abstract commands, hart side), `FR-56` (program buffer), `FR-57` (`Sdtrig` triggers), `FR-58` (single step) and `FR-59` (the `dbg_auth` gate), all `deferred` — plus constraints `C-13` and `C-14`, decisions `D-10` and `D-11`, two §5.2 parameters and the `dbg_auth` strap, the debug port group and `mtime` in §6, the debug and trigger rows of §7 and §7.1, and the Debug Specification v1.0 rows of §0.7. Extends `FR-24`, `FR-25`, `FR-48`, `FR-49` and `FR-50` without changing any of their statuses. No status changed anywhere: `rtl/` has no debug port, no trigger unit, no debug CSR and no debug-mode state, so all seven rows are targets and trace stays untargeted | spec-author (elicitation, round 7 and checkpoint) | designer — round 7 answers |
| 2026-10-28 | v0.7.1 | **Debug rows re-read against the standard**, not an interview: four defects removed from WP2 without adding or retiring a requirement (still 59 `FR` rows, none promoted). `FR-53` now states the `dcsr.cause` encodings by number from §4.1.1 Table 2 — 1 `ebreak`, 2 trigger, 3 `haltreq`, 5 `resethaltreq`, 4 step, with the halt-group cause 6 explicitly absent and the priority `resethaltreq` > `haltreq` > trigger > `ebreak` > step — and gives the `dpc` value per cause from §4.1.9 Table 3, replacing the paragraph that declared the halt case unfixed; the `dpc` read/write rule and the `IALIGN` dependence of its low bits come from the same section, which matters while `IALIGN` = 32 (`FR-44`). `FR-57` makes the trigger unit conditional on `NUM_TRIGGERS`: with 0 the five addresses 0x7A0–0x7A4 are unimplemented and trap (Sdext ch. 4, §5.1.1), replacing the earlier wording in which the unit was said to report an empty trigger list; with more than 0 `tselect` is WARL and an index at or beyond the count is observed through `tdata1.type` = 0 rather than through `tselect` reading back (§5.1.7); `tinfo` is named optional-and-provided. `FR-50` gains the existence rule for the five trigger addresses and its trigger-window acceptance is qualified by the parameter. `FR-54` replaces the circular "the state §3.1.5 defines" with observables — not halted, halt requests ignored and not remembered, `dcsr`/`dpc` at reset values, the last as a stated project choice. `FR-58` removes the unsupported claim that the hart clears `dcsr.step`; the debugger sets and clears it, and the row adds the §4.1.5 trigger and trap interactions, the latter with `mtval` held at 0 (`FR-52`). §0.7 citation rows extended, not added | spec-author (conformance) | designer — review requested |
| 2026-10-28 | v0.7.2 | **Trigger `action` encoding corrected**, verified at the source rather than in an interview: `FR-57` and `FR-59` carried the two actions of Debug Spec v1.0 **§5.1.2 Table 1** swapped. The table reads **action 0** — "Raise a breakpoint exception. ... xepc must contain the virtual address of the next instruction that must be executed to preserve the program flow" — and **action 1** — "Enter Debug Mode. dpc must contain the virtual address of the next instruction that must be executed to preserve the program flow. This action is only legal when the trigger's dmode is 1. Since tdata1 is WARL, hardware must prevent it from containing dmode=0 and action=1." `FR-57` now states both encodings by number, adds the `dmode` rule and the WARL consequence (`tdata1` shall never read back `dmode` = 0 with action = 1), and adds §5.1's "Triggers do not fire while in Debug Mode"; its acceptance arms action 1 to enter debug mode and action 0 to raise cause 3, writes the forbidden `dmode`/action pair, and halts the hart to show that no event fires in debug mode. `FR-59` had its acceptance inverted with the row: with `dbg_auth` = 0 it is the **action-1** trigger that becomes inert, because entering debug mode is that action's only effect, while an **action-0** trigger still raises cause 3. `FR-53` and `FR-58` were checked and already agree — `FR-53` names the action without a number, and `FR-58` quotes §4.1.5's "action=1" for the step case — so neither text changed. The ch. 5 citation of §0.7 now names §5.1, §5.1.2 and Table 1. Still 59 `FR` rows (13 implemented, 8 partial, 2 verified, 36 deferred) and no status changed | spec-author (conformance) | designer — review requested |

| 2026-11-04 | v0.7.6 | **WP-4c (E1c) forwarding, branch-redirect and signed-offset evidence**: `G-27` (the first instruction at a taken branch target was never fetched), `G-26` (the branch compare read stale operands, because the jump-forward selects were unconnected and the EX forward source was undriven) and `G-20` (the jump/branch target added an unsigned immediate slice) are **closed**. The three new harness tests pass — `T-10` (`sanity:rv32ui-p-and`, mutant `branch-redirect`), `T-11` (`sanity:rv32ui-p-addi`, mutant `forward-rs1`) and `T-12` (`sanity:rv32ui-p-beq`, mutant `negative-branch`) — the five baseline tests `T-01`/`T-02`/`T-06`/`T-08`/`T-09` pass, `make regress` is 8/8 at `rtl_digest` `sha256:d12dc1a2…`, and each of the three negative controls fails its own test, so check 11 is real. `NFR-05` is re-measured at **34/42** on the same digest; the eight remaining failures are `G-15` (`fence_i`), `G-07` (`jalr`), `G-19` (`lb`/`lbu`, the load-use stall this run isolated), `G-08` (`sb`/`sh`/`st_ld`) and `FR-42` (`ma_data`). Statuses: `FR-02` (`T-11`), `FR-08` (`T-12` and the six branch images), `FR-16` (`T-11`) and `FR-18` (`T-08`) are `verified`; `FR-06` and `FR-19` move `partial` → `implemented` because `G-20` and `G-26` were their only named gaps and no named test observes their acceptance in full; `FR-07` (`G-07`), `FR-11` (`G-08`) and `FR-17` (`G-19`) stay `partial`. `FR-13` is **not** promoted: its acceptance asks for offsets of `-2048` and `+2047` on a non-zero base at the data port, and neither `T-01` (base `x0`, positive offsets) nor `T-08` (a two-instruction store image) presents them. The `verified` reading is stated explicitly from this revision: a row is `verified` when one named test observes its **main** acceptance cell, and the arms that test does not exercise are carried by a gap instead of withholding the status — `G-04` for `FR-02`'s other five immediate encodings, `FR-16`'s `rs2` and distance-2 cells and `FR-18`'s distance-2, and `G-06` for `FR-08`'s `IALIGN=32` target-alignment arm, which its row already books there. No requirement or acceptance text changed; the `G-19` row gains the `lb`/`lbu` evidence and stays open. Two document-level corrections ride in the same revision: the §4.1 `0100011` row no longer claims the access width is "the low two bits of `funct3`" — the one-bit cast `logic'(rd_wr_ctrl)` (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:127`) carries `funct3[0]` zero-extended, so `00` is byte **and word** and a word access reads `2'b00` (`FR-11`, `G-08`; the reading [uarch.md](uarch.md) §8 and [verification.md](verification.md) already carried) — and the `G-26` statement names the real mechanism (the unconnected `.jmp_src1`/`.jmp_src2` and the undriven `alu_ex`, so the branch compare read stale operands) instead of calling the ALU forward broken; and `NFR-06` moves `deferred` → `partial` on the 91.67 % measurement it already had (`doc/measurements/NFR-06-coverage.md`, `build/cov_report/summary.json`: 4 coverpoints, one bin open, `cg_mem.cp_width.word_w`, the `FR-11` width defect) | spec-author | `WP-4c` gate; release still `blocked` (`NFR-05` 34/42) |
| 2026-11-04 | v0.7.7 | **Reference model architecture fixed for `E3` (`Q-43`)**: the verification reference model shall be driven only by monitor observations and shall run unchanged in a passive agent; the program image is configuration, not a transaction. Adds constraint `C-16` and gap `G-28` (no retire/commit observation: fetch != retire, so a fetch-driven model would execute instructions the core discarded, and a coverage bin can be satisfied by a flushed fetch). Records the current deviation — `program_port` (`tb/env/top/riscv_small_env.sv:36`, `:86`) feeds the model from the sequence (`tb/env/ref_model/riscv_small_ref_model.sv:48-52`) — and assigns the fix (retire observation, delete `program_port`) to `E3`. | 2026-11-18 | v0.8.2 | **WP-9 — the Spike commit-trace comparison (`E3`'s external reference): `G-04` is narrowed and stays open.** The core already carried `valid`, `pc` and the instruction word to write-back (WP-8); WP-9 adds the reference that stream was built for. The monitor dumps one **canonical commit line** per retired instruction — `pc`, `rd`, value and the instruction word, with a dash where there is no register write — under `+RETIRE_LOG`, Spike runs the same image with `--log-commits`, `scripts/verify/spike/normalize.py` reduces both sides to that line and `scripts/verify/spike/compare.py` matches them **by commit index on all four fields**, cutting both traces at the first commit whose `pc` is a `<tohost>` store — a **set** derived from the sanity dump at run time, never a literal (`add` `0x568`/`0x574`, `beq` `0x348`/`0x354`, `st_ld` `0x774`/`0x784`). Three manifest rows are the test — `T-47` (`rv32ui-p-add`, **458/458** commits, mutant `alu-ignore-rs2`), `T-48` (`rv32ui-p-beq`, **284/284**, `negative-branch`) and `T-49` (`rv32ui-p-st_ld`, **477/477**, `store-lane-offset`) — declared by `scripts/verify/spike_trace.cmake` under the `spike` label so the comparison itself is the assertion, and counted by the regression. **`T-46` (`riscv_small_skip3_model_test`, mutant `retire-every-slot`) is the fourth row of the package and the one that fixed the retire checker's invariant:** its taken branch skips **three** words, so the two fetch slots the front end discards no longer equal the number of words the program skips — 12 words placed, 9 executed, 11 fetched, `fetch − commit` = 2 against 3 skipped — and claim 6 became a per-`pc` **set** check while claim 7, the count form `fetch − commit == \|skip_pc ∩ fetched_pcs\|`, is asserted only on the runs where it is still a constant and reported as "NOT asserted" otherwise; `T-45` had satisfied the count form by coincidence. The regression is therefore **45/45** — WP-8's 41 rows plus `T-46` and `T-47`..`T-49` — and `T-46` fails on its mutant like the other three; `make spike-trace` is 3/3, `make sanity` **42 of 43** (`rv32ui-p-ma_data`, `FR-42`) and `make coverage` **50 of 50** report bins and **40 of 40** `A-09` bar bins, at RTL `sha256:120b211f…` and TB `sha256:c8ba82d7…`. **`G-04` is narrowed, not closed.** The comparison sees a commit's `pc`, its instruction, its `rd` and the value it wrote, so for three images the instruction stream is now checked against an implementation this project did not write; it **cannot** see a store's address or data (the canonical line drops Spike's `mem` suffix on purpose, so `store-width-cast` still yields an identical 477/477 trace on `st_ld`), it never reports a commit that writes `x0` (the dump suppresses `rd = x0` by construction), it carries no bus protocol (`FR-20`, `FR-21`), and it spans **3 of the 43 images**. The six as-built rows `G-04` holds — `FR-13`, `FR-14`, `FR-15`, `FR-19`, `FR-20` and `FR-21` — therefore **all stay `implemented`**, the row stays a **release blocker**, and the release stays `blocked` by `G-04` and `G-11`. `T-47`..`T-49` are added to the Evidence cells of `FR-09`, `FR-10`, `FR-11` and `FR-12` as **corroboration only**. No `FR`/`NFR` status, acceptance cell or requirement clause changes: the 59 `FR` rows keep **14 `verified`, 6 `implemented`, 3 `partial` and 36 `deferred`**, before and after. **A re-base was applied to this revision after the `docs-auditor` pass, and it adds no version row either:** the `file:line` citations of this document were re-based against the RTL that WP-8 (`ba4a8c2`) had moved — **73 lines over five files**, notably the decode arms of `rtl/instruction_decode.sv` — because WP-8 re-based only `doc/uarch.md`, and no requirement text, acceptance cell, status or count changed, the 59 `FR` rows keeping **14 `verified`, 6 `implemented`, 3 `partial` and 36 `deferred`**. | spec-author | designer — pending |

| 2026-11-18 | v0.8.4 | **WP-11 P3a — `G-33` closed for the scope its evidence covers.** The reset vector is built and consumed: `rtl/riscv_small.sv:43` declares `RESET_VECTOR` as a `dataBus_t` parameter defaulting to `32'h8000_0000` and hands it to the fetch stage at `:139`, `rtl/instruction_fetch.sv:47` carries it, and `rtl/instruction_fetch.sv:110` now reads `pc <= RESET_VECTOR;` where it read `pc <= 'b0;`, so the first fetch after reset is `0x8000_0000` and not `0x0`. `T-50` (`riscv_small_reset_vector_test`, wp 11, requirements `FR-41,G-33`) is the observation: the checker captures the first fetch after reset at the instruction port and compares it with the elaborated parameter, and the `reset-vector-zero` mutant (`scripts/verify/mutants.tsv`, reverting the line to `pc <= 'b0;`) fails it, so check 11 proves the assignment is load-bearing. **The harness stopped working around the divergence:** `scripts/verify/sanity/link.ld` links every image at `0x80000000` — where the spec says the core leaves reset — `scripts/verify/sanity.cmake` rebases the hex with `objcopy --change-addresses=-0x80000000`, so word 0 of the hex is word 0 of the memory model while `tohost` keeps its absolute address and needs no mask, and Spike runs the same ELF with `--isa=rv32i --log=… --log-commits` only: `-m0:0x40000`, `--no-dm` and `--disable-dtb` are gone, because they existed only because `0x0` collides with the debug module `[0,0x1000)` and the device-tree ROM `[0x1000,0x2000)`. The six gate results, at RTL `sha256:b914f2e1…` and TB `sha256:c4bad285…`: `make regress` **46/46** (the 45 rows of v0.8.3 plus `T-50`, so the manifest carries 46 tests), `make negative-control` **44/44**, `make spike-trace` **3/3** (459 of 459 commits cut at `0x8000057c`, 285 of 285 at `0x8000035c`, 477 of 477 at `0x80000784`), `make sanity` **42/43** (`rv32ui-p-ma_data` fails at `testnum=1`, `FR-42`/`G-22`, unchanged), `make coverage` **50/50 report bins** and **40/40 `A-09` bar bins**. `G-33` closes on exactly what those numbers observe — the parameter exists and the reset consumes it — and **not** on parameter configurability: `T-50` runs the default, where `pc <= RESET_VECTOR` and `pc <= 32'h8000_0000` are indistinguishable and no mutant can separate them, so the "two different values of the parameter in two builds" clause of `FR-41`'s acceptance has no observation and the second elaboration is deferred by the designer on 2026-11-18 (`.opencode/plan/wp-11-reset-vector.md` §9.1). **No status moved:** the 59 `FR` rows keep **14 `verified`, 6 `implemented`, 3 `partial` and 36 `deferred`**, `FR-41` stays `partial` on `G-21` and on that unproven arm, and the release blockers stay `G-04` and `G-11`. The dangling `T-03` reference is gone from `FR-41`'s acceptance, which now names `T-50` for the default value and states the two-build arm as unobserved instead of dropping it; `FR-41`'s Evidence cell, the §4 lead-in for the `partial` rows, §5.2, the `Reset vector` row of §7.1, `FR-34`'s evidence and the §8 orphan list say what is now built rather than claiming the identifier appears nowhere under `rtl/`. **A wording correction was applied to this revision after it was written, and it adds no version row:** `T-50` compares the first fetch after reset with the value the DUT elaborated with (`duv.RESET_VECTOR`, published at `tb/riscv_small_tb.sv:88`), so it passes at **any** default and says nothing about the number `0x8000_0000`; every sentence that could be read as `T-50` observing that default now says what it does observe — the reset consumes the parameter, in a run at the default value. The sites are `FR-41`'s acceptance cell, the §4 lead-in for the `partial` rows, the two paragraphs of §5.2 that follow the parameter table, the `Reset vector` row of §7.1 and the §8 orphan list; `G-33`'s own row and this log entry already carried the distinction. | 2026-11-18 | v0.8.5 | **Testbench fix, RTL untouched — the TB digest moved and the RTL digest did not.** `T-50`'s `reset-vector-zero` mutant was being caught by a **timeout** rather than by its own checker: the directed driver answered a fetch outside the program with a NOP but never asserted `inst_ready`, so a core resetting to an address the program does not occupy stalled and the monitor published nothing to compare — red for the wrong reason, and a red that would survive a broken checker. `tb/env/agents/riscv_small_agent/riscv_small_driver.sv` now asserts `inst_ready` for an unmatched fetch **before the program has been entered**, and warns once instead of once per fetch (196 warnings on the failing run). The narrow guard is load-bearing, not caution: an unconditional answer was measured to add fetch pairs after the program ends and to switch off the retire checker's asserted count claim on `T-06` and `T-46`, so the driver answers only the window in which the core cannot yet have seen the program. The mutant now fails the way it should — `UVM_ERROR [RESET_VECTOR] … pc=0x00000000; RESET_VECTOR expects 0x80000000` — with `RESET_TIMEOUT` count **0**, and a diff of every checker line across all 46 tests before and after the fix is **identical**, so no other test's outcome moved. The gates after the fix, at RTL `sha256:b914f2e1…` and TB `sha256:066dce85…`: `make regress` **46/46**, `make negative-control` **44/44**, `make sanity` **42/43** (`rv32ui-p-ma_data`, `testnum=1`, `FR-42`/`G-22`, unchanged), `make coverage` **50 of 50 report bins** and **40 of 40 `A-09` bar bins**, `make spike-trace` **3/3**, and `make signoff-check SIGNOFF_ARGS="--wp 11"` **signoff-ready**. **Only evidence digests move:** the five sites that carried the old TB digest — the §5 lead measurement paragraph, the `NFR-05` and `NFR-06` Method cells, `A-09`'s last re-measurement sentence and `G-33`'s evidence — now read `sha256:066dce85…`, and the two measurement files carry the re-run with the same verdicts. `rtl_digest` stays `sha256:b914f2e1…` because no file under `rtl/` changed. No `FR`/`NFR` status, acceptance cell, requirement clause or count changes: the 59 `FR` rows keep **14 `verified`, 6 `implemented`, 3 `partial` and 36 `deferred`**, `NFR-05` and `NFR-06` stay `partial`, `FR-41` stays `partial` on `G-21` and on the unproven two-build arm of its acceptance, and the release blockers stay `G-04` and `G-11` | `verif-author` (driver) → `spec-author` (re-stamp) | designer — review requested |
| 2026-11-18 | v0.8.6 | **`NFR-07` measured, and the second non-functional row to leave `deferred`.** The FPGA/synthesis flow was **generalised** so it could be pointed at this core: `FPGA_TOP`, `FPGA_PROJECT_NAME` and `FPGA_PART` are cache variables (`CMakeLists.txt:58-60`, the part recorded here because a number without its part is not evidence), the source list is the `srclist/<top>.srclist` expansion instead of a hand-kept list, and board files are optional — so `make lint_fpga` now lints and synthesises `riscv_small` and no longer the SoC the flow was borrowed from. `scripts/fpga/run_lint.tcl` runs **two** passes, because the flags interact: `synth_design -lint` reports but leaves `current_design` empty, and `synth_design` opens the design but skips the linter; it prints one machine-readable `LINT_SUMMARY` line, refuses to report when the design is not open (an empty design is not "zero latches"), counts **latches by `REF_NAME`** (`LDCE`/`LDPE`/`LDNE`) rather than by `PRIMITIVE_TYPE =~ *LATCH*` — a filter that also matches every flip-flop and reported **1405 latches** on a design that has none, at a cost of four wrong measurements, so the rule is written into the method — counts flip-flops as the denominator, and reads combinational loops from the DRC rule `LUTLP-1`'s own `Violations found:` line instead of from a timing query. **Measured 2026-11-18 at RTL `sha256:b914f2e1…`:** Vivado 2024.1, part `xczu3eg-sbva484-1-i`, out-of-context, default synthesis settings, 10 sources, **4069 cells**, **1405 flip-flops** (`FDRE` 995 + `FDCE` 408 + `FDPE` 2), **0 inferred latches**, **0 combinational loops**, linter `28 Infos, 9 Warnings, 0 Critical Warnings, 0 Errors`, netlist pass `60 Infos, 45 Warnings, 1 Critical Warning, 0 Errors` (`build/vivado.log:354`, `doc/measurements/NFR-07-lint.md`). The single critical warning, `[Synth 8-5799] Converted tricell instance 'u_controli_2086' to logic`, is the **out-of-context mode** converting a boundary tricell because the block has no package pins, and is reported as such rather than omitted. **`NFR-07` moves `deferred` -> `verified`, on its acceptance and not on the file's existence:** the target is a number this document already fixed at zero, and both clauses measured zero; the arms nothing observed are booked as limits in the row itself — **one part** (this document records no target part, `A-08`) and **the only configuration that can be built**, since the three named configurations of §5.2 cannot be elaborated (`G-13`, `G-15`, `G-16`), so each one that becomes buildable needs its own run. **`G-05` is narrowed, not closed**: its statement is the absence of **timing, area and power** evidence, and `NFR-01`..`NFR-04` are untouched by a latch count. The five linter messages — `INFER-2` at `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:264` (synthesis inferred **no** latch, the case assigns its signals beforehand, so it is the linter's latch-risk signal), `RESET-2` on `data_rd_en_ex_reg`, two `ASSIGN-5` partial assignments (`rd0_data`, `rs2_ma`) and one `ASSIGN-6` unread signal (`valid_wb`) — are recorded in the measurement file as findings to triage and not fixed here; the two registers the netlist pass removes, `sepc` and `scause` (`rtl/control.sv:298-310`), are the synthesis view of `G-01`/`G-02` and not a new gap. §8's unimplemented list drops `NFR-07`, and §9 narrates the measurement. **No `FR` requirement, acceptance cell or count changes: the 59 `FR` rows keep 14 `verified`, 6 `implemented`, 3 `partial` and 36 `deferred`.** | spec-author | designer — review requested |
| 2026-11-19 | v0.8.7 | **WP-13 — the word-crossing split, the store grant and the halfword lane: `FR-42` and `FR-38` leave `deferred` for `verified`, `FR-11`/`FR-12` keep `verified` with different evidence, `FR-21` becomes `partial` on a new gap, `G-18`/`G-22`/`G-34` close and `G-35` opens.** A non-atomic access that crosses a word boundary is no longer presented as one unaligned beat: `rtl/misaligned_split.sv` derives `split_needed = access_ma && (({1'b0, lane_ma} + bytes_ma) > 3'd4)` (`:108`), drives the second beat at `(addr & ~32'h3) + 4` with its strobes recomputed for the access's high bytes, and merges the two returned words before the extension, so each beat's strobes stay inside one word while beat 1 carries the access's low bytes at its byte address (`FR-42`, `G-22`). A store is now held in MA while the grant is withheld — `rtl/control.sv:295` extends the stall condition to `data_wr_en_ma` and `:307` adds `split_stall` to the pipeline freeze — so `FR-38` no longer depends on the testbench (`G-18`). The halfword lane the requirement asks for is byte-granular — a halfword at byte offset 1 occupies bytes 1-2 — and the RTL was halfword-granular: `lh`/`lhu`/`sh` at that offset read and wrote bytes 0-1 instead, which `T-51` measured red first and the `halfword-lane-ignore-bit0` mutant re-introduces, so `G-34` opens and closes in this revision and `FR-11`/`FR-12` are re-evidenced rather than re-promoted — their earlier `verified` mark rested on the even halfword offsets only and is stated as overclaimed here. Three tests are added: `T-51` (`riscv_small_halfword_lane_test`, `FR-11`/`FR-12`/`G-34`), `T-52` (`sanity:rv32ui-p-ma_data`, `FR-42`/`G-22`), `T-53` (`riscv_small_store_grant_test`, `FR-38`/`G-18`), each failing on its own mutant (`halfword-lane-ignore-bit0`, `no-misaligned-split`, `store-grant-ignored`). The collector gains `cg_beat`/`cp_beat`, two bins that must both be hit for a split access to count as measured (63 hits each), so the report is **52 of 52** bins over **6** coverpoints with the `A-09` bar still **40 of 40**. Measured at RTL `sha256:f84ea86b…` and TB `sha256:7860d1b0…`: `make regress` **49/49**, `make negative-control` **47/47**, `make sanity` **43/43** (`rv32ui-p-ma_data` passes; 42 of 42 bundled images), `make coverage` **52/52** and **40/40**. **The one finding that is not a fix: a read whose grant is withheld for two or more cycles is lost** — the load is presented for one cycle and the stall acts one cycle later, when the request is already off the bus, which `verif-author` measured as a deadlock against a withholding memory; `FR-21` therefore moves `implemented` → `partial` on the new `G-35` (`E8`, the request/response form of the data port), and its acceptance is stated to cover only the two latencies it already named. `G-05` is unchanged. The 59 `FR` rows move from 14 `verified`, 6 `implemented`, 3 `partial` and 36 `deferred` to **16 `verified`, 5 `implemented`, 4 `partial` and 34 `deferred`** | spec-author | designer — review requested |
| 2026-11-21 | v0.8.8 | **WP-14 — the CSR file and the trap path: nine rows become `verified`, eight of them inside the phase-1 bar; `FR-22` becomes `implemented`, `FR-24` and `FR-50` become `partial`, and `G-37`/`G-38`/`G-39` open.** `rtl/csr_file.sv` is new (239 lines) and brings the machine-mode map of `FR-50`, the six `Zicsr` forms (`FR-47`), `misa` from the build's parameters (`FR-37`), `mhartid` from a new 4-bit `hart_id` input (`FR-32`), `time`/`timeh` from a new 64-bit `mtime` input (`FR-49`), `mscratch` (`FR-51`) and `mtval` (`FR-52`); `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:271-361` becomes one legality table that raises cause 2 on an unknown opcode, a reserved `funct3`/`funct7`, an absent CSR address and a write to a read-only one, and carries cause 11 for `ECALL` and 3 for `EBREAK` (`FR-24`, `FR-25`); and the trap enters at `mtvec.BASE` with `mepc`/`mcause` written at the event (`FR-22`, `FR-23`, `FR-29`). A trap now costs **two** IF/ID flushes, `trap_taken_q` echoing the trap the way `branch_taken_q` echoes a jump: the first version observed 4 of the 6 traps and was red. `T-03` (`riscv_small_csr_test`, wp 14) is the evidence for all ten promoted rows and passes in a **50/50** regression at RTL `sha256:2e91301d…` / TB `sha256:0091e260…`, red first at RTL `766cf5a1…` and red again at `1410ffdf…` when the counter aliases were wrong; its mutant is the two-arm `csr-illegal-noop`, and coverage gains `cg_csr` with `cp_csr_op` **6/6**, `cp_csr_group` **5/5** and `cp_trap_cause` **3/3** (**52 → 66** report bins in total, the `A-09` bar still **40/40**). `T-03` is the evidence for every row this package touches, and two of them are booked rather than promoted: `FR-22` stays `implemented` because `T-03` never asserts the external `exception[3:0]` request (`G-37`), and `FR-24` is `partial` because `ECALL`/`EBREAK` with a non-zero `rd`/`rs1` still retire instead of trapping with cause 2 — a divergence the model mirrors, so no test can see it (`G-38`). `G-37` also carries every other unexercised arm: `mepc` WARL writes, `mtvec.MODE` legalisation, the identifier sweep, the multi-configuration `misa`, `instret` across traps, `rv32mi-p-mcsr` (**which did not run**: no `rv32mi` environment exists), the `NUM_TRIGGERS` > 0 window, and `D12`. `G-13` narrows to the instruction-address-misaligned cause and `G-06` to the `M`/`C`/`FENCE` capability. **A defect the same pass found changes the tally:** `minstret` 0xB02 returns the raw `minstret_q` while `instret` 0xC02 returns `minstret_q + valid_ma + valid_wb` (`rtl/csr_file.sv:147`/`:149` against `:152`/`:155`), so the two addresses of one architectural counter disagree by the instructions in flight, `FR-50`'s "read-only views" clause is not met as built, and `G-39` opens with `FR-50` moved `verified` → `partial`; no test reads `minstret`, which is why `T-03` passed over it, and `FR-49` is untouched because its text is about `instret` counting retirements, which the view does correctly. The phase-1 bar therefore stands at **24 of 47 `verified`** and the tally overall at **25 `verified`, 6 `implemented`, 5 `partial` and 23 `deferred`**, so the gate stays `blocked` | spec-author | designer — review requested |

Evidence that the requirements came from someone, not only from the code.

| 1 | 2026-10-28 | designer; spec-author (answers relayed by `docs-coordinator`) | eight items put, six answered. **1** RTOS mode is a software build choice, so the hardware must support preemption. **2** 2 kB direct-mapped I-cache by default, configurable, no D-cache, `fence.i` flushes it. **3** Instruction fetch fills per line (burst), replacing the per-word contract. **4** `NUM_HARTS` in the main configuration package, default 1; `mhartid` is an input. **6** A data transfer completes on the grant at the clock edge, no write-response channel, must stay compatible with a future D-cache or TCDM. **7** Trap vector is the `mtvec` CSR, no vector input port. Items **5** and **8** were not answered: 5 was `fmax`/part (`Q-08`) and 8 the signoff bar (`Q-11`, `Q-12`); `Q-19` closed as bookkeeping | §0.1 `D-01`..`D-08`; §0.2; `FR-27`..`FR-40`; `C-06`..`C-11`; `A-08`..`A-16` |
| 2 | 2026-10-28 | designer; spec-author (answers relayed by `docs-coordinator`) | eight questions put (`Q-05`, `Q-06`, `Q-13`, `Q-14`, `Q-16`, `Q-17`, `Q-18`, `Q-20`); every proposed default accepted, no counter-proposal. **Q-05** every encoding outside the implemented subset traps, including `ECALL`, `EBREAK`, CSR and multiply encodings; no silent no-operation. **Q-06** both known ISA deviations are fixed before signoff. **Q-13** simple request/length/valid/last-beat burst, no identifiers, no separate read-data channel, same line interface when `ICACHE_BYTES` = 0. **Q-14** 32-byte line, fixed in phase 1. **Q-16** one binary per configuration; `NUM_HARTS`, `ENABLE_A` and `ICACHE_BYTES` are not binary-portable. **Q-17** CLINT plus PLIC, not CLIC; no data cache, so no coherence protocol. **Q-18** every hart leaves reset at the same fetch address; no boot hart. **Q-20** `ENABLE_A` = 0 for `MCU_RTOS`: the MVP RTOS masks interrupts for critical sections, so `A` stays phase 2 and `MCU_SMP` stays the only configuration that carries it | §0.3; `D-05`; `FR-07`, `FR-11`, `FR-12`, `FR-24`, `FR-25`, `FR-27`; §4.1; `C-07`; §5.2; §6; §7.1; `A-10`..`A-12`, `A-14`..`A-16` |
| 3 | 2026-10-28 | designer; spec-author (answers relayed by `docs-coordinator`) | seven questions put (`Q-08`, `Q-09`, `Q-10`, `Q-11`, `Q-12`, `Q-15`, `Q-21`); all seven defaults accepted. **Q-08**-**Q-10** measure first, then fix the number, so the four non-functional targets keep `TBD` and the method becomes the requirement. **Q-11** the signoff bar of `A-09` is confirmed. **Q-12** gap ownership confirmed: designer for spec and ISA gaps, verification lead for DV gaps, all targets 2026-12-31. **Q-15** `fence` is full ordering, not a no-op justified by the port. **Q-21** a short burst is a protocol error, so the 4-beat tolerance goes | §0.4; `NFR-01`..`NFR-06`; `FR-26`; `FR-27`; `A-08`, `A-09`, `A-13`; §9 |

Mode: **elicitation** for everything v0.2 to v0.7 add, **reconstruction** for the as-built rows inherited from
v0.1 (those cite `file:line`). The elicitation evidence is the designer's answers as relayed by the
coordinator; the RTL was not re-read to confirm intent, only to state what exists.

### 0.1 Decisions (2026-10-28)

A decision is a choice with a reason; it is not a requirement. Where a decision can be observed, the
requirement it produces is named. These rows live here because `doc/decisions.md` does not exist yet
(`G-17`); they move there unchanged when it does.

| ID | Decision | Reason | Requirements it produces | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-01 | The product goal is an MCU that runs a simple RTOS; the RTOS mode (preemptive, cooperative, or hybrid) is a software build choice, so the hardware shall support preemption. | the customer wants one core, several software models | `FR-39`, `FR-40` (timer and external interrupt); `FR-36` (phase 2 IPI) | confirmed |
| D-02 | SMP is a later milestone, and the ISA and the interfaces shall not preclude it. | avoid a second tape-out for the same product | `FR-33`, `FR-34`, `FR-35` | confirmed |
| D-03 | `NUM_HARTS` ∈ {1, 2, 4} is a synthesis parameter in the main configuration package; the core stays hart-agnostic; the hart identifier is an input, not a parameter; default 1. | one RTL, three products; identity cannot be a compile-time constant if the SoC wires harts differently | `C-06`, `FR-32`, `FR-33` | confirmed |
| D-04 | A 2 kB direct-mapped instruction cache is the default, configurable; there is no data cache in any phase; `fence.i` flushes the I-cache. | instruction fetch stalls dominate an MCU running from flash; a D-cache would force coherence in SMP | `C-08`, `FR-31` | confirmed |
| D-05 | A compiled binary is valid only for the configuration it was built for; ISA differences between variants are not binary-portable. | keeps `NUM_HARTS` and `ENABLE_A` free instead of freezing one ABI | `C-11` — accepted 2026-10-28, `A-12` confirmed (`Q-16`) | confirmed |
| D-06 | `A` (Zaamo + Zalrsc) enters the target ISA in phase 2 and is not advertised in `misa` before it is implemented. | atomics are needed only when harts can race | `C-07`, `FR-35`, `FR-37` | confirmed |
| D-07 | `M` and `C` are fixed on and are not parameters. | every target toolchain emits them; a build without them saves nothing | `C-10`, §7.1; `FR-43` makes the decision observable for `M` and `FR-44` for `C` | confirmed |
| D-08 | The normative source is the latest **ratified** RISC-V specification; drafts are advisory only, so `mtvec`/`mepc`/`mcause`/`mstatus`/`mie`/`mip` follow the ratified privileged spec and neither RVM23 nor CLIC is adopted. | a draft can change under a validated product | `FR-29`, `FR-30`, `FR-37`; `A-14` | confirmed |
| D-09 | The reset is **hybrid**: `rst_n` low asserts asynchronously, de-assertion is synchronised through `RESET_SYNC_STAGES` flops, and a global macro selects the style so an FPGA build can use a synchronous reset. | the ISA says nothing about reset style, so this is a project choice; an asynchronous reset arriving from an external pin needs a de-assertion synchroniser, and the project's RTL guidance already partitions the two styles by macro (`.opencode/skills/systemverilog-rtl-verification/references/reset-and-fsm.md`, "Global reset selection", "Reset synchronizer") | `C-01`, `FR-41`, `A-06`; gap `G-21` | confirmed 2026-10-28 (`Q-25`) |
| D-10 | The debug contract is the RISC-V **Debug Specification v1.0** (ratified 2025-02-21) with an **external Debug Module behind a JTAG DTM**; the core implements the hart side of that interface, and the Debug Module Interface is the DTM-to-DM link and not a core port. | the specification keeps the abstract commands, the program buffer and the debugger-facing registers in the DM and leaves only the hart side in the core, so one DM can serve several harts and the core carries no transport logic; a core that owned the DMI would have to own the DM | `C-13`, `FR-53`..`FR-56`, the debug group of §6 | confirmed 2026-10-28 (`Q-36`, `Q-37`) |
| D-11 | A `dbg_auth` strap, driven by the platform, gates debug at the hart and is enabled by default on a platform that wants debug; when it is low the hart ignores halt and reset-halt requests, never enters debug mode and reports itself not halted. | a deployed part must not be haltable by an attached probe; the Debug Specification places debugger authentication in the Debug Module (§3.1.12), so the strap adds a hart-side gate beside it rather than replacing it, and keeping the gate a port rather than a build parameter means one binary serves both kinds of part | `C-14`, `FR-59`; one input in the debug group of §6 | confirmed 2026-10-28 (`Q-39`) |

### 0.2 Answers recorded, with their classification

| # | Answer (paraphrased back to the designer) | Classified as | Recorded in |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The hardware must support preemption, but preemptive, cooperative, and hybrid are software build choices rather than synthesis choices. | decision `D-01`; requirements `FR-39`, `FR-40` | §0.1, §4 |
| 2 | The MVP ships a 2 kB direct-mapped instruction cache, sized by a parameter, with no data cache, invalidated by `fence.i`. | decision `D-04`; constraints `C-08`, `C-09`; requirement `FR-31` | §0.1, §5.1, §5.2, §4 |
| 3 | A fetch fills a whole line as one burst, so the per-word `inst_ready` contract is replaced. | requirement `FR-27`; assumption `A-10` (burst shape) | §4, §9 |
| 4 | The hart count is a parameter with default 1, held in the main configuration package, while the hart identifier arrives as an input. | decision `D-03`; constraint `C-06`; requirements `FR-32`, `FR-33` | §0.1, §5.1, §5.2, §4 |
| 6 | A load or a store completes on the data memory's grant at the clock edge, with no write-response channel, and the contract must survive a later D-cache or TCDM. | requirement `FR-38`; assumption `A-01` (load half confirmed) | §4, §9 |
| 7 | A trap vector comes from the `mtvec` CSR; there is no vector input port. | decision `D-08`; requirements `FR-29`, `FR-30` | §0.1, §4 |
| 5 | Item 5 was the `fmax` and target-part question, and item 8 the signoff bar. Neither was answered; they stay `Q-08`, `Q-11` and `Q-12`, and `Q-19` closes as bookkeeping. | no new artifact | `Q-08`, `Q-11`, `Q-12` |

### 0.3 Round-2 answers (2026-10-28), with their classification

All eight were answered "yes" to the proposed default, so each row below is the default as the designer accepted
it, not a paraphrase of new intent. Nothing was overturned.

| Q | Answer (paraphrased back to the designer) | Classified as | Recorded in |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-05` | Every encoding outside the implemented subset **at that phase** raises a trap — CSR, multiply/divide and compressed encodings included while their extension is unimplemented — and nothing executes silently as a no-operation; each extension stops trapping for its own encodings when its requirement lands (`FR-43`, `FR-44`, `FR-47`). | requirement text `FR-24`, `FR-25`; conformance list §4.1; gap scope `G-06`; carve-outs `FR-43`..`FR-47` | §4, §4.1, §9 |
| `Q-06` | Both known ISA deviations are fixed before signoff: `JALR` clears bit 0 of the target, and a sub-word access selects the addressed lane inside the word. | acceptance `FR-07`, `FR-11`, `FR-12`; `G-07`, `G-08` become release blockers. **2026-11-05:** WP-6 closed `G-08` and WP-7 closed `G-07`, so neither deviation is a blocker: `FR-11` is `verified` (`T-13`..`T-15`) and `FR-07`'s mask half is observed by `T-17`/`T-37`, while that row stays `partial` on the instruction-address-misaligned clause of its acceptance (`G-13`) | §4, §7.1, §9 |
| `Q-13` | The fill burst is one request per line with a length, a valid, and a last-beat flag on the final beat; no identifiers, no separate read-data channel; the cache-less build keeps the same line interface. | requirement `FR-27`; interface summary §6; assumption `A-10` confirmed | §4, §6, §9 |
| `Q-14` | The instruction-cache line is 32 bytes, eight words, fixed in phase 1 and not independently tunable. | constraint `C-09` (unchanged); parameter row `ICACHE_LINE`; assumption `A-11` confirmed | §5.1, §5.2, §9 |
| `Q-16` | One binary per configuration: `NUM_HARTS`, `ENABLE_A` and `ICACHE_BYTES` are not binary-portable. | decision `D-05` accepted; constraint `C-11`; assumption `A-12` confirmed | §0.1, §5.1, §9 |
| `Q-17` | The interrupt controller is the ratified CLINT for the timer plus the PLIC for external requests; CLIC is not used; nothing is cached in data memory, so no coherence protocol exists. | assumptions `A-14`, `A-15` confirmed; the `MTIP`/`MEIP` obligation and the vectored scope of `FR-30` stand | §1, §9 |
| `Q-18` | Every hart leaves reset at the same fetch address; the core designates no boot hart. | assumption `A-16` confirmed; `FR-33` unchanged | §1, §9 |
| `Q-20` | `ENABLE_A` = 0 for `MCU_RTOS`: the MVP RTOS masks interrupts for critical sections and needs no atomics; `A` stays phase 2 and `MCU_SMP` stays the only configuration that carries it. If Zephyr becomes the target, the default flips. | constraint `C-07` note; configuration table; §7.1 phase table; `FR-35` unchanged at phase 2 | §5.1, §5.2, §7.1 |

### 0.5 Round-4 answers (2026-10-28): five assumptions answered against the standard

The designer asked what the normative text says and instructed that this specification follow it, so each answer
below is a citation where the standard decides and a project decision where it does not.

| Q | Answer (paraphrased back to the designer) | Citation or decision | Recorded in |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-22` | `x0` is hardwired to zero because volume I §1.1.1 says so: "Register `x0` is hardwired with all bits equal to 0". This is not a project choice, so it cannot be an assumption; the companion rule that a load with `rd = x0` still raises its exceptions and causes its side effects follows from §1.1.6. | cited rule; `A-02` retired, `C-12` added | §5.1, §9 |
| `Q-23` | After reset only the state listed in privileged volume II §2.1.4 is defined; "All other hart state is UNSPECIFIED", so no register value is a contract. | cited rule; `A-04` rewritten and confirmed | §9 |
| `Q-24` | Misaligned behaviour is EEI-dependent (§1.1.6), and this EEI guarantees full support, so the core passes every byte address through and raises no misaligned exception. The data memory returns the aligned word and the core selects the lane. | cited rule plus one EEI guarantee; `A-05` rewritten and confirmed; lane contract in §6 | §6, §9 |
| `Q-25` | The ISA says nothing about reset style. The project uses a hybrid reset: asynchronous assertion, de-assertion through a `RESET_SYNC_STAGES` synchroniser, style selected by a global macro. | project decision `D-09`, taken from `.opencode/skills/systemverilog-rtl-verification/references/reset-and-fsm.md`; `A-06` revised, `FR-41`, `G-21` | §0.1, §4, §5.1, §5.2, §9 |
| `Q-26` | The standard constrains ordering, never the number of in-flight transactions (RVWMO volume I chapter 17; `FENCE` §1.1.7; `FENCE.I` Zifencei chapter 4). One outstanding access per port is a microarchitecture choice whose remaining normative obligation is `FENCE` and `FENCE.I`. | cited rule; `A-07` confirmed as microarchitecture; `Q-27` opened | §9 |

### 0.6 Round-5 answers (2026-10-28): the data port, settled against maintained open cores

The audit found the data port describing a byte-strobe capability it did not have. The designer answered both
halves by comparing three maintained open cores rather than by preference.

| Q | Answer (paraphrased back to the designer) | Evidence and consequence | Recorded in |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-28` | The port gains `data_wr_strb[3:0]`. The core places the value in the addressed lane and asserts the strobes for the bytes it drives; the memory never shifts or justifies. | PicoRV32 exposes `mem_wstrb[3:0]`, Ibex `data_be_o[3:0]` with a word-aligned address, and CV32E40P carries OBI byte enables, so this port was the outlier; the §6 contract stops describing a signal the DUT does not have; `G-08` becomes a strobe-and-lane fix | §6, §4, §9 |
| `Q-29` | An access that straddles two aligned words is split by the core into two word-aligned transactions — the address advances, the strobes are recomputed for the second beat, the pipeline is held through both — and such an access is not atomic. **2026-11-19:** beat 1 carries the access's byte address with its strobes confined to that word; only beat 2 is word-aligned. | Ibex and CV32E40P split; PicoRV32 instead raises a bus error on a misaligned access, and that route was rejected. `A-05`'s EEI guarantee survives unchanged and is now implementable; `FR-42` carries it | §4, §9 |

Every row below cites one of these. The edition is RISC-V ISA **v20260120**; a section number that the edition
renumbers is a documentation fix, not a requirement change. The debug rows cite a second document, the RISC-V
**Debug Specification v1.0** (ratified 2025-02-21), name its chapter, and are kept in their own block: it is a
different specification and its sections never renumber with an ISA edition.

| Citation | What it decides | Cited by |

| vol. I §1.1.6 | naturally aligned accesses never raise a misaligned exception; misaligned behaviour is EEI-dependent; a load with `rd = x0` still raises its exceptions and causes its side effects | `A-05`, `C-12`, `FR-42` |
| vol. I §1.1.7 | `FENCE` orders device I/O as well as memory; reserved configurations are treated as `FENCE` with `fm=0000`; `rs1` and `rd` are ignored | `FR-26` |

| vol. I §27.1, §27.1.1 | the `C` extension and `IALIGN=16`: a 32-bit instruction may start on any 16-bit boundary, so one may span two fetched words | `FR-44`, `FR-20`, `FR-27` |
| vol. I A | atomics: `LR`/`SC`/`AMO` must be naturally aligned or take a misaligned or access-fault exception; `SC` success and failure rules and the forward-progress guarantee | `FR-35`, `A-05`, `FR-42` |
| vol. I Zifencei | `FENCE.I` and its reserved `imm[11:0]`, `rs1` and `rd` fields | `FR-31` |
| vol. I `Zicsr` | the `CSR*` instructions: read-modify-write to `rd`, no write at all when `rs1 = x0` or `uimm = 0`, and an illegal-instruction trap on a write to a read-only CSR or an access to an address the configuration does not implement | `FR-47`, `FR-50` |
| vol. II §1.1.1 | the CSR address map: `csr[11:10] = 2'b11` marks a read-only address and a write there raises illegal-instruction, while a read/write register may contain read-only bits, whose writes are ignored | `FR-50`, `FR-52` |

| vol. II §2.1.1.6.5 | `mstatush`: RV32-only, and its fields are zero without big-endian data or a lower privilege mode | `FR-50` |
