| Debug Spec v1.0 ch. 4: §4.1.1 (debug mode, and its Table 2 with the `dcsr.cause` encodings and their priority), §4.1.5 (single step, and the trigger and trap interactions quoted in `FR-58`), §4.1.9 (the core debug registers, and its Table 3 "Virtual address in DPC"), §4.1.10 (the virtual debug registers, `dcsr.prv`) | what debug mode does to the hart: the events that enter it, the reason recorded in `dcsr.cause` and which of two simultaneous causes wins, the value `dpc` holds per cause, that interrupts and the instructions' synchronous exceptions are not taken in it, and how a single step is counted and ended | `FR-53`, `FR-58` |
| Debug Spec v1.0 §4.1.9, §4.1.10 | the addresses `dcsr` 0x7b0, `dpc` 0x7b1, `dscratch0` 0x7b2, `dscratch1` 0x7b3, and `dcsr.prv` reading the privilege the hart was in, which is machine mode here (`A-19`) | `FR-50`, `FR-53` |
| Debug Spec v1.0 ch. 5: §5.1 ("Triggers do not fire while in Debug Mode"), §5.1.1 (trigger enumeration, and why an unimplemented Sdtrig traps), **§5.1.2 and its Table 1** (the `action` and `dmode` fields), §5.1.7 (the trigger module registers and the `tselect` WARL rule) | the addresses `tselect` 0x7a0, `tdata1` 0x7a1, `tdata2` 0x7a2, `tdata3` 0x7a3, `tinfo` 0x7a4, the `mcontrol6` type the trigger unit implements, that the addresses trap when no trigger is implemented, that a written index at or beyond the count may legalise, and — from Table 1 — the two `action` encodings by number: **action 0** "Raise a breakpoint exception" with `xepc` at the next instruction to execute, **action 1** "Enter Debug Mode" with `dpc` at that same address and "only legal when the trigger's `dmode` is 1", so that "Since `tdata1` is WARL, hardware must prevent it from containing `dmode=0` and `action=1`" | `FR-50`, `FR-57`, `FR-59` |
| Debug Spec v1.0 app. A, Table 1 | the suggested DMI between one DTM and one DM — `REQ_VALID`, `REQ_READY`, `REQ_ADDRESS`, `REQ_DATA`, `REQ_OP` and `RSP_VALID`, `RSP_READY`, `RSP_DATA`, `RSP_OP` — which is what places the DMI outside the core when the DM is external | `C-13` |

### 0.8 Round-7 answers (2026-10-28): the implementation choices the standard leaves open

Each row below is a place where the standard permits more than one implementation and the designer chose one.
None of them is re-derived in §4: the requirement cites the row and states the consequence.

| Q | Answer (paraphrased back to the designer) | Classified as | Recorded in |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-30` | `misa` is **read-only**: it reports the built configuration, every write is left unhonoured, and no write traps. This is admissible because `misa` is WARL and a WARL field may be implemented read-only, so a write is a legal write that legalises to the value already there — it is not a write to a read-only CSR, which would trap. `IALIGN` is therefore fixed by the build. | requirement clause `FR-37`; assumption `A-19` fixes which bits exist at all | §4, §9 |
| `Q-31` | `WFI` is a **no-operation**: it retires immediately, and an interrupt taken afterwards leaves `mepc` at the following instruction. | requirement clause `FR-46`; the stall variant would add a halt state and a wake-up rule | §4 |
| `Q-32` | `mtval` is **read-only zero**: every write is ignored, every read returns 0, no exception writes it, and no write traps — the rules are those of a read/write address whose bits are read-only (vol. II §1.1.1). A handler that needs the faulting encoding reads it from `mepc`, which §2.1.1.16 names as the alternative. | requirement `FR-52`; `FR-24` keeps no `mtval` check | §4 |
| `Q-33` | The reset vector is the build-time parameter `RESET_VECTOR`, default `0x8000_0000`, and the first fetch after reset is at that address. It is a build input, not a CSR, so software cannot move it. | requirement clause `FR-41`; parameter row in §5.2 | §4, §5.2 |
| `Q-34` | **No profile** is claimed. The ISA surface is exactly §7.1, `A-05` stays an EEI guarantee rather than a profile obligation, and no profile conformance suite joins the signoff bar. The same decision fixes the product as machine-mode only: `S`/`U`-mode, an MMU, `F`/`D` and `V` are out, and task isolation is not a requirement. | assumption `A-19` confirmed; constraint `C-15`; `NFR-05` scope unchanged | §5.1, §7.1, §9 |
| `Q-35` | A synchronous trap request carries its reason on the cause bus `exception[3:0]`, widening the 1-bit input; `mtip`, `meip` and `msip` are separate level-sensitive interrupt inputs. The core captures the cause at entry and does not re-read the bus afterwards, so a request withdrawn mid-trap still reports the cause it presented. | requirement clauses `FR-22`, `FR-39`, `FR-40`; `FR-48` keeps the architectural effect and no port | §4, §6 |
| `Q-36` | Debug reaches the hart through an **external Debug Module behind a JTAG DTM**: the core implements the hart side of that interface, never JTAG pins, and the DMI stays between the DTM and the DM (appendix A, Table 1). The standard puts the abstract commands in the DM and leaves only the hart side in the core. | constraint `C-13`; decision `D-10`; requirements `FR-54`, `FR-55` | §0.1, §5.1, §6, §7.1 |
| `Q-37` | The hart side is the **full** interface the Debug Module needs — run control, status, the abstract-command channel and the program-buffer channel — not a reduced halt-and-resume port. Corrected the same day: the DMI itself is the DTM-to-DM link and therefore is not a core port, so "full hart-side interface" replaces the earlier wording "full DMI". | requirement text `FR-54`, `FR-55`, `FR-56`; the debug group of §6; `C-13` | §4, §6 |
| `Q-38` | `NUM_TRIGGERS` is 0, 2 or 4 with default 2, and the only trigger type implemented is `mcontrol6`, matching an instruction address, a load/store address or store data. | parameter row in §5.2; requirement `FR-57` | §4, §5.2 |
| `Q-39` | A `dbg_auth` strap gates debug at the hart, driven by the platform and enabled by default: when it is low the hart ignores halt and reset-halt requests and never enters debug mode. This is an **additional** gate — Debug Spec v1.0 §3.1.12 places authentication in the DM — so it neither replaces that mechanism nor is a substitute for it. The gate is an input strap, not a build parameter, so the same binary is locked down or debuggable according to the platform. | constraint `C-14`; decision `D-11`; requirement `FR-59`; port row in §6 | §0.1, §4, §5.1, §6 |
| `Q-40` | `resethaltreq` is supported, so the DM can halt the hart at the reset vector before the first instruction retires. | requirement clause `FR-54`; the reset sequence of `FR-41` must not preclude it | §4 |
| `Q-41` | No non-maskable interrupt in this revision: the machine interrupt set stays the three maskable causes, and a watchdog NMI is a phase-2 obligation. | §7.1 row; `FR-48` keeps causes 3, 7 and 11 only | §7.1 |
| `Q-42` | `time` and `timeh` are read-only views of a dedicated 64-bit `mtime` input driven by the platform; `cycle` and `instret` stay the core's own counters, and the core issues no memory access to read the timer. | requirement clause `FR-49`; port row in §6 | §4, §6 |

`Q-36`..`Q-42` are the debug increment this version also applies. Each row above is a choice the standard left
open or a scope decision that belongs to this product; none of them is re-derived in §4, and no clause there
re-decides one.

**Why `mtval` and `mstatush` are writable.** Both sit at read/write addresses (`csr[11:10] = 2'b00`), and vol. II
§1.1.1 draws the trapping line at the address rather than at the register's content: "Attempts to write a
read-only register raise illegal-instruction exceptions. A read/write register might also contain some bits that
are read-only, in which case writes to the read-only bits are ignored." The "read-only zero" of §2.1.1.16 is
therefore a statement about bits in a read/write register, so `csrw` to `mtval` is a legal write that changes
nothing. The alternative reading — the whole register is read-only, so a write faults — was considered and
rejected: it would make a context save built from `csrw` of every machine CSR fault on a register that carries
no information at all. `FR-50` and `FR-52` state the accepted reading.

### 0.9 Round-8 answer (2026-11-04): the reference model is monitor-driven

This is one designer decision about the verification architecture, recorded because it fixed what phase `E3`
had to build; WP-8 met it on 2026-11-07. It changes no functional requirement, no status and no acceptance cell.

| Q | Answer (paraphrased back to the designer) | Classified as | Recorded in |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-43` | The verification **reference model is driven only by the monitor**: the observed instruction and data stream reaches it through the monitor's analysis port, and the same model must run unchanged in a passive agent (`is_active = UVM_PASSIVE`, no driver, no sequencer). The **program image** — the instructions a directed sequence assembles, or an `rv32ui` hex image — is **configuration, not a stimulus transaction**: it initialises the model's own architectural state once, and no sequence, test or driver writes a transaction into the model. The model keeps its own architectural state and never reads the memory the driver mutates. This is also the seam for the Spike co-simulation of `E3`, which consumes the same retired-instruction stream. The obligation is **met 2026-11-07 by WP-8**: the core publishes a retire event — `valid_wb && ma_wb_clk_en` through the bound `riscv_small_retire_if` probe — the monitor republishes it, and the model consumes only the monitor while keeping its own architectural state. `program_port`, `rm_export` and `publish_program()` are deleted, so nothing but an observation reaches the model, and `riscv_small_program_image` arrives as configuration through `uvm_config_db`, once. `T-44` (72 commits for 72 fetches) and `T-45` (10 commits for 12 fetches, the two discarded words never retiring) observe the distinction, each failing on the `retire-every-slot` mutant. | constraint `C-16` (met 2026-11-07), gap `G-28` (closed 2026-11-07); `NFR-06`'s sampling rule is unchanged — coverage still samples the monitor, and its bins are still fetches | §5.1, §9 |

## 1. Purpose and scope

This document specifies the externally observable behaviour of the `riscv_small` core, and the behaviour its
product intends. The product is a 32-bit RISC-V MCU core that runs a simple RTOS: an RTOS mode is a software
build choice, and the hardware obligation is that a preemption source exists and that a trap can be taken and
returned from (`D-01`). Multi-hart execution is a later milestone that the instruction set and the memory
interfaces must not preclude (`D-02`).

The document therefore has two layers. The **as-built** layer (v0.1, reconstructed) states which instructions
the core executes today, how it interacts with external instruction and data memories, how it resolves data
hazards, and what it does on a trap. The **target** layer (v0.2 to v0.7, elicited and reviewed against the standard) states the extension set with a
phase per extension (§7.1), the synthesis configuration (§5.2), and the requirements that follow from them,
each with its phase in the status or the row text.

The RTOS goal fixes the shape of the phase-1 deliverable. Machine mode shall be **complete**, not partial: a
CSR file with a defined address map (`FR-50`), trap entry with a software-chosen vector, a cause, a writable
return address and a scratch register (`FR-29`, `FR-30`, `FR-23`, `FR-45`, `FR-48`, `FR-51`), maskable timer
and external interrupts with a priority order (`FR-39`, `FR-40`, `FR-48`), and a `misa` that reports what the
build actually executes (`FR-37`). A context switch needs all five: an entry point software can move, a way to
find the interrupted frame, a way to return, a reason for the trap, and a way to be interrupted at all
(`D-01`). Machine mode is the only privilege level the product provides, and that is the scope assumption
`A-19` states.

In scope: the decoded instruction subset; address generation for loads, stores, branches and jumps; the
instruction-fill and data-grant contracts; the register-file contract; forwarding and stall obligations on
software; trap entry and return; the machine-mode CSR file and its address map, in full and not only as far as
other rows reach into it; and the non-functional targets that are currently unmeasured.

Out of scope (non-goals), so that scope is not re-argued:

- Supervisor and user mode, an MMU, floating point, vector, and hypervisor extensions. Not targeted, and
  `A-19` records the condition under which that changes: a requirement for task isolation would need `S`/`U`
  mode plus physical-memory protection, not an MMU. §7.1 records the set as absent with no claim.
- A data cache and any cache-coherence protocol, in every phase (`D-04`, `A-15`).
- Trace (N-Trace and E-Trace) and JTAG pins. Not targeted in this revision: the core exposes no JTAG pin, and the
  JTAG DTM and the Debug Module behind it sit outside the core (`C-13`, `D-10`). Debug itself is specified and
  not built: `FR-53`..`FR-59` are `deferred`, and §6 describes a port group that no build instantiates yet.
- The choice of interrupt controller and the boot protocol of secondary harts, beyond the two answers of
  2026-10-28. The controller is the ratified CLINT (timer) plus the PLIC (external), not CLIC (`A-14`,
  `Q-17`); every hart leaves reset at the same fetch address and the core designates no boot hart (`A-16`,
  `Q-18`). Wiring and hart selection stay SoC concerns; the core-level obligation is only that a timer, an
  external, and (phase 2) a software interrupt be taken as traps (`FR-39`, `FR-40`, `FR-36`).
- Timing, area, power, and verification signoff. No timing, area or power measurement exists (`G-05`); the
  verification measurements that do exist are not signoff and live in `doc/measurements/` (`NFR-05` 42 of the
  42 bundled images and 43 of the 43 images run, `NFR-06` 40 of 40 bar bins and 66 of 66 report bins over
  9 coverpoints).

The as-built rows of v0.1 remain the description of today's RTL. Where an as-built row conflicts with a
target row, the as-built row keeps its status and the difference is a `G-xx`: v0.2 adds intent, it does not
upgrade capability.

## 2. Stakeholders and concerns

| Stakeholder | Concern | Addressed by |
|---|---|---|
| RTL designer | confirm or correct reconstructed behaviour; settle the questions of `Q-01`..`Q-42`; own the spec and ISA gaps, with a 2026-12-31 target for each (`A-09`, `Q-12`) | §4, §5, §9 |
| Verification engineer | what to test, what is already covered, and which configuration is under test; own the DV gaps (`G-04`, `G-11` — `G-03` closed on 2026-11-06 and `G-26`/`G-27` on 2026-11-04), which are release blockers | acceptance column, §5, §5.2, `G-04`, `G-11` |
| SoC integrator | memory handshake, clock and reset, trap vector source, hart identity, interrupt wiring, the platform timer, whether the debug port exists in the build, and a data memory that accepts any byte address | §5.2, §6, `A-01`, `A-05`, `A-16`, `C-13`, `G-14`, `FR-38` (the store half of the memory handshake, `verified` under `T-53`) and `G-35` (the read half, still open) |
| RTOS and application developer | preemption and its latency, CSR visibility, cache behaviour around self-modifying code, register state after reset | `FR-29`..`FR-31`, `FR-39`, `FR-40`, `A-04` |
| Compiler and toolchain owner | the exact ISA subset, alignment, `x0`, which extensions may be assumed | §4.1, §7, §7.1, `C-12`, `A-05`, `A-12` |
| Reviewer and auditor | traceability and honest status | §8, [verification.md](verification.md) |

## 3. Features

| ID | Feature | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| F-01 | Integer arithmetic and logic execution | the decoded subset is enumerated in §4.1, not the full RV32I |
| F-02 | Control transfer: six conditional branches, `JAL`, `JALR` | taken branches and jumps redirect the fetch stream; the redirect penalty is unmeasured (`G-05`) |
| F-03 | Word and sub-word load and store through an external data port | the width, the addressed lane and the byte strobes are driven out of the core (`FR-11`, `FR-12`); a word-crossing access is issued as two beats, each confined to one word, and a store waits for its grant (`FR-42`, `FR-38`, both `verified` in v0.8.7); what the port still cannot do is hold a **read** whose grant is withheld for two or more cycles (`FR-21`, `G-35`) |
| F-04 | Instruction fetch through an external instruction port | as built: one word per cycle (`FR-20`); target: a line fill (`FR-27`, `FR-28`, `G-15`) |
| F-05 | Hazard-free single-issue execution | phase 1 target; forwarding plus automatic stalls, so software needs no inserted NOPs (`FR-15`..`FR-19`) — the forwarding is what makes the phase-1 `FR-01`..`FR-03` execute as written |
| F-06 | Architectural register file with hardwired `x0` | phase 1 target; `x0` reading zero and discarding every write is the ISA rule, not a choice (`C-12`), and the post-reset contents of the other 31 registers stay unspecified (`A-04`) |
| F-07 | One clock domain, a global clock enable, hybrid reset: asynchronous assertion and synchronised de-assertion | `C-01`, `C-02`, `FR-41`, `D-09` |
| F-08 | Trap entry and return | entry built in v0.8.8: `mtvec.BASE` from the CSR, the cause in `mcause` and the interrupted address in `mepc` (`FR-22`, `FR-23`, `FR-29`); the return half is absent — `MRET` is not decoded (`FR-45`) — and vectored mode is `FR-30`, still `deferred` |
| F-09 | Machine-mode privileged architecture: CSR file, trap CSRs, counters | phase 1 target, built in v0.8.8: the CSR file is `FR-50`, with `mscratch` `FR-51`, `mtval` `FR-52`, `misa` `FR-37` and the counters `FR-49`; what is still absent is the interrupt half (`G-14`) and the instruction-address-misaligned cause (`G-13`) |
| F-10 | Instruction cache with line fills, invalidated by `fence.i` | phase 1 target; absent today (`G-15`) |
| F-11 | Configurable hart count with atomics and inter-processor interrupts | phase 2 target; absent today (`G-16`) |
| F-12 | Debug: halt and resume, abstract commands, a program buffer, triggers, and single step, driven by an external Debug Module | debug-phase target; absent today — the port list carries no debug signal (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:45-62`) and no debug CSR exists. The hart side is `FR-53`..`FR-59` and the transport stays outside the core (`C-13`, `D-10`) |

## 4. Functional requirements

Read the `Evidence` column as "where this behaviour is visible in the RTL today". Status is `implemented`
when the RTL exists and no gap contradicts the clause, `partial` when the clause executes but a named gap
narrows it for part of its domain — **or** when the requirement's own `shall` names a second behaviour that has
no implementation at all, which is what keeps `FR-07` `partial` while its mask clause is observed — `verified`
only where a named test in this repository observes the main
acceptance cell of the row, and `deferred` for capability that is absent. A row whose `shall` is implemented
and whose *acceptance* carries an unexercised arm stays `verified`, with that arm booked to a gap: the
`IALIGN=32` target-alignment arm of `FR-06` and `FR-08` is the case, and it sits in `G-06`. The `verified` rows name their tests
in the Evidence cell: `T-01` (`LW`/`SW`), `T-06` (the sub-word loads), `T-08` (a bundled `rv32ui-p-*` image
through the sanity harness), `T-10`..`T-15` (the same harness on the `and`, `addi`, `beq`, `sb`, `sh` and
`st_ld` images), `T-16`..`T-43` — the WP-7 images, which give every arithmetic, shift,
upper-immediate, jump and branch instruction of §4.1 an image of its own — and the three WP-13 tests:
`T-51` (`riscv_small_halfword_lane_test`, the halfword byte lane at offset 1), `T-52` (the word-crossing
split, run as `sanity:rv32ui-p-ma_data`) and `T-53` (`riscv_small_store_grant_test`, a store against a memory
that withholds its grant for two cycles). `T-03` (CSR and trap),
`T-04` (timer and interrupt) and `T-05` (debug) remain the tests the machine-mode and debug rows name, and
[verification.md](verification.md) registers them as `deferred`, not yet implemented. `T-02` is registered in
the same manifest but adds no stimulus of its own, because it is a factory override onto `T-01`'s sequence, so
no row cites it
(`G-11`). A row whose Status says `deferred` and whose text names phase 2 is a commitment to
the SMP milestone, not to the MVP. The `partial` rows are `FR-07` (`G-13`), `FR-21` (`G-35`), `FR-24` (`G-38`) and `FR-41` (`G-21`); each names its gap in the Evidence cell, and [verification.md](verification.md) is the
reverse index for the same statuses — `G-20`, `G-26` and `G-27` stopped narrowing `FR-06`, `FR-08`, `FR-16`,
`FR-18` and `FR-19` on 2026-11-04, `FR-11` left this set in v0.7.9 when `T-13`..`T-15` observed its
acceptance, and `G-19` and `G-30` released `FR-17` in v0.8.0. `FR-07` keeps `partial` in the same revision
for the other reason: `G-07` closed under `T-17`/`T-37`, which observe the mask, while the instruction-address-misaligned
clause of its acceptance has no implementation (`G-13`, designer decision 2026-11-05). The second reason is FR-21, added to this list in v0.8.7: its clause holds for two of its three latencies, and a read whose grant is
withheld for two or more cycles is lost (`G-35`, found by WP-13 on 2026-11-19), so the row is wrong for that part
of its domain and the gap is cited in it rather than the status being left at `implemented`. `FR-24` joined in
v0.8.8: the decoder traps for the families of §4.1 it names, while a reserved configuration of an implemented
instruction — `ECALL`/`EBREAK` with a non-zero `rd`/`rs1` — still retires (`G-38`). `FR-41` is the only `partial` row whose RTL is in two halves:
its assertion half exists, its reset-vector half is built and observed by `T-50` in a run at the default value
(`G-33`, closed 2026-11-18 for that scope), and its synchronised release does not (`G-21`). The machine-mode rows of v0.7
(`FR-50`..`FR-52`) leave `deferred` in v0.8.8 with the CSR file that retires them; what stays `deferred` in that
area is the return path (`MRET`, `FR-45`) and the interrupt set (`G-14`). The seven debug rows of the same
version (`FR-53`..`FR-59`) are `deferred` for that reason and one more: `rtl/` has no debug port, no trigger
unit, no debug CSR and no debug-mode state, so that part of the table is a target and nothing in it is built.
`T-05` is the test those rows name; it is registered as `deferred` in [verification.md](verification.md) and
not yet implemented, and no test file exists under `tb/tests/`, so not one of them can be `verified` in this
revision.

| ID | Requirement (shall) | Evidence (source) | Acceptance | Traces to | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FR-01 | The core shall execute the register-register integer instructions `ADD`, `SUB`, `SLL`, `SLT`, `SLTU`, `XOR`, `SRL`, `SRA`, `OR`, `AND`, with `SUB` and `SRA` selected by bit 5 of the register-form function field. | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:341-347` selects the operation from `funct3` and bit 5 of the register-form function field and `rtl/alu.sv:52-92` executes all ten operations; every one of the ten has a directed image and all pass at RTL `sha256:b914f2e1…`: `T-43` (`add`), `T-10` (`and`), `T-29` (`sub`), `T-30` (`sll`), `T-31` (`slt`), `T-32` (`sltu`), `T-33` (`xor`), `T-34` (`srl`), `T-35` (`sra`) and `T-36` (`or`). Nine of them fail on the `alu-ignore-rs2` mutant and `T-10` on `branch-redirect`, so `AND` is the one encoding of the ten that no isolating fault-injection run observes: `T-10`'s manifest mutant injects a branch-redirect fault rather than an `ALU` one, and what witnesses `AND` is `rv32ui-p-and`'s own `tohost` self-check; `T-43` makes the point the other eight cannot, because its mutant fails `rv32ui-p-add` at `testnum=3` — `TEST_RR_OP(3, add, 0x2, 0x1, 0x1)` at `src/riscv-tests/isa/rv64ui/add.S:21` — so the failure is the `ADD` result itself and not an incidental `la`/`li` breakage | A directed test per encoding writes a known `rs1`/`rs2` pair and reads `rd` back through a store; any mismatch against the architectural model fails. | `F-01`, `BLK-ID`, `BLK-ALU` | verified |
| FR-02 | The core shall execute the immediate integer instructions `ADDI`, `SLTI`, `SLTIU`, `XORI`, `ORI`, `ANDI` with the 12-bit immediate sign-extended. | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:327-340`, `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:225-229`; each of the six encodings now has a named image — `T-11` (`addi`), `T-23` (`slti`), `T-24` (`sltiu`), `T-25` (`xori`), `T-28` (`andi`) and `T-39` (`ori`) — and all six pass at RTL `sha256:b914f2e1…`, `T-11` failing on the `forward-rs1` mutant and `T-23`..`T-28`/`T-39` on the `imm-zero` mutant. The images' cases include positive and negative 12-bit immediates and `riscv_small_sanity_checker` judges the value each writes to `tohost` | Directed test per encoding, including negative immediates, compared against the model (`G-04`). | `F-01`, `BLK-ID`, `BLK-ALU` | verified |
| FR-03 | The core shall execute `SLLI`, `SRLI` and `SRAI`, taking the shift amount from the low five bits of the immediate and choosing logical or arithmetic from the high function bit. | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:330-336`, `rtl/alu.sv:56-81`; `T-26` (`srli`), `T-27` (`srai`) and `T-40` (`slli`) run the three images and pass at RTL `sha256:b914f2e1…`, each failing on its manifest mutant (`imm-zero` for the two immediate shifts, `shift-left-zero` for `slli`), so all three encodings are observed | Shift by 0, 1, 31 and the logical/arithmetic pair compared against the model (`G-04`). | `F-01`, `BLK-ALU` | verified |
| FR-04 | `LUI` shall write the upper 20 immediate bits shifted left by 12 into `rd`. | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:265-270`, `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:237-239`; `T-41` runs `rv32ui-p-lui`, whose cases check immediates `0x00000`, `0x7ffff`, `0x80000` and `0xfffff` (and that `lui x0` leaves `x0` at zero), and passes at RTL `sha256:b914f2e1…`, failing on the `lui-ignores-imm` mutant | `rd` value read back for immediates `0x00000` and `0xFFFFF` (`G-04`). | `F-01`, `BLK-ID` | verified |
| FR-05 | `AUIPC` shall write the address of the instruction plus the upper 20 immediate bits shifted left by 12 into `rd`. | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:271-277`; `T-42` runs `rv32ui-p-auipc`, whose two cases form a `pc`-relative address with offsets of `+10000` and `-10000` through `auipc` and check the result, and passes at RTL `sha256:b914f2e1…`, failing on the `auipc-no-pc` mutant | `rd` value read back at two different program addresses (`G-04`); depends on `G-09`. | `F-01`, `BLK-ID` | verified |
| FR-06 | `JAL` shall write the address of the next instruction into `rd` and continue at the current address plus the sign-extended jump offset. | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:278-285`, `rtl/jump_decision.sv:98-112`, `rtl/instruction_fetch.sv:108-121`; `rtl/jump_decision.sv:111` adds the whole sign-extended immediate, so a backward offset lands correctly and `G-20` closed on 2026-11-04. `T-18` runs `rv32ui-p-jal`, whose cases check both the link value and the landed address, and passes at RTL `sha256:b914f2e1…` while failing on the `jal-offset-zero` mutant | Forward and backward jump with `rd != x0`, checking both the link value and the landed address (`G-04`). While `C` is not implemented (`IALIGN=32`) a taken `JAL` whose target has bit 1 set raises instruction-address-misaligned (cause 0) on the `JAL` itself; once `C` lands the same target is legal and is taken (vol. I §1.1.5.1, `FR-44`). | `F-02`, `BLK-JMP`, `BLK-IF` | verified |
| FR-07 | `JALR` shall write the address of the next instruction into `rd` and continue at the target address computed as `(rs1 + sign-extended 12-bit offset) & ~32'h1`, so bit 0 of the computed target is cleared; while `C` is not implemented the cleared target must also have bit 1 clear, and a target whose bit 1 is set shall raise instruction-address-misaligned (cause 0) on the `JALR` itself. | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:287-295` decodes `JALR` on the I-type path, sets `base_addr_sel = RS1` and exports `jalr` (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:289`); `rtl/jump_decision.sv:115-116` clears bit 0 of the target when `base_addr_sel == RS1`, which is the `JALR` case only (`G-07`, closed 2026-11-05 by WP-7); `rtl/control.sv:137-172` forwards `rs1` through the jump-compare mux for `cond_jump` and `jalr`, so a `JALR` reads the value its producer still carries (`G-31`, found and closed in the same work package); `G-20` closed on 2026-11-04, so the offset is added sign-extended. `T-17` runs `rv32ui-p-jalr` and `T-37` runs the project-local `rv32ui-p-jalr_unaligned`, the only image that jumps to a target with bit 0 set (`scripts/verify/sanity/local/rv32ui-p-jalr_unaligned.S`); both pass at RTL `sha256:b914f2e1…`, `T-17` failing on the `jalr-no-forward` mutant and `T-37` on `jalr-keep-bit0`, and removing the mask makes `T-37` fetch an odd address and fail `testnum=3`. The second clause of the acceptance has no implementation at all: there is no instruction-address-misaligned path, so a masked target that keeps bit 1 set is fetched rather than trapped with cause 0. That clause is what keeps the row `partial` — the mask half is observed by `T-17`/`T-37`, and the missing half belongs to `G-13`, whose fix is epic `E6` (the CSR, `Zicsr` and trap-entry work package) | Two `JALR`s whose `rs1` plus offset differ only in bit 0 land at the same address, the one with bit 0 cleared, and each writes the link value the model predicts to `rd` — that is the `IALIGN=16` behaviour, correct **only** once `C` is implemented (`FR-44`, vol. I §1.1.5.1). With `IALIGN=32` the same pair must instead raise instruction-address-misaligned on the odd target and take the even-but-bit-1-set target as a trap too, because `& ~1` alone is not enough (`G-07`). | `F-02`, `BLK-JMP` | partial |
| FR-08 | The core shall decide `BEQ`, `BNE`, `BLT`, `BGE`, `BLTU`, `BGEU` from the source values, continuing at the branch target when the condition holds and at the next instruction otherwise. | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:297-300`, `rtl/jump_decision.sv:117-164`, `rtl/jump_decision.sv:111` (the branch target adds the whole sign-extended immediate); each of the six conditions has its own image and all pass at RTL `sha256:b914f2e1…`: `T-12` (`beq`), `T-38` (`bne`), `T-19` (`blt`), `T-20` (`bge`), `T-21` (`bltu`), `T-22` (`bgeu`), plus `T-10` (`and`) as a harness control; each fails on a branch mutant (`negative-branch` for `beq`, `branch-always-taken` for the other five, `branch-redirect` for `and`) and `riscv_small_sanity_checker` judges the value each writes to `tohost`. `G-20`, `G-26` and `G-27` closed on 2026-11-04; the `IALIGN=32` target-alignment arm of the acceptance is unexercised and stays with `G-06`, as `FR-44` states | Taken and not-taken case for each of the six conditions, with one signed and one unsigned boundary pair each (`G-04`); a taken branch whose target bit 1 is set raises instruction-address-misaligned on the branch itself while `IALIGN=32`, and is taken unchanged once `C` lands (vol. I §1.1.5.1, `FR-44`). | `F-02`, `BLK-JMP` | verified |
| FR-09 | `LW` shall read the 32-bit word at the effective address and write it into `rd`. | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:301-314`, `rtl/memory_access.sv:144-146`; `T-49` corroborates it against Spike, because each of its 12 `LW` commits retires at the commit index Spike predicts, with the same `rd` and the same value, among the 477 of 477 commits that match — which adds to `T-01` without replacing its read-back. `T-47` (`rv32ui-p-add`) and `T-48` (`rv32ui-p-beq`) cannot corroborate it: neither program loads from memory at all — the only memory access in either is the `tohost` store — so their 459 and 285 commits carry no `LW` and a load defect would leave both traces identical | `T-01`: 31 random loads whose values are then stored; the stored data is compared against the reference model; verified by `T-01`. | `F-03`, `BLK-MA` | verified |
| FR-10 | `SW` shall write the value of `rs2` to the 32-bit word at the effective address. | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:315-326`, `rtl/execution.sv:101-128` (the EX/MA register carrying `rd0_wr_en_ma` and `rs2_ma`), `rtl/riscv_small.sv:233` (write enable) and `:283` (store data on the port); `T-47`..`T-49` corroborate only its **retirement**: the `SW` commits sit at Spike's commit indices, and because a store writes no register neither its address nor its data appears in the compared line (`G-04`), so `T-01` remains the test that checks address and data | `T-01`: 31 random stores; address and data are compared against the reference model; verified by `T-01`. | `F-03`, `BLK-MA` | verified |
| FR-11 | `SB` and `SH` shall place the byte or halfword of `rs2` in the addressed lane of the word, selected by the low address bits, assert `data_wr_strb` for exactly the byte lanes it drives, and report the access width (byte or halfword) on the width output. | `rtl/execution.sv:205-243` shifts `rs2` into the lane the EX address selects and raises `data_wr_strb_ex` for exactly those bytes, `rtl/execution.sv:127-129` registers the lane value and the strobe into MA, and `rtl/riscv_small.sv:283-284` drives them onto `data_wr` and `data_wr_strb[3:0]` through `u_misaligned_split`, which passes the EX/MA lane and strobe through unchanged when the access does not cross (`rtl/misaligned_split.sv:125-126`); `rtl/riscv_small.sv:139` reports the width from `access_size(rd_wr_ctrl)` — `00` byte, `01` halfword, `10` word (`rtl/riscv_definitions_pkg.sv:276-283`) — in place of the one-bit `logic'(rd_wr_ctrl)` cast that made a word access read `2'b00`. `T-13` (`rv32ui-p-sb`), `T-14` (`rv32ui-p-sh`) and `T-15` (`rv32ui-p-st_ld`) pass at RTL `sha256:b914f2e1…` and again at the current digest, `sha256:f84ea86b…`, each failing on its own mutant (`store-lane-offset`, `store-strb-all`, `store-width-cast`), and `riscv_small_strb_checker` (`tb/env/top/riscv_small_strb_checker.sv:133-209`, `[STRB_WIDTH]`) fails any observed write whose strobe disagrees with the reported width in the directed and the image environment alike. Lane, strobe and width are therefore observed, and **the halfword half of that claim was overclaimed until v0.8.7**: the lane the RTL selected for a halfword was halfword-granular, so `SH` at byte offset 1 wrote bytes 0-1 instead of 1-2 (`G-34`, the halfword version of the `G-08` defect), and `T-13`/`T-14`/`T-15` never presented an odd halfword offset. The lane is now byte-granular — `rtl/execution.sv:224` places the halfword at `4'b0011 << alu_result.u_data[1:0]` — and `T-51` (`riscv_small_halfword_lane_test`, wp 13) observes offset 1 in both directions, failing on the `halfword-lane-ignore-bit0` mutant at RTL `sha256:f84ea86b…` and TB `sha256:7860d1b0…`. The word-crossing split (`FR-42`, `T-52`) and the store's grant wait (`FR-38`, `T-53`) are built in the same revision and no longer narrow this row. `T-49`'s `sb` and `sh` commits also agree with Spike's sequence, which corroborates that they retire and not the lane or the strobe they drove (`G-04`). | Write a byte to each of the four byte offsets of one word, and a halfword to both halfword offsets, then read the word back: each store changes only the addressed lane, every other byte keeps its previous value, `data_wr_strb` is asserted for exactly the driven bytes and no others, and the memory performs no justification of its own (§6, `Q-28`). `Q-06` (2026-10-28) fixed the lane deviation, which made always writing the lowest lane a release blocker (`G-08`); that bar is now met, `G-08` closed 2026-11-05 by `T-13`/`T-14`/`T-15`. | `F-03`, `BLK-MA` | verified |
| FR-12 | `LB`, `LH`, `LBU` and `LHU` shall select the byte lane or the halfword lane of the returned word addressed by the effective address, using the low address bits, and then sign-extend it for `LB`/`LH` or zero-extend it for `LBU`/`LHU`. | `rtl/memory_access.sv:84` takes the byte lane from `alu_wb.u_data[1:0]` and `:96` takes the halfword lane from it byte-granularly (`(ld_lane == 2'd3) ? 2'd2 : ld_lane`), with the sign or zero extension over `ld_word` at `:139-151`; `T-06` proves it at the even byte and halfword offsets. **The odd halfword offset was broken until v0.8.7 and the row was overclaimed before it:** `T-06` presents `LH`/`LHU` at offsets 0 and 2 only, and the RTL selected a halfword-granular lane, so a halfword at offset 1 returned bytes 0-1 (`G-34`). `T-51` (`riscv_small_halfword_lane_test`, wp 13) now observes `LH` and `LHU` at offset 1 and fails on the `halfword-lane-ignore-bit0` mutant, which restores the halfword-granular lane at both `rtl/memory_access.sv:96` and `rtl/execution.sv:224`; the run is at RTL `sha256:f84ea86b…` and TB `sha256:7860d1b0…`. | FR-50 | The core shall implement a machine-mode CSR file with exactly the following addresses, in five groups. **Machine trap and status**: `mstatus` 0x300, `misa` 0x301, `mie` 0x304, `mtvec` 0x305, `mstatush` 0x310 (read-only zero, because an RV32 little-endian machine-only build has no field it could hold, vol. II §2.1.1.6.5), `mscratch` 0x340, `mepc` 0x341, `mcause` 0x342, `mtval` 0x343, `mip` 0x344. **Machine counters**: `mcycle` 0xB00, `minstret` 0xB02, `mcycleh` 0xB80 and `minstreth` 0xB82, read/write (vol. II §2.1.1.10), with the read-only views `cycle` 0xC00, `time` 0xC01, `instret` 0xC02, `cycleh` 0xC80, `timeh` 0xC81 and `instreth` 0xC82 (`FR-49`). **Identification**: `mvendorid` 0xF11, `marchid` 0xF12, `mimpid` 0xF13, `mhartid` 0xF14 and `mconfigptr` 0xF15, all read-only, with `mhartid` reporting the hart identifier (`FR-32`) and the other four reading zero because no identifier is registered and there is no configuration structure. **Performance counters**: `mhpmcounter3`–`mhpmcounter31` 0xB03–0xB1F and `mhpmevent3`–`mhpmevent31` 0x323–0x33F, read-only zero, because only event 0 exists. **Triggers and debug**: `tselect` 0x7A0, `tdata1` 0x7A1, `tdata2` 0x7A2, `tdata3` 0x7A3 and `tinfo` 0x7A4, accessible in machine mode, whose count, field layout and `tinfo` value are governed by `FR-57`; and `dcsr` 0x7B0, `dpc` 0x7B1, `dscratch0` 0x7B2 and `dscratch1` 0x7B3, which exist only while the hart is in debug mode, so an access to any of the four from machine mode shall raise illegal-instruction (cause 2) — Debug Spec v1.0 §4.1.9 places these registers in debug mode, and this document reads that placement as making the machine-mode access illegal. The five trigger addresses exist only while `NUM_TRIGGERS` is greater than 0: with `NUM_TRIGGERS` = 0 no trigger is implemented, so 0x7A0–0x7A4 are unimplemented addresses and an access to any of them shall raise illegal-instruction (cause 2) like any other absent address (`FR-57`, Debug Spec v1.0 §5.1.1). An access to any other CSR address shall raise an illegal-instruction trap (cause 2) with `mepc` holding the address of the `CSR*` instruction itself — among them `medeleg` 0x302, `mideleg` 0x303, `mcounteren` 0x306, `mcountinhibit` 0x320, `satp`, every supervisor and hypervisor CSR, and the unused remainder of the trigger window 0x7A5–0x7AF. `mcounteren` should not exist without a lower privilege mode (vol. II §2.1.1.11) and `mcountinhibit` is optional (§2.1.1.12), so both take the trap rather than exist as registers nothing can read. A write shall raise cause 2 exactly when the address is a read-only address, `csr[11:10] = 2'b11`: `mhartid`, `mvendorid`, `marchid`, `mimpid`, `mconfigptr`, and the six counter views at 0xC00–0xC02 and 0xC80–0xC82. A write to a read/write address whose register holds only read-only bits shall be accepted, ignored and shall not trap: `mtval` 0x343, `mstatush` 0x310, `mip` 0x344, whose `MTIP`, `MSIP` and `MEIP` bits are read-only and read the input levels (`FR-48`), the performance counters at 0xB03–0xB1F and 0x323–0x33F, and `misa`, which legalises to the value it already holds (`FR-37`, `Q-30`). Vol. II §1.1.1 draws the line at the address: "Attempts to write a read-only register raise illegal-instruction exceptions. A read/write register might also contain some bits that are read-only, in which case writes to the read-only bits are ignored." A `CSRRS` or `CSRRC` with `rs1 = x0` shall read the addressed CSR, leave it unchanged and raise no trap, even when the CSR is read-only; a `CSRRSI` or `CSRRCI` with `uimm = 0` shall do the same. A WARL field shall legalise a written value rather than trap. The behaviour of each register is its own row — `misa` `FR-37`, `mhartid` `FR-32`, `mscratch` `FR-51`, `mtval` `FR-52`, `mepc`/`mcause` `FR-23`, `mtvec` `FR-29`/`FR-30`, `mstatus`/`mstatush`/`mie`/`mip` `FR-48`, the counters `FR-49` — and the instructions that reach the file are those of `Zicsr` (`FR-47`). | built in v0.8.8 as `rtl/csr_file.sv` (239 lines): the address map is `rtl/riscv_definitions_pkg.sv:335-359` and the three rules — present, read-only, writable-bits — are the functions at `:367-404`, which both the decoder (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:344-352`) and the file use, so the legality decided in ID cannot drift from the register that exists. `mepc`/`mcause` are written only by the trap path (`rtl/csr_file.sv:232-234`), `mstatush`/`mtval`/`mip` read zero (`:140`, `:144-145`), the identification registers read zero but for `mhartid` (`:156`), and the trigger and debug windows 0x7A0-0x7B3 are absent from `csr_implemented`, so an access raises cause 2. `T-03` (`riscv_small_csr_test`, wp 14) walks the map — every implemented address read with `CSRRS rd, csr, x0`, `mvendorid`/`marchid`/`mimpid`/`mconfigptr`/`mtval`/`mhpmcounter3` read zero, `csrrs mhartid` returns the driven identifier, the absent `medeleg` 0x302 and the read-only writes raise cause 2 with `mepc` at the `CSR*` — 50/50 at RTL `sha256:2e91301d…` / TB `sha256:0091e260…`, failing on the double `csr-illegal-noop` mutant; coverage `cg_csr` **5 of 5** group bins. **Two arms are unobserved and booked to `G-37`:** `rv32mi-p-mcsr`, which the acceptance names as the identification half's independent evidence and which **did not run** — there is no `rv32mi` environment (no `riscv_test_mi.h`; plan §4.3) — and the `NUM_TRIGGERS` > 0 window, since the built configuration has `NUM_TRIGGERS` = 0, so the five trigger addresses trap and the other branch of the acceptance is never taken. The `mstatus.MPP` WARL arm belongs to `FR-48`'s row (`FR-50` fixes no `mstatus` field) and is untested by `T-03` | `T-03` walks the map, and `rv32mi-p-mcsr` — an image the `NFR-05` bar already commits to — repeats the identification half. Every implemented address is read with `csrrs rd, csr, x0`, every read/write address is written with `csrrw x0, csr, rs1`, and the value read back obeys the register's own row: `mvendorid`, `marchid`, `mimpid` and `mconfigptr` read 0 on both accesses and raise nothing; `mhpmcounter3` reads 0; `mtval` reads 0; `misa` reads the same before and after the write. `csrrw x0, mcycle, rs1` with `rs1` = `0xFFFF_FFFF` changes what `cycle` (0xC00) reads, and the same holds for `minstret` and `instret`, while a `csrrw` to `cycle` itself raises cause 2. An access to `medeleg` (0x302) raises cause 2 with `mepc` at the `CSR*` instruction, and so do `mcountinhibit` (0x320) and any other address outside the list, sampled at two further addresses by the test. `csrrw x0, mhartid, rs1` raises cause 2, and so does a `csrrw` to each of the other read-only addresses — the three identification registers, `mconfigptr` and the counter views — while `csrrs rd, mhartid, x0` returns the hart identifier and raises nothing; `csrrw x0, mtval, rs1` with `rs1` = `0xFFFF_FFFF` leaves `mtval` at 0 and raises nothing, which is the read/write-address half of the rule (vol. II §1.1.1). In a build with `NUM_TRIGGERS` greater than 0, `csrrs rd, tselect, x0` at 0x7A0 reads a value and raises nothing, and `csrrw` to it and to `tdata1`..`tinfo` completes without a trap; in a `NUM_TRIGGERS` = 0 build each of the five addresses raises cause 2 as a read and as a write (`FR-57`), and `csrrs rd, dcsr, x0` at 0x7B0 raises cause 2 with `mepc` at the `CSR*`, as do `dpc`, `dscratch0` and `dscratch1`, because the debug window is reachable only in debug mode (`FR-53`, `FR-57`). A `csrrs` with `rs1 = x0`, and a `csrrsi` with `uimm = 0`, leave the CSR unchanged, which the test observes by reading the register before and after and by keeping a value that a stray write would destroy. A `csrrw` that writes a WARL field with an unsupported value — `mstatus.MPP` = `2'b00` — reads back a legal value and raises no trap (`FR-48`). **And one clause of this row is not met as built (`G-39`):** `minstret` 0xB02 returns the raw `minstret_q` (`rtl/csr_file.sv:147`, `:149`) while `instret` 0xC02 returns `instret_view = minstret_q + valid_ma + valid_wb` (`:125-127`, `:152`, `:155`), so the two addresses of one architectural counter disagree by the instructions in flight; `mcycle`/`cycle` are consistent because both read `mcycle_view`. The "read-only views" clause is therefore met for `cycle`, `cycleh`, `time` and `timeh` and not for `instret`/`instreth`, and no test reads `minstret`, which is why `T-03` passes over it | `F-08`, `F-09`, `BLK-CTL`, `G-37`, `G-39` | partial |
| FR-51 | The `mscratch` CSR shall be a read/write register at address 0x340 with no side effects: a read returns the value most recently written to it, and all six `Zicsr` instructions reach it. It shall reset to zero. That reset value is a **project choice for determinism, not an obligation of the standard**: vol. II §2.1.4 defines the state that exists after reset and leaves the rest UNSPECIFIED, so no software may assume zero in another implementation — but this implementation may, and does, promise it. The purpose of the register is the one the trap architecture gives it: it is the scratch state a handler uses as its frame pointer when no general-purpose register can be spared, which is the difference between a trap that can save the interrupted context and one that cannot, and therefore between a core that can switch tasks and one that cannot. | built in v0.8.8: `mscratch` is the register behind 0x340 (`rtl/csr_file.sv:141` read, `:225` write, `:210` reset to zero) and every `Zicsr` form reaches it through the shared read-modify-write path at `:167-172`. `T-03` (`riscv_small_csr_test`, wp 14) writes a pattern with `CSRRW` and reads it back with `CSRRS`, runs the other five forms on it, and its first `CSRRW` reads the reset value 0 into `rd` — 50/50 at RTL `sha256:2e91301d…` / TB `sha256:0091e260…`, failing on `csr-illegal-noop`. The return from the trap in this build is `JALR` to `mepc + 4`, because `MRET` is not decoded (`FR-45`, still `deferred`): the value surviving a trap entry is observed, the `MRET` form the acceptance names is not | `T-03` writes a pattern with `csrrw` and reads it back with `csrrs rd, mscratch, x0`; the value survives a trap entry and the return from it (`JALR` to `mepc + 4` in this build, `MRET` being `FR-45`), while the handler uses `mcause`, `mepc` and reads of `mstatus` and leaves `mscratch` alone; `csrrs`, `csrrc`, `csrrwi`, `csrrsi` and `csrrci` each reach the register, and a write of a new pattern through each form reads back unchanged, which is what "no side effects" means here. Immediately after reset it reads zero, and that observation is this implementation's choice and is not asserted of any other implementation. | `F-09`, `BLK-CTL` | verified |
| FR-52 | The `mtval` CSR at address 0x343 shall read as zero and shall carry no information: a read returns 0, no exception shall write it, and a write shall be accepted, ignored and shall never raise a trap (`Q-32`). The write rule is the address rule of vol. II §1.1.1: 0x343 is a read/write address, so `mtval` is a read/write register whose bits are read-only, and "writes to the read-only bits are ignored" — a write is legal, changes nothing, and does not trap. The consequence shall be stated for the handler that needs it: because `mtval` carries no encoding, an illegal-instruction handler that must know which instruction faulted reads that instruction from the address in `mepc`, which vol. II §2.1.1.16 names as the alternative to an informative `mtval`. | built in v0.8.8: `mtval` reads as a constant zero (`rtl/csr_file.sv:144`), its write mask is zero so a write is accepted, changes nothing and does not trap (`rtl/riscv_definitions_pkg.sv:397-398`), and the trap path writes only `mepc`/`mcause` (`rtl/csr_file.sv:232-234`), so no exception writes it. `T-03` (`riscv_small_csr_test`, wp 14) reads it with `CSRRS rd, mtval, x0`, writes `0xFFFF_FFFF` to it with `CSRRW` immediately before the trap section and observes no trap and no change, and reads it again after the traps — 50/50 at RTL `sha256:2e91301d…` / TB `sha256:0091e260…`, failing on `csr-illegal-noop`. Two acceptance arms are unobserved and booked to `G-37`: the read after a **request presenting 2 on `exception[3:0]`**, because `T-03` never asserts that pin, and the handler that fetches the faulting instruction through `mepc` | `T-03` reads `mtval` immediately after reset and after each trap it provokes — illegal instruction (cause 2), `ECALL` (11) and a synchronous request presenting 2 on `exception[3:0]` — and every read returns 0, both in the handler and after the `MRET`. `csrrw x0, mtval, rs1` with `rs1` holding `0xFFFF_FFFF` leaves `mtval` at 0 and raises no trap, which is what distinguishes it from `mhartid` and the counters (`FR-50`); `csrrs rd, mtval, x0` returns 0 and raises nothing. A context save built from `csrw` of every machine CSR therefore completes, and the value it reads back for `mtval` is 0. The handler's alternative is exercised too: after the illegal-instruction trap, `mepc` still holds the address of the faulting instruction, which the test fetches and decodes to recover the encoding. | `F-09`, `BLK-CTL`, `G-37` | verified |
| FR-53 | The core shall implement debug mode. It shall enter debug mode on a halt request from the Debug Module, on an `EBREAK` while `dcsr.ebreakm` is set, on a trigger whose action is enter-debug, and on the completion of a single step (`FR-58`). On entry it shall record the reason in `dcsr.cause` as one of the five values below, hold in `dpc` the value Debug Spec v1.0 §4.1.9 Table 3 fixes for that reason, record machine mode so that `dcsr.prv` reads `2'b11` (§4.1.10) — the only privilege level the hart has (`A-19`) — and preserve `dcsr.step`. **`dcsr.cause`** shall use the five values of §4.1.1 Table 2 that this implementation needs: 1 for entry by `EBREAK`, 2 for a trigger, 3 for a halt request, 4 for the completion of a single step, and 5 for the reset-halt request (`Q-40`). Table 2's sixth value, 6, is the halt-group cause and is not implemented, because this hart belongs to no halt group, so `dcsr.cause` shall never read 6. When two causes are present on one entry the hart shall record the one Table 2 ranks higher, and this implementation shall keep the table's order where the table permits a deviation — "`resethaltreq` and `haltreq` are allowed to be at different positions than shown as long as `resethaltreq` is higher priority than `haltreq`" — so the order shall be `resethaltreq` (5) over `haltreq` (3) over trigger (2) over `EBREAK` (1) over step (4), which is the order Table 2 shows once the unimplemented halt-group row is removed ("Implementations should implement priorities as shown"). **`dpc`** shall be a read/write register holding, per Table 3 ("Virtual address in DPC"), the "Address of the `EBREAK` instruction" after a breakpoint entry, the "Address of the next instruction to be executed" after a single step, "the address of the instruction which caused the trigger to fire" after a trigger entry, and the "Address of the next instruction to be executed at the time that debug mode was entered" after a halt request. `dpc` "must be able to hold all valid virtual addresses and the writability of the low bits depends on IALIGN" (§4.1.9), and with `IALIGN` = 32 until `FR-44` lands an address whose bit 1 or bit 0 is set is not a value `dpc` may hold: a written 1 in either low bit is not retained, and what `dpc` reads is always a 4-byte-aligned address. While the hart is in debug mode it shall not take interrupts (`FR-48`), shall not take the synchronous exceptions the instructions it executes would otherwise raise (§4.1.1), and shall execute only the instructions the specification allows in that state. `DRET` shall set the program counter to `dpc` and leave debug mode. | no debug support of any kind exists: the port list carries no debug signal (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:45-62`), the system opcode group decodes as a no-operation, so `DRET` and an `EBREAK` that enters debug mode are absent (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:348-353`), and no debug CSR is addressable (`G-13`) | `T-05` provokes each entry cause and reads `dcsr.cause`, which distinguishes them. The test asserts each cause number and not only that the four values differ: `EBREAK` entry reads `dcsr.cause` = 1, a trigger entry 2, a halt request 3, a single-step completion 4, and a reset-halt entry 5 (`FR-54`), and no observation reads 6. `dpc` is asserted against Table 3 for all four causes: after the `EBREAK` entry `dpc` equals the address of the `EBREAK` instruction itself and not the following instruction; after the trigger entry it equals the address of the instruction which caused the trigger to fire; after the single step it equals the address of the next instruction to be executed (`FR-58`); and after a halt request asserted at a known point it equals the address of the instruction that would have executed had debug mode not been entered, so the instruction at `dpc` executes on resume and the one after it does not. `dpc` is also written and read back: a write of `0x1003` reads back 4-byte aligned with bit 1 and bit 0 zero while `IALIGN` = 32, and the `DRET` at the end of the test resumes at exactly the address `dpc` then holds. An interrupt made pending while the hart is in debug mode is not taken there and is taken only after `DRET`, and then only while `mstatus.MIE` and the matching `mie` bit are set; `DRET` resumes at the address in `dpc` and no instruction between that address and the `DRET` executes twice. Phase: debug. | `F-12`, `BLK-CTL`, `C-13` | deferred |
| FR-54 | The core shall expose the hart side of the run control of Debug Spec v1.0 §3.1.5: `dbg_dmactive`, `dbg_haltreq`, `dbg_resumereq` and `dbg_resethaltreq` as inputs and `dbg_halted`, `dbg_running`, `dbg_havereset` and `dbg_resumeack` as outputs (§6, `C-13`). While `dbg_dmactive` is low the hart shall not be in debug mode and shall never enter it, and the low level shall be observable in three ways: `dbg_halted` shall read 0, `dbg_haltreq` and `dbg_resethaltreq` shall be ignored, and `dcsr` and `dpc` shall hold their reset values — `dcsr` = 0 and `dpc` = the `RESET_VECTOR` of `FR-41`. A halt request asserted while `dbg_dmactive` is low shall not be remembered: when `dbg_dmactive` later rises with no new request, the hart keeps running. That register pair is a **project choice for determinism, not an obligation of the standard**: §3.1.5 defines the run-control requests and their status and does not say what `dcsr` or `dpc` must read while the Debug Module is inactive, so no debugger may rely on this pair in another implementation, while this one promises it. A halt request shall be honoured at an instruction boundary and never in the middle of an instruction, and a halted hart shall ignore a further halt request. A resume request shall make a halted hart leave debug mode, and `dbg_resumeack` shall be asserted only once the hart is running. A reset-halt request shall make the hart enter debug mode immediately after reset, before the first instruction retires (`Q-40`). `dbg_havereset` shall report that the hart has been reset since the Debug Module last acknowledged it. No `ndmreset` shall be a core input: `dmcontrol.ndmreset` resets the platform (`Q-36`), and the hart observes that only as `rst_n`. | no debug signal in the port list and no run-control state in the core (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:45-62`); the reset is the two-state `rst_n` of `FR-41` with no halt-after-reset mode (`G-23`) | `T-05` drives each handshake and observes the status pair: while `dbg_haltreq` is asserted the hart reports `dbg_halted` with `dbg_running` low, and after `dbg_resumereq` it reports `dbg_running` with `dbg_halted` low; `dbg_resumeack` is never observed asserted in a cycle in which `dbg_running` is low; a halt request asserted in the middle of a multi-cycle instruction takes effect at the next instruction boundary, and that instruction completes and is not re-executed on resume; with `dbg_resethaltreq` asserted out of reset the hart is halted before the instruction at `RESET_VECTOR` retires and `dpc` holds `RESET_VECTOR`; `dbg_havereset` reads set after `rst_n` and is cleared only when the Debug Module acknowledges it, which the test drives; with `dbg_dmactive` low, `dbg_haltreq` asserted for the length of a whole program is ignored: `dbg_halted` stays 0 in every cycle, `dbg_running` stays high, and the hart retires the same instruction sequence it retires with no Debug Module attached; `dbg_dmactive` is then raised with no new request and the hart keeps running, which is what shows the low-phase request was not remembered. The reset values of the pair are observed on the reset-halt path of the same test, where the hart enters debug mode with `dcsr` = 0 apart from the field that entry sets (`cause` = 5) and `dpc` = `RESET_VECTOR`. The port group carries no `ndmreset` input, which the test checks in the port list of the build. Phase: debug. | `F-12`, `BLK-CTL`, `C-13`, `D-10` | deferred |
| FR-55 | While in debug mode the hart shall execute the abstract commands of Debug Spec v1.0 §3.1.7 for three accesses: a general-purpose register among the 32, a CSR among the addresses of `FR-50`, and a memory location through the data port. It shall report completion and the error condition the Debug Module expects, so that a command it cannot complete leaves a defined error code on `dbg_cmderr` with `dbg_busy` low instead of leaving the command outstanding. A memory access of an abstract command shall use the same data interface as an ordinary load or store (`FR-21`, `FR-38`). The `abstractcs`, `command` and `data0` registers of §3.1.14 shall stay Debug Module state and shall not become hart registers: the hart implements the execution and reporting side of the commands, not the registers. | no debug port, no CSR file and no debug-mode state (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:45-62`, `G-13`) | `T-05` reads and writes every one of the 32 general-purpose registers through the command channel and compares each value with the model, including `x0`, which stays zero whatever is written; it writes and reads back `mscratch` (0x340) the same way; it writes a word to a data address and reads it back, and the two accesses are observed on the same `data_addr`, `data_wr`, `data_wr_strb` and `data_rd` signals an ordinary store and load use, with no second port; a command naming a CSR outside the map of `FR-50` completes with a non-zero `dbg_cmderr` and `dbg_busy` low, the hart is still in debug mode afterwards, and `dpc` and `dcsr` read what they read before the command, so the command neither hangs nor corrupts debug state. Phase: debug. | `F-12`, `BLK-CTL`, `C-13` | deferred |
| FR-56 | With the program buffer enabled the hart shall execute the instructions the Debug Module places in it, in the order they were placed, while in debug mode, and shall return control to the Debug Module at the end of the buffer instead of executing the instruction at `dpc`. An exception an instruction of the buffer would raise shall be reported to the Debug Module as a command error and shall not be taken as a trap. The program counter used to run those instructions shall not be the architectural `pc` that `dpc` holds, so a run of the buffer leaves `dpc` unchanged. | no debug port and no program buffer; the system opcode group is a no-operation (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:45-62`, `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:348-353`) (`G-23`) | `T-05` loads a two-instruction buffer that writes a register and reads it back through the command channel, and observes the value the write produced and the order of the two instructions; a buffer instruction that would fault — an `ECALL`, and a `CSR*` of an address outside `FR-50` — is reported with a non-zero `dbg_cmderr`, no trap is taken, the hart is still in debug mode and its debug state is intact; `dpc` reads the same value before and after the buffer runs, which is what distinguishes the buffer's program counter from the architectural one. Phase: debug. | `F-12`, `BLK-CTL` | deferred |
| FR-57 | The core shall implement `NUM_TRIGGERS` triggers of the `mcontrol6` type only, exposed at `tselect` 0x7a0, `tdata1` 0x7a1, `tdata2` 0x7a2, `tdata3` 0x7a3 and `tinfo` 0x7a4 (Debug Spec v1.0 §5.1.7, `Q-38`). A trigger shall be able to match on the address of an instruction, on the address of a load or store, and on the data of a store. A `tselect` value beyond the last implemented trigger shall select no trigger, and a write to a trigger CSR beyond that range shall not create one. That unit is conditional on the parameter: with `NUM_TRIGGERS` = 0 the core shall implement no Sdtrig register at all, so an access to any of the five addresses 0x7A0–0x7A4 shall raise an illegal-instruction exception (cause 2) and shall not answer with a trigger count instead. The obligation is the Sdext chapter's: "If Sdext is implemented and Sdtrig is not implemented, then accessing any of the Sdtrig CSRs must raise an illegal instruction exception", and a debugger detects the case the way §5.1.1 prescribes: "Write 0 to `tselect`. If this results in an illegal instruction exception, then there are no triggers implemented." With `NUM_TRIGGERS` greater than 0, `tselect` shall be WARL and a write of an index greater than or equal to `NUM_TRIGGERS` shall legalise to a value the implementation chooses, neither trapping nor arming anything, because §5.1.7 states that "writes of values greater than or equal to the number of supported triggers may result in a different value in this register than what was written"; nothing therefore requires `tselect` to read back the written value, and what such an index shall report is that no trigger is selected — `tdata1.type` reads 0 and `tinfo` reports that no trigger is selected, whatever value the write legalised to in `tselect`. `tinfo` itself is optional in the standard — §5.1.1 tells the debugger to "Read tinfo. If that caused an exception, the debugger must read tdata1" — and this implementation provides it, which the standard permits; no requirement here depends on it, because `tdata1.type` answers the same question. A trigger's `action` field shall carry one of the two encodings of Debug Spec v1.0 §5.1.2 Table 1 and no other. **action 0** is "Raise a breakpoint exception", and `xepc` — `mepc` in this machine-mode-only build (`A-19`) — "must contain the virtual address of the next instruction that must be executed to preserve the program flow". **action 1** is "Enter Debug Mode", and `dpc` "must contain the virtual address of the next instruction that must be executed to preserve the program flow". action 1 shall therefore be legal only when the trigger's `dmode` is 1, which §5.1.2 states: "This action is only legal when the trigger's `dmode` is 1. Since `tdata1` is WARL, hardware must prevent it from containing `dmode=0` and `action=1`." Because `tdata1` is WARL, the core shall never read back `dmode` = 0 together with action = 1: a write of that pair shall legalise to some other value `tdata1` is permitted to hold, and shall arm no debug-entry trigger. No trigger shall fire while the hart is in debug mode, which §5.1 states as "Triggers do not fire while in Debug Mode". The five trigger CSRs shall be accessible in machine mode, while `dcsr`, `dpc`, `dscratch0` and `dscratch1` shall be accessible in debug mode only (`FR-50`, `FR-53`). | no trigger unit and no trigger CSR: there is no `tselect`..`tinfo` decode and no comparison against an instruction address, a data address or store data (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:348-353`, `G-24`; no CSR file at all, `G-13`) | `T-03` reads the five addresses after reset: `tselect` reads 0, `tdata1` reports `mcontrol6` for each implemented index, `tinfo` reports a trigger for each of the `NUM_TRIGGERS` indices, and every access raises no trap. A write to `tselect` of the index `NUM_TRIGGERS` and of `NUM_TRIGGERS`+1 arms nothing, and that is observed through `tdata1.type`, which reads 0, and through `tinfo`, which reports that none is selected; the test does not assert what `tselect` itself reads back, because §5.1.7 lets the write legalise. `T-05` arms, with action 1 and `dmode` = 1, a trigger on the address of a chosen instruction and observes debug mode entered with `dpc` at that instruction; it arms the same instruction with action 0 and observes the breakpoint exception of cause 3 with `mepc` at that instruction and no debug entry; it writes `dmode` = 0 together with action = 1 and observes that `tdata1` does not read that pair back and that no debug entry follows; it halts the hart by a halt request with a trigger armed on the following instruction and observes no event from that trigger while the hart is in debug mode; it arms a load-address trigger and observes a match on the matching access only, with the loads that miss producing no event; it arms a store-data match and observes it fire on the store carrying that data only. With `NUM_TRIGGERS` = 0 built, each of the five addresses raises cause 2 with `mepc` at the `CSR*` instruction, as a read (`csrrs rd, tselect, x0`) as well as a write (`csrrw x0, tselect, rs1`), and no match ever fires. Phase: debug. | `F-12`, `BLK-CTL`, `C-13` | deferred |
| FR-58 | `dcsr.step` shall be a read/write field of `dcsr` with reset value 0 that the **debugger** writes: while it is 1 and the hart is not in debug mode, every resume request shall execute exactly one instruction before the hart re-enters debug mode, and the hart shall leave the value alone — set by the debugger, unchanged by the hart, cleared by the debugger. That reading is the standard's: §4.1.9's field table defines the bit as R/W with reset 0, describes it as "When set and not in Debug Mode, the hart will only execute a single instruction and then enter Debug Mode", and constrains only the debugger — "The debugger must not change the value of this bit while the hart is running" — so no clause of the standard assigns the hart a role in clearing it, and this document does not invent one. After the one instruction executes the hart shall re-enter debug mode with `dpc` holding the address of the next instruction to be executed and `dcsr.step` still reading 1, so a second resume steps again. A step whose instruction is a jump or a taken branch shall leave `dpc` at the target. A step whose instruction fires a trigger that enters debug mode shall follow §4.1.5: "If executing or fetching the instruction causes a trigger to fire with action=1, Debug Mode is re-entered immediately after that trigger has fired. In that case cause is set to 2 (trigger) instead of 4 (single step)" — so the step ends in debug mode with `dcsr.cause` = 2 rather than 4 and `dpc` holding what Table 3 gives for a trigger entry (`FR-53`). A step whose instruction traps shall take the trap and re-enter debug mode, which §4.1.5 also fixes: "If control is transferred to a trap handler while executing the instruction, then Debug Mode is re-entered immediately after the PC is changed to the trap handler, and the appropriate tval and cause registers are updated. In this case none of the trap handler is executed" — so `mcause` and `mepc` record the trap, `mtval` still reads 0 because it is read-only zero (`FR-52`), `dcsr.cause` reads 4, `dpc` holds the handler's entry address, and no instruction of the handler executes. A step whose instruction is `WFI` shall retire it and advance `dpc` to the following instruction, because `WFI` is a no-operation in this implementation (`FR-46`, `Q-31`). | no debug mode and no `dcsr.step`: the system opcode group is a no-operation and there is no debug port (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:45-62`, `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:348-353`) (`G-23`) | `T-05` sets `dcsr.step`, resumes, and observes exactly one retirement with `dpc` advanced by 4 over an `addi` and `dcsr.step` still reading 1; it steps over a taken branch and observes `dcsr.step` still 1 and `dpc` at the target, not at the branch plus 4; a second resume with no new write to `dcsr` steps exactly one more instruction, which is what distinguishes a field the hart leaves alone from one it clears; it steps over an `ECALL` and observes `mcause` and `mepc` recorded at the `ECALL`, `dcsr.cause` = 4, `mtval` still 0, no instruction of the handler retired, and `dpc` at the handler's entry address; it steps over the instruction a trigger matches and observes `dcsr.cause` = 2 and `dpc` at that instruction, per §4.1.5; it steps over a `WFI` and observes the instruction retired with `dpc` at the instruction after it. In each case `instret` advances by exactly one instruction retired, except on the `ECALL` step, which retires none (`FR-49`), and the hart is halted afterwards with `dcsr.step` unchanged. Phase: debug. | `F-12`, `BLK-CTL` | deferred |
| FR-59 | When the `dbg_auth` strap is low the hart shall ignore `dbg_haltreq` and `dbg_resethaltreq`, shall never enter debug mode, shall never halt, and shall report `dbg_halted` = 0; an `EBREAK` with `dcsr.ebreakm` set shall raise a breakpoint exception (cause 3) instead of entering debug mode; and a trigger whose action enters debug mode shall be inert: entering debug mode is that action's only effect, so the strap forbids it and the trigger raises no exception and the instruction it matched retires, while a trigger whose action is 0 shall still raise the breakpoint exception of cause 3 (`FR-57`), because that action needs no debug entry and the strap removes none of it. When it is high, the behaviour of `FR-53`..`FR-58` shall hold. `dbg_auth` is a one-bit input the platform drives into the hart and is not Debug Module state, so a single binary can be locked down or left debuggable by the SoC (`C-14`, §6). The strap shall be an **additional** hart-side gate: Debug Spec v1.0 §3.1.12 places debugger authentication in the Debug Module — "When authenticated is clear, the DM must not interact with the rest of the hardware platform, nor expose details about the harts connected to the DM" — and this strap neither replaces that mechanism nor substitutes for it | no debug support and no strap: the port list has no `dbg_auth` input (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:45-62`), and `EBREAK` is a no-operation, so neither the cause-3 nor the debug-mode path exists (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:348-353`) (`G-23`) | With the strap low, `T-05` asserts `dbg_haltreq` and the hart keeps executing to the end of the program without entering debug mode, with `dbg_halted` = 0 in every cycle and `dbg_running` high; `dbg_havereset` still reports the reset, so a Debug Module can tell a reset from a halt; an `EBREAK` with `dcsr.ebreakm` set raises cause 3 with `mepc` at the `EBREAK` and does not enter debug mode; a trigger armed with action 1 produces no entry and the instruction it matched retires with no exception raised, while a trigger armed with action 0 raises cause 3 with `mepc` at the instruction it matched and does not enter debug mode. With the strap high, the same program follows `FR-53`..`FR-58`, which the same test observes in a second run with `dbg_auth` driven high. Phase: debug. | `F-12`, `C-14`, `D-11` | deferred |

Notes on the rows above:

- `FR-22` was the reconstruction of an existing mechanism while the vector was a constant; since v0.8.8 the
  vector is `mtvec.BASE` and the cause is the one the trap carries (`FR-29`, `FR-23`), and the row stays
  `implemented` for one reason only: its own arm — a request on the external `exception[3:0]` bus — is not
  exercised by any test (`G-37`).
- Twenty-five rows are `verified` today, each on the main acceptance cell of its named test, and §8 lists them:
  `FR-09` and `FR-10` to `T-01`, `FR-12` to `T-06` and `T-51`, `FR-18` to `T-08`, `FR-02` and `FR-16` to `T-11`,
  `FR-08` to `T-12` and the branch images `T-10`/`T-19`..`T-22`/`T-38`, `FR-11` to `T-13`, `T-14`, `T-15` and
  `T-51`, `FR-17` to `T-16`, `FR-06` to `T-18`, `FR-01` to `T-10`, `T-29`..`T-36` and `T-43`,
  `FR-03` to `T-26`, `T-27` and `T-40`, `FR-04` to `T-41`, `FR-05` to `T-42`, `FR-38` to `T-53`, `FR-42` to
  `T-52` and `FR-23`, `FR-25`, `FR-29`, `FR-32`, `FR-37`, `FR-47`, `FR-49`, `FR-51` and `FR-52` to
  `T-03`. `FR-07` is not one of them:
  `T-17` and `T-37` observe its mask clause, while the instruction-address-misaligned clause has no
  implementation (`G-13`), so the row is `partial`. `T-01` checks stores
  directly; the load
  result is checked only through the value that is subsequently stored, which is why `FR-09` and not only
  `FR-10` can be marked `verified`.
- Every as-built row is `implemented`, `partial` or `verified` unless its Status column says `deferred`, and
  the rows still waiting on the scoreboard are the ones `G-04` names: the reference model tracks the load/store
  subset (`LW`, `LB`, `LH`, `LBU`, `LHU`, `SW`) and the scoreboard compares store predictions only, so a row
  whose main acceptance cell no named test observes stays
  `implemented` or `partial` with `G-04` in its acceptance text. A second and independent model exists since
  2026-11-18 — the Spike commit-trace comparison of `T-47`..`T-49`, which agrees with the core commit by commit
  on `pc`, instruction, `rd` and written value for three of the 43 images — and it releases no row either: it
  observes no store address or data, never a commit that writes `x0`, and no bus protocol. Of the five
  `partial` rows, `FR-07` is held by `G-13` on its instruction-address-misaligned
  clause, `FR-21` by `G-35` on a read whose grant is withheld twice, `FR-24` by `G-38` on the reserved SYSTEM
  encodings and `FR-50` by `G-39` on `minstret`/`instret` disagreeing; each cites its gap in the Evidence
  cell, and the fifth, `FR-41`, is `partial` on `G-21` for its release half and on the unobserved two-build
  arm of its acceptance, `G-33` having closed on 2026-11-18 for the half `T-50` observes.
- `FR-20` and `FR-21` describe today's per-word fetch and the load's one-cycle presentation. They are not
  withdrawn, because the RTL still does this; `FR-27` and `FR-28` supersede the fetch for the product, and
  the load half of `FR-21` is superseded by the request/response port of `E8` (`G-35`) rather than by `FR-38`,
  which v0.8.7 built as the store half of the same stall. `A-03` is retired.
- `FR-26` is the row the elicitation round promoted from "unspecified" to a phase-1 obligation with ratified
  semantics; it did not need a new ID, so it keeps `FR-26` and changes text and acceptance only. `Q-15`
  (2026-10-28) settled the semantics as full ordering, so the row is `deferred` because the decoder orders
  nothing, not because the obligation was open.
- Rows `FR-27`..`FR-40` were written after the 2026-10-28 round. They are `deferred` because no RTL exists,
  not because the intent is doubtful: each names the phase in its acceptance text.
- `FR-41` was written in round 4 and is the only requirement that is not as-built and still `partial`: the
  asynchronous assertion half is what `rtl/` already does, while the synchronised release and the style macro
  do not exist (`G-21`). It is in §4 rather than §5 because a reset that releases at a different time changes
  what software observes, not only how the core is built.
- `FR-43` and `FR-44` exist because `FR-37` may not advertise what no requirement executes: `misa` reports a
  feature by its observable behaviour (vol. II §2.1.1.1), so the `M` bit needs `FR-43` and the `C` bit needs
  `FR-44`. Before those two rows existed, `C-10` and §7.1 committed both extensions with no row to satisfy, which made `FR-37`
  unsatisfiable. The pair also fixes the `IALIGN` question: it is 32 until `FR-44` lands, which is why the
  `FR-06`..`FR-08` acceptances carry a trap case the target will not need.
- `FR-45`, `FR-46` and `FR-47` are the carve-outs `FR-24` was missing. `MRET` is always provided and `WFI` is
  legal in machine mode (vol. II §2.1.3.2, §2.1.3.3), so a decoder that traps the whole system opcode is
  non-conformant even before `Zicsr` lands; the same holds for a reserved `FENCE` configuration and for the
  `rs1`/`rd` fields of `FENCE`.
- `FR-48` carries interrupt *entry*, not just interrupt *detection*: `FR-39` and `FR-40` say an interrupt is
  taken with a distinguishing cause, and this row says what the trap writes and in what order simultaneous
  requests are served. The signal set that carries a cause was `Q-35`, answered on 2026-10-28 as a cause bus
  beside three separate interrupt inputs; the port side of that answer is in `FR-22`, `FR-39` and `FR-40`, and
  this row keeps the architectural effect only.
- `FR-42` is the round-5 row: splitting a word-crossing access into two beats, each confined to one word. It is
  `verified` since v0.8.7 (`T-52`, `rtl/misaligned_split.sv`) and it is the row
  that makes `A-05`'s guarantee implementable rather than a promise the memory has to absorb. `FR-13` keeps
  the address formation and points here for what happens after it.
- `FR-06`..`FR-08` held for forward offsets only until 2026-11-04, because the jump/branch target added an
  unsigned `imm[20:0]` (`G-20`); with that closed and WP-7's evidence in, `FR-06` and `FR-08` are `verified` —
  `T-18` for the first, `T-12` and the five branch images for the second — and `FR-07`'s mask clause is
  observed by `T-17`/`T-37`, once `G-07` closed and the missing `rs1` forward (`G-31`) was added; that row
  stays `partial` because the instruction-address-misaligned half of its acceptance has no implementation
  (`G-13`, designer decision 2026-11-05). `G-19` recorded a write-back the load-use freeze could
  suppress and closed on 2026-11-05; `G-30`, the branch that freeze evaluated before the load result arrived,
  closed the same day under `T-16`, which is why `FR-17` is `verified`.
- `FR-07`, `FR-11` and `FR-12` state the intended behaviour since v0.3.1: `FR-07` continues at
  `(rs1 + imm) & ~1`, and `FR-11`/`FR-12` select the addressed lane before writing or extending. `FR-12` is
  `verified` since v0.7.4 (`T-06`) and `FR-11` since v0.7.9 (`T-13`..`T-15`); `FR-07` became half-observed in
  v0.8.0, when `rtl/jump_decision.sv:115-116` began clearing target bit 0 and `T-17`/`T-37` observed it
  (`G-07` closed), and it stays `partial` because a target whose bit 1 is set is fetched instead of raising
  instruction-address-misaligned (`G-13`).
- `FR-11` left the `partial` set in v0.7.9: the store path places the value in the addressed lane, raises the
  strobe for exactly the bytes it drives and reports the width decoded from `funct3`, which is what `T-13`,
  `T-14` and `T-15` observe, so the row is `verified` and no gap contradicts its clause. Until then it was
  `partial` because `Q-06` restated the clause as intent while the store drove one lane whatever
  `data_addr[1:0]` said (`G-08`, a release blocker until WP-6 closed it on 2026-11-05) — a requirement whose clause the RTL contradicts is not
  `implemented`; the earlier v0.3.1 draft of this note said the opposite and is superseded.
  `FR-12` left the category the same way in v0.7.4: the load path selects the lane from `data_addr[1:0]` and
  `T-06` proves all four offsets.
- `FR-24`, `FR-25` and `FR-27` were decided in round 2 and stay `deferred`: a decision is not an
  implementation, and no test observes them. `FR-24` and `FR-25` move to `implemented` only when the decoder
  traps, `FR-27` only when the fetch issues a line request.
