- `FR-50`, `FR-51` and `FR-52` are the machine-mode rows of v0.7, and they describe the CSR file **as a unit**
  — the address map, the access rules and the two registers the other rows reach into but do not define.
  `FR-47` says a `CSR*` instruction exists; `FR-50` says which addresses it may touch and what happens when it
  touches another. The per-register behaviour stays in the rows that own it, so no register is specified twice.
- `FR-37`, `FR-39`, `FR-40`, `FR-41`, `FR-46` and `FR-48` changed text in v0.7 without changing status: each
  now states the choice the standard left open, citing the round-7 answer rather than asking again (`Q-30`,
  `Q-31`, `Q-33`, `Q-35`). A row that names a decided question as open would have been a contradiction, not a
  deferral.
- The three uninterruptible points `FR-48` names are the ones a real-time kernel depends on: an interrupt
  raised while `MIE` is being set must be taken before the instruction after the `CSR*`, or the critical
  section that follows would run with a pending interrupt it believed it had missed.
- `FR-53`..`FR-59` are the debug rows of v0.7, and every one of them is `deferred` because nothing in `rtl/`
  implements a debug mode: there is no debug signal in the port list, no debug or trigger CSR, no trigger
  comparison and no `dcsr.step`. They are written against Debug Spec v1.0 and cite it by chapter (§0.7), and
  they do not describe the Debug Module: `FR-54` is the run-control handshake the core answers, `FR-55` and
  `FR-56` are the execution and reporting side of the commands, and the DM's own registers (`dmcontrol`,
  `dmstatus`, `abstractcs`, `command`, `data0`) stay outside the core (`C-13`).
- Four behaviours the debug standard leaves to the implementation are **named here and left to the uarch
  phase**, not decided: the value of `dpc` after an asynchronous halt (`FR-53`, where §4.1.1 governs it and is
  not paraphrased); what `DRET` does when it executes outside debug mode, which this revision does not require
  either way; how many words the program buffer holds, which is the DM's `progbufsize` and not a core
  parameter; and the cycle-level timing of the command and program-buffer handshakes, which belongs to
  `doc/uarch.md` beside the beat-level timing of the instruction port (§6). Naming them keeps a reader from
  mistaking silence for a requirement.
- `FR-59` is a gate, not the standard's authentication: Debug Spec v1.0 §3.1.12 makes the DM responsible for
  authenticating the debugger, so a system that relies on this strap alone and lets an unauthenticated DM drive
  the port is not conformant to that section. `C-14` says the same thing as a constraint, and no sentence here
  claims the Debug Specification defines a hart-side authentication strap.

### 4.1 Decoded instruction subset: as built, and phase-1 conformance

The first table is what `rtl/instruction_decode.sv` actually decodes today. It is **not** the target ISA of §7.1
and it is not what `README.md` claims. The second table is the obligation that `FR-24` and `FR-25` put on the
same decoder: it names the encodings that must **not** execute, and each row is what a phase-1 conformance
suite has to observe.

| Opcode (binary) | Mnemonic | Accepted `funct3` | Function-field handling | Decode site |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0110111` | `LUI` | undefined | upper immediate only | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:382-387` |
| `0010111` | `AUIPC` | undefined | upper immediate only | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:388-394` |
| `1101111` | `JAL` | undefined | jump immediate | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:395-403` |
| `1100111` | `JALR` | only `000` — any other `funct3` traps with cause 2 | I-type immediate | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:294-296` (legality), `:404-413` (decode) |
| `1100011` | `BEQ`, `BNE`, `BLT`, `BGE`, `BLTU`, `BGEU` | `000`, `001`, `100`, `101`, `110`, `111` — `010` and `011` trap with cause 2 | comparison in `jump_decision`, not the ALU | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:297-302` (legality), `:414-417` (decode), `rtl/jump_decision.sv:117-164` |
| `0000011` | `LB`, `LH`, `LW`, `LBU`, `LHU` | `000`, `001`, `010`, `100`, `101` — `011`, `110`, `111` trap with cause 2 | `funct3[2]` selects zero or sign extension | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:303-308` (legality), `:418-431` (decode), `rtl/memory_access.sv:136-152` |
| `0100011` | `SB`, `SH`, `SW` | `000`, `001`, `010` — `011`..`111` trap with cause 2 | width comes from `access_size(funct3)` (`rtl/riscv_definitions_pkg.sv:276-283`, `rtl/riscv_small.sv:139`): `00` is byte, `01` halfword, `10` word; the store carries `data_wr_strb[3:0]` with the value already in the addressed lane (`rtl/execution.sv:205-243`, `rtl/riscv_small.sv:283-284`), and `FR-11` is `verified` by `T-13`/`T-14`/`T-15`. **Two behaviours sit outside the width, and both are built since v0.8.7:** a word-crossing access leaves the core as two beats, each confined to one word — beat 1 at the access's byte address with the strobes that stay inside that word, beat 2 at `(base & ~32'h3) + 4` with the remaining ones (`FR-42`, `T-52`) — and a store is held in MA until the data memory grants it, with no separate write response (`FR-38`, `T-53`) | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:309-314` (legality), `:432-443` (decode), `rtl/riscv_small.sv:139` |
| `0010011` | `ADDI`, `SLLI`, `SLTI`, `SLTIU`, `XORI`, `SRLI`, `SRAI`, `ORI`, `ANDI` | `000`, `001`, `010`, `011`, `100`, `101`, `110`, `111` | shift amount and arithmetic/logical choice from the immediate's high bits; the shift `funct7` must be `0000000`, or `0100000` on `101` (`SRAI`), or the encoding traps with cause 2 | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:315-323` (legality), `:444-457` (decode) |
| `0110011` | `ADD`, `SUB`, `SLL`, `SLT`, `SLTU`, `XOR`, `SRL`, `SRA`, `OR`, `AND` | `000`..`111` | bit 5 of the register function field selects `SUB`/`SRA`, and `funct7` must be `0000000` — or `0100000` on `000`/`101` — or the encoding traps with cause 2, the `M` group included | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:324-330` (legality), `:458-464` (decode) |
| `0001111` | `FENCE` (and `FENCE.I`, aliased) | — | decoded as no operation | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:465-467` |
| `1110011` | `ECALL`, `EBREAK`, `MRET`/`WFI` (legal no-operation), `CSR*` | `000` for `ECALL`/`EBREAK`/`MRET`/`WFI`, `001`/`010`/`011`/`101`/`110`/`111` for `CSR*`; `100` traps | `CSR*` validated against the address map of `FR-50` | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:331-356` (legality), `:468-481` (decode) |

Phase-1 conformance: the encodings below are outside the implemented subset, so `FR-24` requires each of them
to trap **until the requirement that implements it lands**. The middle column is what happens today, and is the
reason the row exists; the last column names the requirement that retires the trap, so a trap is never a
permanent statement about an extension.

| Encoding family | As built today | Phase-1 obligation | Governed by |
|---|---|---|---|
| an opcode with no decode arm | traps with cause 2 (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:357-364`), no register write and no memory access | illegal-instruction trap | `FR-24` |
| `0110011` with `funct7` other than `0000000` and `0100000`, including the whole `M` group (`funct7` `0000001`) | traps with cause 2 (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:324-330`) | `M` executes (`FR-43`); until then the encoding traps | `FR-24`, `FR-43` |
| `0010011` with a non-zero `funct7`, other than `0100000` on `funct3` `101` | traps with cause 2 (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:315-323`) | illegal-instruction trap | `FR-24` |
| `1100111` (`JALR`) with `funct3` other than `000` | traps with cause 2 (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:294-296`) | illegal-instruction trap | `FR-24` |
| `1100011` with `funct3` `010` or `011` | traps with cause 2 (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:297-302`) | illegal-instruction trap | `FR-24` |
| `0000011` with `funct3` `011`, `110` or `111` | traps with cause 2 (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:303-308`) | illegal-instruction trap | `FR-24` |
| `0100011` with `funct3` `011`..`111` | traps with cause 2 (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:309-314`) | illegal-instruction trap | `FR-24` |
| `0001111` with `funct3` other than `000` (`fence`) and `001` (`fence.i`) | accepted and executes as a no-operation — the legality arm takes `FENCE` unconditionally (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:291-293`), so the reserved `funct3` is not policed | illegal-instruction trap | `FR-24` |
| `1110011` `ECALL` and `EBREAK` | legal traps with cause 11 and 3 (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:335-336`), `mepc` at the instruction; **a non-zero `rd`/`rs1` is accepted rather than trapped** (`G-38`) | a trap that is not a no-operation: causes 11 and 3, `mepc` at the instruction itself, and the reserved fields policed | `FR-24`, `FR-25`, `G-38` |
| `1110011` `MRET` (`0x3020_0073`) and `WFI` (`0x1050_0073`) | legal no-operation (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:337`) | **never trap**: `MRET` is always provided (`FR-45`), `WFI` is a no-operation (`FR-46`) | `FR-45`, `FR-46` |
| `1110011` CSR encodings | the six `Zicsr` forms execute (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:341-356`), an absent address and a write to a read-only address trap with cause 2 | the six CSR instructions execute against the address map of `FR-50` | `FR-47`, `FR-50` |
| `1110011` `DRET` (`0x7b20_0073`) | traps with cause 2: it is a `CSR`-field encoding 0x7b2 outside the map | executes only in debug mode, where it returns to `dpc` (`FR-53`); what the encoding does outside debug mode is not required (`FR-53`) | `FR-53`, `FR-24` |
| a compressed encoding (low two bits other than `11`) | not decoded at all: the 32-bit word is decoded on its `opcode` field, and a parcel whose low bits are not `11` reaches no decode arm, so it traps with cause 2 | illegal-instruction trap until `FR-44` | `FR-44`, `FR-24` |
| a `FENCE` with `fm` other than `0000` (`fence.tso`, or a reserved configuration the base treats as `FENCE`), and any value in the `rs1`/`rd` fields of `FENCE` or `FENCE.I` | no operation | **never trap**: a reserved configuration behaves as `fm=0000`, and the fields are ignored (`FR-26`, `FR-31`) | `FR-26`, `FR-31` |

Absent entirely from the decoder as an execution path: RV32M (`funct7 = 0000001` shares opcode `0110011` and,
since v0.8.8, **traps with cause 2** instead of aliasing onto the base ALU), RV32A, RV32C, RV32F/D,
`Zifencei`, the debug-mode instruction set (`DRET`), every trigger and debug CSR of `FR-50`'s fifth group, and
every supervisor or hypervisor encoding (`G-06`); `Zicsr` and the machine CSRs are no longer in this list — they
are built (`FR-47`, `FR-50`). `M` and `C` are nevertheless part of the target ISA in phase 1 (`D-07`), which
is why the gap, not the decoder, is the open item; `Q-05` (2026-10-28) fixed the obligation — every family in
the conformance table traps until its own requirement implements it, and nothing is silently a no-operation.

## 5. Non-functional requirements

No timing, area, power, or coverage **target** number exists yet: what exists is the measurement, and no
measured figure has been promoted to a target. `NFR-05` passes **42 of the 42 bundled images** — and **43 of
the 43** images the harness runs — measured on 2026-11-19 at RTL `sha256:f84ea86b…` and TB
`sha256:7860d1b0…`; the extra image is one **project-local** one, `rv32ui-p-jalr_unaligned`, built from
`scripts/verify/sanity/local/`, which passes and is `FR-07` evidence rather than a bundled-suite image. There
is **no failure left**: `rv32ui-p-ma_data` passes since v0.8.7 (`FR-42`, `T-52`), and `rv32ui-p-fence_i` and
`rv32ui-p-jalr` passed before it, so the `rv32ui` leg of the bar is met and nothing in it is attributed to
`G-15`, `G-07`, `G-22`, `G-31` or `G-34` any more. `NFR-06` is measured on 2026-11-19 at **40 of the 40 bins
of its `A-09` bar** — 37 instruction bins, one per decoded instruction of §4.1, plus 3 memory-width bins — and
at **all 52 of the bins the report returns**, 100 %, with `uncovered` empty, over **6** coverpoints: `cp_inst`
37/37, `cp_width` 3/3 and `cg_beat` 2/2, plus the `cg_mem` 13 (`cp_width` 3, `cp_lane` 4, `cp_dir` 2 and
`cp_subword_lane` 4), of which the bar counts the three width bins. `cg_beat`/`cp_beat` is WP-13's addition —
`split_beat1` and `split_beat2`, both hit 63 times, so a split access is measured by the coverage report and
not only checked by a testbench checker. The bar is therefore closed and `G-03` with it. What the bar does **not** do is observe a
retire — a bin is still an accepted fetch, not a retire, because coverage was never migrated to the retire
stream WP-8 added, and `G-28`'s closure does not remove that limit — so what remains of `Q-11`'s
signoff bar on the coverage side is the `FR` half of `A-09`, not the bins. The retire stream is used
elsewhere since 2026-11-18: `T-47`, `T-48` and `T-49` compare the core's commit trace against Spike's
`--log-commits` output commit by commit on `pc`, instruction, `rd` and written value — 459 of 459 commits
on `rv32ui-p-add`, 285 of 285 on `rv32ui-p-beq` and 477 of 477 on `rv32ui-p-st_ld` — which is the strongest
external check this document has that the instruction stream is right, and still no `verified` mark for
`G-04`'s six rows, because the compared line carries no store address or data, no commit that writes `x0`
and no bus protocol, and covers 3 of the 43 images. The bar is now
declared as a covergroup (`cg_inst`'s `cp_inst` and `cg_mem`'s `cp_width`) rather than only as prose, so
the denominator moves with the declaration instead of being restated. The 37 instruction bins are exactly the 37
decoded instructions of §4.1: the table's two no-operation rows — `FENCE`/`FENCE.I` and
`ECALL`/`EBREAK`/`CSR*`, which the RTL decodes as no-operations (`G-06`, `G-13`) — carry no bin, and a word
outside the decoded subset decodes to a sentinel that names no bin, so 37 + 3 = 40 is derivable here without
reading the testbench. Both measurements are recorded in `doc/measurements/`. `E5` delivered the memory side —
`cg_mem` is closed and `cp_width` is 3/3 — `E4` closed the nineteen instruction bins WP-6 left open, and `E3`
delivered the retire observation on 2026-11-07 (WP-8, §9), so no epic item is owed behind the instrument; the
covergroup itself still samples the fetch, so a bin remains a fetch and not a retire (the epics of
`.opencode/plan/macro-plan-verification-to-signoff.md`: `E3` the retire observation, `E4` RV32I closure, `E5`
the store width and lane). Rows that need one carry `TBD` and a gap; the
rows whose target is countable (a pass/fail bar) state it and name the gap that blocks it. The dates are the date
by which the owner must supply a number, not a target value. v0.2 moved every `TBD` date to 2026-12-31, the
deadline agreed in the 2026-10-28 round; no number was invented.

Round 3 (`Q-08`, `Q-09`, `Q-10`) settled the four `TBD` rows as **measure first, then fix the number**: for
`NFR-01`..`NFR-04` the measurement method is normative and the number is not — a report with a measurement in
it is the deliverable, and a frequency, a CPI, an area or a power figure becomes a requirement only after that
report exists. `NFR-05` and `NFR-06` are the opposite case: their bar is a decision, `Q-11` confirmed it, and
the gaps that block it are release blockers.

| ID | Requirement (shall) | Target | Condition | Method | Traces to | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| NFR-01 | The core shall report the clock frequency its first synthesis achieves, and the part it was achieved on, before any frequency target is recorded. | TBD(designer, 2026-12-31) — no frequency and no target part are recorded, and none may be until the first report exists (`A-08`, `Q-08`) | worst-case slow corner, for each named configuration of §5.2 | post-implementation static timing report: achieved Fmax, worst negative slack over all paths, and the part; the measured number then becomes the `NFR-01` target | `G-05` | deferred |
| NFR-02 | The core shall report the cycles it takes to run one named benchmark kernel, before any throughput target is recorded. | TBD(designer, 2026-12-31) — no CPI or kernel target is recorded until the measurement exists (`Q-09`) | kernel and memory latency model fixed, instruction cache enabled | cycles retired over cycles spent, counted in simulation on the named kernel; the measured value then fixes the target | `G-05` | deferred |
| NFR-03 | The core shall report the resources its first synthesis consumes, with and without the instruction cache, before any area target is recorded. | TBD(designer, 2026-12-31) — no area budget is recorded until the report exists (`Q-10`) | default synthesis settings, target part | post-synthesis resource or gate-count report, at `ICACHE_BYTES` = 0 and 2048 | `G-05` | deferred |
| NFR-04 | The core shall report the power its implementation consumes under a named activity vector, with and without the instruction cache, before any power budget is recorded. | TBD(designer, 2026-12-31) — no power budget is recorded until the report exists (`Q-10`) | activity from a named simulation vector | post-implementation vector-based power analysis, at `ICACHE_BYTES` = 0 and 2048 | `G-05` | deferred |
| NFR-05 | The core shall pass every applicable image of the bundled RISC-V test suite: `rv32ui-p-*`, `rv32um-p-*`, `rv32uc-p-*` and `rv32mi-p-*`, plus `rv32ua-p-*` where `ENABLE_A` = 1. | 0 failures over the applicable subset; the `rv32ui` leg is the signoff bar of 2026-10-28 (`A-09`, `Q-11`), and the `rv32um` and `rv32uc` legs are owed by `FR-43` and `FR-44`, while the `rv32mi` leg is owed by `FR-24`, `FR-46` and `FR-47` together | Vivado XSim, images generated by the sanity-test flow; the applicable subset is fixed by §7.1 phase 1 — `rv32um` because `M` is committed (`FR-43`), `rv32uc` because `C` is (`FR-44`), `rv32mi` for CSR access, illegal instruction, `ma_fetch`, `wfi` and `mcsr`, and `rv32ua` only where `A` is built (`FR-35`) | compile each test image from **two** source directories — the bundled `src/riscv-tests/isa/rv32ui/*.S` and the project-local `scripts/verify/sanity/local/*.S`, which `scripts/verify/sanity.cmake` globs together — simulate it, record pass or fail per test; `G-10` closed 2026-10-30; measured **42 of the 42 bundled images** and **43 of the 43 images run** on 2026-11-19 at RTL `sha256:f84ea86b…` and TB `sha256:7860d1b0…`, the 43rd being the project-local `rv32ui-p-jalr_unaligned`, which is `FR-07` evidence and not a bundled-suite image (`build/sanity/summary.json`: 43 pass, 0 fail); `rv32ui-p-ma_data` is the image that moved, and it passes since v0.8.7 (`FR-42`, `T-52`) | `G-06` and `G-13`, which the `rv32um`/`rv32uc`/`rv32mi` legs wait on — the `rv32ui` leg has no failure left, and the row stays `partial` because its `shall` names four legs and only one is measured | partial |
| NFR-06 | Functional coverage shall close on the instruction set and memory behaviour it claims. | every **phase-1** `FR` row `verified`, one functional-coverage bin per decoded instruction of §4.1 and one per memory width — the signoff bar of `A-09` | covergroup populated and sampled by the regression | coverage report from `xcrg` or IMC, with uncovered bins enumerated; measured **40 of the 40 `A-09` bar bins** (`cp_inst` 37/37, `cp_width` 3/3) and **52 of the 52 report bins** over **6** coverpoints (`cg_mem` 13/13, `cg_beat` 2/2, `cp_inst` 37/37), 100 %, no uncovered bin, on 2026-11-19 at RTL `sha256:f84ea86b…` and TB `sha256:7860d1b0…` over the **46** of the **49** manifest rows the coverage run executes — the three `spike:` rows `T-47`..`T-49` are trace comparisons and not coverage runs (`build/cov_report/summary.json`) — **and again on 2026-11-21 at RTL `sha256:2e91301d…`, 66 of the 66 report bins over 9 coverpoints with the `A-09` bar still 40 of 40, over 47 of the 50 manifest rows** (the three `spike:` rows excluded by design); the bar is still `cp_inst` + `cp_width` and is derived in `scripts/verify/coverage.cmake:333`, so a new covergroup moves the report total and not the bar; the bins are fetches, not retires — coverage was not migrated to the retire stream that closed `G-28` — so that limit stays | `G-04`, `G-11`; `G-28` (closed 2026-11-07) leaves the fetch-not-retire limit behind | partial |
| NFR-07 | The core shall synthesise with no inferred latches and no combinational loops. | 0 latches, 0 loops | default synthesis settings, target part, every named configuration | synthesis and lint report; measured **0 inferred latches** (`REF_NAME` = `LDCE`/`LDPE`/`LDNE`) and **0 combinational loops** (DRC `LUTLP-1`, `Violations found: 0`) on 2026-11-18 at RTL `sha256:b914f2e1…` by `make lint_fpga` — Vivado 2024.1, part `xczu3eg-sbva484-1-i`, out-of-context, default settings, 10 sources from `srclist/riscv_small.srclist`, 4069 cells, 1405 flip-flops, linter 0 errors and 0 critical warnings, the netlist pass's single critical warning being the OOC tricell conversion (`doc/measurements/NFR-07-lint.md`, `build/vivado.log:354`) | `G-05`, narrowed to `NFR-01`..`NFR-04` by this measurement: one part and the only buildable configuration, because the named configurations of §5.2 cannot be elaborated yet (`G-13`, `G-15`, `G-16`), so each one that becomes buildable needs its own run | verified |

## 5.1 Constraints

| ID | Constraint | Origin | Impact |
|---|---|---|---|
| C-01 | One clock domain; the reset input is active-low `rst_n`; the reset style is hybrid — asynchronous assertion, de-assertion synchronised inside the core through `RESET_SYNC_STAGES` flops (`FR-41`, `D-09`). | `D-09`; today `rtl/riscv_small.sv:45-47`, `rtl/instruction_fetch.sv:108-110` have no synchroniser (`G-21`) | the integrator drives `rst_n` directly and does not add a second synchroniser; de-assertion timing is the core's responsibility (`FR-41`) |
| C-02 | A top-level clock-enable input exists, but in the current RTL it reaches only the trap-state registers and `branch_taken_q` inside `control`, the register file's write-back enable, and — since WP-13 — `misaligned_split`; the pipeline stages themselves are not gated by it. | `rtl/riscv_small.sv:292` (`control`), `:166` (the register file's write-back enable) and `:271` (`misaligned_split`), `rtl/control.sv:103-110` (`branch_taken_q`) and `:341-342` (`sepc`/`scause`) | pausing `clk_en` does not pause the core (`G-12`) |
| C-03 | Instruction and data memories are external ports; no cache and no memory are instantiated inside the core today. | `rtl/riscv_small.sv:49-61` | memory latency and behaviour are the integrator's contract (`A-01`, `A-10`); the target adds an instruction cache inside the core (`D-04`, `C-08`) |
| C-04 | Simulation and elaboration are Vivado XSim 2024.1 or newer, driven by CMake and `make`. | `README.md`, `CMakeLists.txt` | no other simulator is targeted or checked |
| C-05 | The default DUT top is `riscv_small` and the default verification top is `riscv_small_tb`. | `srclist/riscv_small.srclist`, `CMakeLists.txt` | port names and widths are fixed by these files |
| C-06 | `NUM_HARTS` shall be a synthesis parameter of the main configuration package with legal values 1, 2 and 4 and default 1; the hart identifier shall be an input, not a parameter; at least one instantiated hart shall have identifier 0, identifiers shall be unique, and with `NUM_HARTS` = 1 the identifier shall be 0. | `D-03`; vol. II §2.1.1.5 | one RTL serves one, two or four harts (`FR-33`); the SoC must wire each instance's identity; elaboration fails on any other value |
| C-07 | `ENABLE_A` shall gate the `A` extension, default 0 in phase 1, and elaboration shall fail when `NUM_HARTS > 1` and `ENABLE_A = 0`. | `D-03`, `D-06` | an SMP build cannot be produced without atomics; `misa` follows the parameter (`FR-37`). `ENABLE_A` = 0 is the default for every phase-1 configuration, `MCU_RTOS` included, because the MVP RTOS masks interrupts for its critical sections and needs no atomics (`Q-20`, 2026-10-28); `A` stays phase 2 and `MCU_SMP` is the only named configuration that sets it to 1, a default that flips only if Zephyr becomes the target |
| C-08 | `ICACHE_BYTES` shall select the instruction cache size from 0, 1024, 2048 and 4096 bytes, default 2048; the value 0 shall remove the cache; elaboration shall fail when `ICACHE_BYTES > 0` and `Zifencei` is not enabled. | `D-04` | a cache that software cannot invalidate is not buildable (`FR-31`); `ICACHE_BYTES = 0` is the flash-resident build |
| C-09 | `ICACHE_LINE` shall be 32 bytes (8 words) in phase 1 and shall not be independently tunable. | `D-04`, `A-11` confirmed 2026-10-28 (`Q-14`) | the fill burst length follows from the line size (`FR-27`); a different line size is a new variant, not a knob |
| C-10 | `M` and `C` shall always be enabled and shall not be parameters, which is only meaningful because `FR-43` requires the `M` instructions to execute and `FR-44` requires the 16-bit encodings and `IALIGN=16`. | `D-07`; vol. II §2.1.1.1 | every toolchain target emits them; `misa` may report them only because those rows exist (`FR-37`); without them this constraint would advertise an extension the core does not implement |
| C-11 | A compiled binary shall be valid only for the configuration it was built for; nothing is binary-portable across `NUM_HARTS`, `ENABLE_A` or `ICACHE_BYTES`. | `D-05` accepted and `A-12` confirmed 2026-10-28 (`Q-16`) | releases are named per variant; a test run must state the configuration it exercised |
| C-12 | Register `x0` shall read as all zeros and every write to it shall be discarded, because volume I §1.1.1 says "Register `x0` is hardwired with all bits equal to 0"; a load with `rd = x0` shall still raise the exceptions its access would raise and still cause its side effects, per volume I §1.1.6. | normative rule, RISC-V ISA v20260120 vol. I §1.1.1 and §1.1.6; replaces retired assumption `A-02` (`Q-22`) | this is not a project choice and not tunable at synthesis; `FR-14` is its test |
| C-13 | The debug contract shall be the RISC-V **Debug Specification v1.0** (ratified 2025-02-21) with an external Debug Module behind a JTAG DTM: the core shall implement the hart side of that interface, and the Debug Module Interface (`REQ_*`/`RSP_*` of appendix A, Table 1) shall stay the DTM-to-DM link and shall not become a core port. | `D-10`, accepted 2026-10-28 (`Q-36`, `Q-37`); Debug Spec v1.0 ch. 3 and app. A | one DM can serve several harts and the core carries no transport logic; the SoC wires JTAG to the DM and the debug port group of §6 to the hart. Adding the DMI to the core would put the DM inside it and rewrite `FR-54`..`FR-56` |
| C-14 | `dbg_auth` shall be a one-bit **input** the platform drives into the hart — not a synthesis parameter and not Debug Module state — and it shall be an **additional hart-side gate** and not the Debug Specification's authentication: §3.1.12 places authentication in the Debug Module ("When authenticated is clear, the DM must not interact with the rest of the hardware platform, nor expose details about the harts connected to the DM"), so the strap gates what the hart does with a request and neither replaces nor substitutes for the DM's own check. | `D-11`, accepted 2026-10-28 (`Q-39`); Debug Spec v1.0 §3.1.12 | `FR-59` states the behaviour when the strap is low. A platform that skips the DM's authentication and relies on this strap alone is not conformant to §3.1.12, and this document makes no claim that the standard defines a hart-side authentication strap. Because the gate is a port and not a build switch, one binary serves both a locked-down and a debuggable part |
| C-15 | The machine-mode-only scope of `A-19` shall constrain the CSR and trap surface, not only the documentation: the core shall implement no `medeleg` or `mideleg`, no supervisor or hypervisor CSR, no `satp`, and no `mstatus` field that has meaning only below machine mode — except the fields the standard makes mandatory in M-mode, which `FR-48` lists and defines. A trap shall never delegate to a lower privilege level, and an access to any of those addresses shall take the illegal-instruction trap of `FR-50`. A request for task isolation is a new product, not a configuration of this one: it retires this constraint and reopens `FR-48`, `FR-50` and §7.1 together. | `A-19` confirmed 2026-10-28 (`Q-34`) | the CSR map of `FR-50` is closed and small, and `FR-48` states `MPRV`, `TW`, `FS` and `XS` as read-only zero instead of implementing fields nothing can use; the delegation registers are `medeleg` 0x302 and `mideleg` 0x303, and both trap; no profile conformance suite joins `NFR-05`, because no profile is claimed |
| C-16 | The verification reference model shall be driven only by observations published by the monitor: no sequence, test or driver shall write the program or the instruction stream into it, so the same model runs in a passive environment (`is_active = UVM_PASSIVE`) and can be replaced by a Spike commit-trace comparison. The program image is configuration — a test-time input that initialises the model's own state once — and not a transaction; the model keeps its own architectural state and never reads the memory the driver mutates. | `Q-43`, accepted 2026-11-04; **met 2026-11-07 by WP-8** — the monitor publishes the retire event and nothing else feeds the model | met: the model consumes the retire events of `riscv_small_retire_if` (`valid_wb && ma_wb_clk_en`) and keeps its own architectural state; `program_port`, `rm_export` and `publish_program()` are deleted (`grep` empty in `tb/` and `scripts/`) and `riscv_small_program_image` reaches it as configuration through `uvm_config_db`, once. The model therefore runs unchanged with `is_active = UVM_PASSIVE`, and the retire stream is the seam a Spike commit-trace comparison consumes. The reason the rule exists is unchanged: a model fed by the sequence can be reused in neither a passive agent nor a Spike comparison |

## 5.2 Configuration

The parameters below are the whole configuration surface of the product. Every legal combination is formed
from these eight; the three named configurations after the table are the ones this specification uses
elsewhere. A configuration is chosen at synthesis and is not discoverable at run time except through `misa`
(`FR-37`).

| Parameter | Legal values | Default | Effect on behaviour | Constraint |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `NUM_HARTS` | 1, 2, 4 | 1 | number of harts the design instantiates; each hart sees the same core and a distinct identifier | `C-06`, `FR-33` |
| `ENABLE_A` | 0, 1 | 0 | the `A` extension (Zaamo and Zalrsc) is present and advertised only when 1 | `C-07`, `FR-35`, `FR-37` |
| `ENABLE_M` | 0, 1 | 0 | declared in v0.8.8 beside `ENABLE_A`, and reaching `misa` bit 12 and nothing else: it advertises `M` without making the encodings execute, which `FR-37` forbids — the parameter exists so `FR-43` has a place to land, and setting it before then is booked to `G-37` | `FR-37`, `FR-43`, `D-11` |
| `ENABLE_C` | 0, 1 | 0 | the same for `C` and `misa` bit 2: declared, advertised only, and setting it before `FR-44` lands is booked to `G-37` | `FR-37`, `FR-44`, `D-11` |
| `ICACHE_BYTES` | 0, 1024, 2048, 4096 | 2048 | instruction cache capacity; 0 removes the cache, so no fetch can hit | `C-08`, `FR-27`, `FR-31` |
| `ICACHE_LINE` | 32 | 32 | cache-line size in bytes, hence the fill burst length; fixed in phase 1 and not independently tunable | `C-09`, `FR-27` |
| `RESET_SYNC_STAGES` | integer ≥ 2 | 2 | number of clock-enable flops the reset de-assertion passes through before the core sees a release, so it sets the release latency (`FR-41`) | `C-01`, `FR-41` |
| `RESET_VECTOR` | any 32-bit address, aligned to the instruction alignment of the build (`IALIGN`) | `0x8000_0000` | the address of the first fetch after reset (`FR-41`); a build input, not a CSR, so software cannot move it and the boot image must link at it | `FR-41`, `Q-33` |
| `NUM_TRIGGERS` | 0, 2, 4 | 2 | number of `mcontrol6` triggers the trigger unit implements; 0 removes the unit, so the five addresses 0x7A0–0x7A4 are unimplemented and an access to any of them traps with cause 2 (`FR-57`) | `FR-57`, `Q-38` |
| `DEBUG_ENABLED` | 0, 1 | 1 | 1 builds the debug block and the debug port group of §6; 0 removes both, so the hart has no debug mode and `EBREAK` always raises cause 3 | `C-13`, `FR-25`, `FR-53`, §6 |

| Named configuration | `NUM_HARTS` | `ENABLE_A` | `ICACHE_BYTES` | Product it serves |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `MCU_MIN` | 1 | 0 | 0 | smallest area, code executed in place from fast memory, no cache |
| `MCU_RTOS` | 1 | 0 | 2048 | the MVP: a preemptive RTOS on one hart (`D-01`, `D-04`), with `ENABLE_A` = 0 because the RTOS masks interrupts instead of using atomics (`Q-20`) |
| `MCU_SMP` | 4 | 1 | 2048 | the SMP milestone (`D-02`); `NUM_HARTS` = 2 is the same configuration with fewer harts |

Elaboration shall reject an illegal configuration rather than silently substitute a default:

| Check | Fails elaboration when | Source |
|---|---|---|
| hart count | `NUM_HARTS` is not 1, 2 or 4 | `C-06` |
| atomics with multiple harts | `NUM_HARTS > 1` and `ENABLE_A = 0` | `C-07` |
| cache without invalidation | `ICACHE_BYTES > 0` and `Zifencei` is not enabled | `C-08` |
| line size | `ICACHE_LINE` is not 32 | `C-09` |
| reset synchroniser depth | `RESET_SYNC_STAGES` is less than 2, which would not be a synchroniser | `FR-41` |
| reset vector alignment | `RESET_VECTOR` is not aligned to the instruction alignment of the build | `FR-41` |

None of these checks exists in the RTL today, and of the table's parameters four are declared: `RESET_VECTOR`,
which the fetch stage consumes (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:43`, `rtl/instruction_fetch.sv:110`, observed by `T-50`,
`G-33`, closed 2026-11-18 for that half), and `ENABLE_A`/`ENABLE_M`/`ENABLE_C`, declared in v0.8.8
(`rtl/riscv_small.sv:44-46`) and read only by `misa` (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:171`, `FR-37`) — with all three at 0
the core executes no `A`, `M` or `C` encoding, and a build that sets one advertises an extension it does not
execute, which is `G-37`. There is no configuration package, no cache (`G-15`) and no multi-hart support
(`G-16`). The rest of the table is the target of the parameters, and
§7.1 says which phase each one lands in.

`RESET_VECTOR` is the one parameter of the table the RTL already consumes, and no behaviour other than the first
fetch depends on it: vol. II §2.1.4 requires a reset vector to exist and leaves its value to the implementation, and
`Q-33` (2026-10-28) fixed the value as a build input defaulting to `0x8000_0000` rather than as a CSR. What is
observed is that the reset consumes that parameter: `T-50` compares the first fetch address after reset with the
elaborated parameter, and the second build at another value of it, which `FR-41`'s acceptance also asks for, has
not been elaborated (designer, deferred 2026-11-18), so configurability is not demonstrated. Software that needs to
relocate its vector table after reset does so through `mtvec`, not through this parameter (`FR-29`, `FR-30`).

A question that used to sit outside the table, and no longer does, is the cache-less build: `ICACHE_BYTES` = 0
keeps the same line-based instruction interface, so removing the cache changes capacity and hit behaviour but
not the integrator's memory model (`Q-13`, `A-10`, `FR-27`).

The last two rows are the debug increment, and neither is fixed by a named configuration: `NUM_TRIGGERS`
sizes the trigger unit (`FR-57`) and `DEBUG_ENABLED` decides whether the debug block exists at all.
`DEBUG_ENABLED` = 0 is the lockdown build — the port group of §6 is not
instantiated, the hart has no debug mode, and `EBREAK` raises cause 3 whatever a debug build would do with
`dcsr.ebreakm` (`FR-25`). The debug gate is **not** a build switch: it is the one-bit `dbg_auth` strap the
platform drives into the hart (§6, `C-14`, `FR-59`), so the same binary can be locked down or left
debuggable by the SoC. A test run states both parameters beside `ICACHE_BYTES` and `ENABLE_A`, for the reason
`C-11` gives: a result means nothing if the build that produced it is unstated.

## 6. Interfaces (summary)

One row per external interface, with the contract this specification promises; the interface catalogue is in
`doc/uarch.md` section 4.1 and the full port table in section 4.4. The `Status` column separates the contract as built from the contract the product
intends, because a reader of a specification should not have to guess which one is being described.

| Interface | Direction | Summary (target) | Contract | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Instruction memory | master | line address, burst request and valid, beat count, beat data, beat valid, last-beat | a whole cache line is fetched per request, as one request carrying the line address, the beat count and a valid qualifier, and the burst ends on last-beat (`FR-27`); no transaction identifier and no separate read-data channel; a cache-less build uses the same line interface; an outstanding fetch is never lost and its address stays stable (`FR-28`) | target: line fill; as built it is one word per cycle with a per-word ready (`FR-20`, `G-15`) |
| Data memory | master | `data_addr[31:0]`, `data_wr[31:0]`, `data_wr_strb[3:0]`, `data_rd_wr_ctrl[1:0]`, `data_rd_en_ma`, `data_wr_en_ma`, `data_rd[31:0]`, `data_ready` | `data_addr` is a byte address and the memory takes its word address from `data_addr[31:2]`; the core places the store data in the addressed lane of `data_wr` and asserts `data_wr_strb[3:0]` for exactly the bytes it drives, so the memory never shifts, justifies or masks data (`FR-11`, `Q-28`); on a load the memory returns the aligned word and the core selects the byte or halfword lane and extends it (`FR-12`); an access that crosses a word boundary is issued by the core as two beats, each confined to one word — beat 1 carries the low bytes of the access and sits at its byte address, beat 2 carries the high bytes at `(base & ~32'h3) + 4` — and is therefore not atomic (`FR-42`, `Q-29`, `A-05`) — atomics never take that path, because `LR`/`SC`/`AMO` must be naturally aligned and otherwise trap (`FR-35`); in phase 2 the interface also carries the store-conditional success indication, which is not one of the sixteen signals counted below (`FR-35`); a load or a store completes on the grant at the clock edge and there is no write-response channel (`FR-21`, `FR-38`); the contract must survive a later D-cache or TCDM | today: all eight signals — the lane, the byte strobes and the decoded width are implemented and observed (`FR-11`, `FR-12`, `T-13`..`T-15`, `T-51`) — and a crossing access is issued as two beats, each strobed within one word (`FR-42`, `T-52`) with a store held in MA until it is granted (`FR-38`, `T-53`). What the port does **not** keep is the read half of the grant contract: a read whose grant is withheld for two or more cycles is lost (`FR-21`, `G-35`), because the flat port has no request/response form and the request is not re-presented — that form is `E8` |
| Hart identity | input | `hart_id[3:0]` — the hart identifier value | `mhartid` reads the input (`FR-32`); no other result depends on it | present since v0.8.8 (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:56`); one hart, the multi-hart set is absent (`G-16`) |
| Interrupt requests | input | `mtip`, `meip`, `msip`, one per machine interrupt source, each level-sensitive | each is taken as a trap with its own cause while the matching `mie` bit and `mstatus.MIE` are set, and stays pending otherwise (`FR-39`, `FR-40`, `FR-48`); priority between them is `FR-48`; the phase-2 software request arrives on `msip` (`FR-36`) | absent: there is no interrupt input at all (`G-14`) |
| Trap cause | input | `exception[3:0]` — the reason for a synchronous trap request | a synchronous request presents its cause code on this bus and the core writes that code to `mcause` as the trap is taken; the core captures the value at entry and does not re-read the bus afterwards (`FR-22`, `Q-35`). It is a request, so the cause is only meaningful in the cycle the request is asserted | built since v0.8.8: four bits, captured with the trapping instruction's `pc` (`rtl/control.sv:362-377`, `rtl/csr_file.sv:232-234`); an internal cause takes priority, and a request on the bus itself is not exercised by a test (`G-37`) |
| Trap vector | — | none — the vector is software state | `mtvec` supplies the entry address, direct or vectored (`FR-29`, `FR-30`) | no port by decision (`D-08`); since v0.8.8 the address is `mtvec.BASE` (`rtl/csr_file.sv:103`, `rtl/riscv_small.sv:201`) |
| Control | input | `clk`, `clk_en`, `rst_n` | one clock domain; `rst_n` is active-low and asserts asynchronously, de-assertion is synchronised inside the core through `RESET_SYNC_STAGES` (`C-01`, `FR-41`, `D-09`) | assertion as built; the synchroniser is absent (`G-21`, `C-02`, `G-12`) |
| Platform timer | input | `mtime[63:0]` — one 64-bit input | the platform's real-time counter; `time` (0xC01) and `timeh` (0xC81) are read-only views of it, while `cycle` and `instret` are the core's own counters, and the core issues no memory access to read it (`FR-49`, `Q-42`) | present since v0.8.8: `rtl/riscv_small.sv:55`, read as `time`/`timeh` by `rtl/csr_file.sv:151`,`:155` and observed by `T-03` |
| Debug run control | input/output | `dbg_dmactive`, `dbg_haltreq`, `dbg_resumereq`, `dbg_resethaltreq` in; `dbg_halted`, `dbg_running`, `dbg_havereset`, `dbg_resumeack` out — eight signals, one bit each | the hart side of the run control of Debug Spec v1.0 §3.1.5 (`FR-54`): a halt request is honoured at an instruction boundary and a further one is ignored while halted; a resume request leaves debug mode and `dbg_resumeack` follows `dbg_running`; `dbg_resethaltreq` halts before the first instruction retires (`Q-40`); `dbg_havereset` reports a reset until the Debug Module acknowledges it; `dbg_dmactive` low holds the debug state idle. There is no `ndmreset` input: the run control places it in the Debug Module, and the hart sees a platform reset only as `rst_n` | target: absent — the port list carries no debug signal (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:45-62`, `G-23`) |
| Debug authorization | input | `dbg_auth` — one bit, driven by the platform strap | the hart-side gate of `FR-59`: when the strap is low the hart ignores halt and reset-halt requests, never enters debug mode and reports `dbg_halted` = 0, while an `EBREAK` whose `dcsr.ebreakm` is set and a trigger armed with action 0 still raise cause 3; when it is high the debug behaviour of `FR-53`..`FR-58` holds (`C-14`, `D-11`). It is a gate beside the authentication the Debug Specification places in the DM, and it is **not** Debug Module state and **not** a synthesis parameter: the platform drives it from a strap, a lifecycle controller or a secure-boot decision, so one binary serves both a locked-down and a debuggable part | target: absent — the port list carries no `dbg_auth` input (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:45-62`, `G-23`) |
| Debug abstract command | input/output | `dbg_command[31:0]` in, `dbg_data0_wdata[31:0]` in, `dbg_data0_rdata[31:0]` out, `dbg_busy` out, `dbg_cmderr[2:0]` out — five signals | the command the Debug Module issues and the completion it reads back (`FR-55`): the hart executes it in debug mode, a memory access uses the data port of the row above rather than a port of its own, and a command it cannot complete leaves a non-zero `dbg_cmderr` with `dbg_busy` low instead of hanging; the `abstractcs`, `command` and `data0` registers of Debug Spec v1.0 §3.1.14 are Debug Module state and are not these signals | target: absent |
| Debug program buffer | input/output | `dbg_progbuf_inst[31:0]` in, `dbg_progbuf_wr_en` in, `dbg_progbuf_ready` out, `dbg_progbuf_done` out — four signals | the instruction words the Debug Module places in the program buffer and the completion it observes (`FR-56`): the hart runs them in order in debug mode and reports an exception as a command error rather than taking it, and its own program counter is not `dpc`. How many words the buffer holds and the cycle-level handshake are microarchitecture, not this row | target: absent |

**The data-lane contract, stated once.** `data_addr` is a byte address, and `data_addr[1:0]` selects the lane
inside the word; the memory takes the word address from `data_addr[31:2]`. The **core**, not the memory, does
the lane work. On a store the core places the value in the addressed lane of `data_wr` and asserts
`data_wr_strb[3:0]` for exactly the bytes it drives, so the memory applies the strobes as given and never
shifts, justifies or masks a store. On a load the memory returns the aligned word and the core selects the
byte or halfword out of it and extends it (`FR-11`, `FR-12`). An access that crosses a word boundary is not
sent to the memory as one access: the core issues two beats, each confined to one word — beat 1 carries the low bytes of the access, at its byte address, and beat 2 the high bytes, at `(base & ~32'h3) + 4` — and reassembles the value, which is how `A-05`'s
EEI guarantee is discharged (`FR-42`, `Q-29`). Byte strobes are what the maintained open
cores converge on — PicoRV32's `mem_wstrb[3:0]`, Ibex's `data_be_o[3:0]` with a word-aligned address, and
CV32E40P's OBI byte enables — so this port follows them rather than masking from the address and the width
signal (`Q-28`).

The DUT exposes **eighteen** external signals today — four to the instruction memory, eight to the data memory,
four control (`clk`, `clk_en`, `rst_n`, `exception[3:0]`), the 64-bit `mtime` input and the 4-bit `hart_id`
input — with `data_wr_strb[3:0]` already in the port list (`FR-11`), so eighteen is the as-built count and
not a target: it was sixteen until v0.8.8 added the timer and the hart identifier, and the synchronous request
grew from one bit to four without adding a group. That eighteen counts the memory, control, timer and identity
signals only, and a reader should not fold anything else into it: it
does **not** include the three interrupt requests (`mtip`, `meip`, `msip`), the store-conditional success
indication the phase-2 data interface needs (`FR-35`), or the eighteen debug signals
of the four debug rows. The target port count of the whole core is therefore larger than eighteen, and the trap,
timer and debug signals are counted separately, in their own rows above: eight run-control, one authorization,
five abstract-command and four program-buffer signals make **eighteen** in the debug group.

The debug group is the hart side of a Debug Module and nothing else (`C-13`, `D-10`). It is **absent** when
`DEBUG_ENABLED` = 0: the eighteen signals are not instantiated, the hart has no debug mode, and an `EBREAK`
always raises cause 3 (`FR-25`, §5.2). It carries no `ndmreset`, because the run control keeps that in the
Debug Module and the hart observes a platform reset as `rst_n` (`FR-54`); it carries no DMI, because the DMI is
the DTM-to-DM link of appendix A, Table 1 and is not a core port (`Q-37`).

Two of those rows — the interrupt requests and the trap cause — were a single open question until 2026-10-28. `Q-35` decided that a request carries its
reason on a bus beside it, instead of the core inferring a cause from context, and that the three maskable
machine interrupts arrive on their own level-sensitive inputs rather than being multiplexed onto one request
line. The reason is visible in `FR-48`: an interrupt is masked by a `mie` bit and unmasked by it, per source,
so the sources must be distinguishable at the boundary and not only after the trap is taken.

The handshake of the instruction port is fixed by `FR-27` and `FR-28` and was confirmed on 2026-10-28: the
simple form — one request per line, a beat count, a valid, and a last-beat flag on the final beat — with no
identifiers and no separate read-data channel (`Q-13`, `A-10`). The memory delivers exactly the requested beat
count, and a short burst is a protocol error, not a shorter line (`Q-21`). The beat-level timing (how many
cycles each beat may take, and when `valid` is first sampled) is not yet specified; it is a microarchitecture
question for `doc/uarch.md`, not a requirement here.

## 7. Compliance and ISA scope

Two columns, because the v0.1 rows were written when only one answer existed. "As built" is what the RTL
does; "target" is what §7.1 commits to and by when.

| Item | As built | Target | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| RV32I arithmetic, logic, shift, upper-immediate, jump, branch, load, store | complete for the subset of §4.1 — every encoding executes, its legality is decided in ID, and the reserved configurations of the base instructions trap | complete in phase 1 | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:283-365` (legality), `:370-482` (decode), `G-38` for the reserved `SYSTEM` fields |
| RV32I `FENCE` | no — executes as a no-operation | ordering in phase 1 (`FR-26`) | `G-06`, `A-13` |
| RV32I `ECALL` / `EBREAK` | built in v0.8.8 — cause 11 and 3, `mepc` at the trapping instruction, neither retires | trap in phase 1 (`FR-25`), decided 2026-10-28 (`Q-05`) | `T-03`; `G-38` for the reserved fields of the encodings |
| RV32I illegal-instruction exception | built in v0.8.8 for the families of §4.1 — unknown opcode, reserved `funct3`/`funct7`, absent or read-only CSR address — while a reserved configuration of `SYSTEM` (`ECALL`/`EBREAK` with a non-zero `rd`/`rs1`) still retires | trap in phase 1 (`FR-24`), decided 2026-10-28 (`Q-05`); the encodings that must trap are listed in §4.1 | `T-03`, `G-38` for the reserved `SYSTEM` fields |
| RV32I misaligned-access exception | no exception — an address is passed to the memory unchecked and a crossing access is issued as two beats, each confined to one word, by the core | **EEI guarantee** for non-atomic loads and stores only: no exception is raised and the core issues the crossing access as two beats, each confined to one word — beat 1 carries the low bytes of the access, beat 2 the high bytes (`FR-42`, volume I §1.1.6, `A-05`); a misaligned `LR`/`SC`/`AMO` **does** raise address-misaligned or an access fault (`FR-35`, volume I A) | `A-05`, `FR-35`; the split is built and observed by `T-52` since v0.8.7 (`FR-42`) |
| `M` (multiply and divide) | no — the encodings now trap with cause 2 instead of aliasing onto the base ALU | fixed on in phase 1 (`FR-43`, `D-07`) | `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:316-329`, `G-06` |
| `C` (compressed) | no — the encodings trap with cause 2 (opcode `11` is taken as a 32-bit word, so a compressed parcel is not formed) | fixed on in phase 1 (`FR-44`, `D-07`), which also sets `IALIGN=16` | absent from the decoder, `G-06` |
| `Zicsr` and machine-mode CSRs | built in v0.8.8 — the six `Zicsr` forms and the file at `rtl/csr_file.sv` | phase 1 (`FR-23`, `FR-29`, `FR-32`, `FR-37`, `FR-47`, and the file itself in `FR-50`..`FR-52`) | `T-03` observes the six forms and the map; `G-37` for the arms it does not |
| `Zifencei` | no | phase 1 (`FR-31`) | `G-06`, `G-15` |
| `Zicntr` (`cycle`, `time`, `instret`) | built in v0.8.8 — `cycle`/`cycleh` are views of `mcycle`, `instret`/`instreth` of `minstret`, `time`/`timeh` of the `mtime` input | phase 1 (`FR-49`, reached through the map of `FR-50`, with `time`/`timeh` reading the `mtime` input of §6) | `T-03`; the `instret`-across-traps arm is `G-37` |
| Machine trap entry | built in v0.8.8 — `mtvec.BASE` entry, `mepc`/`mcause` and the trap discard; `MRET` is still absent | phase 1, `mtvec` direct and vectored, the entry stack, `MRET` and a scratch register for the handler (`FR-29`, `FR-30`, `FR-45`, `FR-48`, `FR-51`) | `G-14` (the interrupt set) and `FR-30`/`FR-45`; the entry half is `T-03` |
| Machine timer and external interrupts | no | phase 1 (`FR-39`, `FR-40`, `FR-48`, on the `mtip`/`meip` inputs of §6) | `G-14` |
| `A` (Zaamo + Zalrsc) | no | phase 2 (`FR-35`, `D-06`) | `G-16` |
| Machine software interrupt (IPI) | no | phase 2 (`FR-36`, on the `msip` input of §6) | `G-14`, `G-16` |
| `mhartid` and more than one hart | no | phase 2 (`FR-32`, `FR-33`) | `G-16` |
| Debug (`Sdext`, the hart side of an external Debug Module) | no — no debug port, no debug mode, no debug CSR, and the system opcode group is a no-operation | the debug phase (`FR-53`..`FR-56`, `FR-58`, `FR-59`), all `deferred`; the Debug Module, the JTAG DTM and the DMI stay outside the core (`C-13`, `D-10`) | no debug signal in `rtl/riscv_small.sv:45-62`; `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:348-353` |
| `Sdtrig` (triggers) | no — no trigger unit and no `tselect`..`tinfo` | the debug phase (`FR-57`), `deferred`, with `NUM_TRIGGERS` of 0, 2 or 4 | `G-13` for the address, `FR-57` for the unit |
| Trace (`N-Trace`, `E-Trace`) | no | **not targeted in this revision**: it needs a trace encoder and an output port this core does not have, and no requirement asks for one | absent by scope; §1 non-goals |
| `F`, `D`, `V`, supervisor and user mode, MMU | no | not targeted (`A-19`), and the condition under which that changes is recorded there: a requirement for task isolation would need `S`/`U` mode plus physical-memory protection, not an MMU (also `C-15`) | absent; §1 non-goals, `A-19` |

### 7.1 Target ISA and phases

Phase 1 is the MVP: one hart running a preemptive RTOS (`D-01`). Phase 1 is therefore a *complete*
machine-mode implementation and not a partial one — the CSR file, the trap entry and return, the maskable
timer and external interrupts and `misa` all land together (§1, `FR-50`..`FR-52`) — because a kernel needs the
whole set before it can switch a task. Phase 2 is the SMP milestone (`D-02`). A third label, `debug`, marks the
debug increment of `FR-53`..`FR-59`: it is not phase 1 and it does not move with phase 2, and the one row of that
increment that is *not* debug is the non-maskable interrupt, which `Q-41` puts in phase 2. A
row's phase is a commitment to a milestone, not a promise that it exists: the last column states today's
status with the v0.1 evidence, and a row cannot move to `implemented` in this document until the RTL shows it.
The normative reference for every row is the latest **ratified** specification (`D-08`) — for this revision,
RISC-V ISA v20260120 volume I and volume II; RVM23 and CLIC are drafts and are advisory only. Nothing is
advertised in `misa` before it is implemented (`FR-37`). Rows whose content the standard fixes cite the
volume and section; rows the standard leaves open say so and record a project decision (`D-09` is the first).

| Extension | Why | Phase | Status today |
|---|---|---|---|
| `I` (RV32I base) | the instruction set the toolchain targets | 1 | complete but for one encoding family: every instruction of §4.1 executes and is now observed by its own image (`FR-01`..`FR-06` and `FR-08` `verified`; `FR-07` `partial` on the instruction-address-misaligned clause, `G-13`), and `Q-06`'s two deviations are fixed — `G-08` (the sub-word lane and strobe) closed on 2026-11-05 by WP-6 under `T-13`/`T-14`/`T-15`, `G-07` (`JALR` bit 0) the same day by WP-7 under `T-17`/`T-37`, and `G-34` (the halfword lane ignored `addr[0]`) on 2026-11-19 by WP-13 under `T-51`. Since v0.8.7 all **42** bundled `rv32ui` images pass (`NFR-05`), including the misaligned-access image `rv32ui-p-ma_data` (`FR-42`, `T-52`). What keeps the extension `partial` is that an unimplemented encoding still executed as a no-operation; since v0.8.8 the legality table traps instead (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:283-365`), so the extension's only open item is the reserved `SYSTEM` configuration of `G-38` |
| `M` | fixed on (`D-07`); multiply and divide without a helper library | 1 | absent: the encodings alias onto the base ALU (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:341-347`, `G-06`); required by `FR-43` |
| `C` | fixed on (`D-07`); code size for a small MCU; `IALIGN=16` follows from it | 1 | absent: not decoded (`G-06`); required by `FR-44` |
| `Zicsr` | required for `mtvec`, the trap CSRs, `mhartid` and `misa`, and therefore for the whole file (`FR-50`) | 1 | built in v0.8.8: the six forms decode (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:341-356`) and `T-03` observes them; required by `FR-47` and `FR-50`, both `verified` |
| `Zifencei` | `fence.i` makes the cache coherent with stores (`D-04`); it is the only standard mechanism by which a store becomes visible to a later fetch on the same hart, so the obligation applies with or without the cache | 1 | absent: `fence.i` is a no-operation (`G-06`, `G-15`); required by `FR-31`, and `Q-27` closed on 2026-10-28 with `Zifencei` kept in this phase |
| `Zicntr` | a tick source that does not consume a timer peripheral | 1 | built in v0.8.8 (`rtl/csr_file.sv:150-155`); required by `FR-49`, at the six addresses the map of `FR-50` reserves for it |
| `misa` | tells a toolchain or a boot loader which extensions the build actually executes; read-only, so `IALIGN` cannot change under a running pipeline (`Q-30`) | 1 | built in v0.8.8 from the build's parameters (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:171`, `rtl/riscv_definitions_pkg.sv:412-421`); `FR-37` fixes the bit set, `C-10` fixes `M` and `C` on, and no bit is advertised before its requirement lands — which is why a build that sets `ENABLE_M`/`ENABLE_C` early is `G-37` |
| Machine trap architecture: `mtvec`, `mepc`, `mcause`, `mstatus`, `mie`, `mip`, `mscratch`, `mtval`, `mret` | trap entry and return, the precondition for any RTOS; `MRET` is `FR-45`, the entry state and maskable interrupts are `FR-48`, direct and vectored entry are `FR-29`/`FR-30`, and the file that exposes them is `FR-50` | 1 | entry built in v0.8.8: `mtvec.BASE`, `mepc`/`mcause`, `Zicsr` over the map, `T-03`; **partial on the return path** — `MRET` is not decoded (`FR-45`) — and on the interrupt state (`G-14`), and vectored mode is `FR-30` |
| `mscratch` | the handler's frame pointer when no general-purpose register can be spared, which is what makes a context switch possible at all (`FR-51`) | 1 | built in v0.8.8 (`rtl/csr_file.sv:141`, `:225`); `FR-51` fixes read/write behaviour, no side effects and a zero reset value as a project choice |
| `mtval` | the trap value register; read-only zero by decision (`Q-32`), so a handler that needs the faulting encoding reads it from `mepc` (`FR-52`) | 1 | built in v0.8.8 (`rtl/csr_file.sv:144`, zero write mask in `rtl/riscv_definitions_pkg.sv:397-398`); no exception is required to report an address or an encoding through it |
| Reset vector | the address of the first fetch after reset, a build input and not a CSR (`FR-41`, `Q-33`, §5.2) | 1 | built: the `RESET_VECTOR` parameter (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:43`, default `0x8000_0000`) is the value the reset loads into the program counter (`rtl/instruction_fetch.sv:110`), `T-50` observes the first fetch in a run at the default value, and the `reset-vector-zero` mutant proves the assignment is load-bearing; a second build at another value of the parameter is deferred (2026-11-18), so configurability is not demonstrated (`G-33`, closed for the half `T-50` observes) |
| Machine timer interrupt (`MTIP`) | the preemption tick of `D-01` | 1 | absent (`G-14`); `FR-39` takes it from the `mtip` input and `FR-48` fixes the entry state and priority |
| Machine external interrupt (`MEIP`) | device service without polling | 1 | absent (`G-14`); `FR-40` takes it from the `meip` input and `FR-48` fixes the entry state and priority |
| `A`: Zaamo + Zalrsc | atomics for harts that can race (`D-06`); the MVP RTOS masks interrupts in its critical sections instead and needs none (`Q-20`, `C-07`) | 2 | absent (`G-16`) |
| Machine software interrupt (`MSIP`, IPI) | cross-hart wake-up and scheduling in SMP | 2 | absent (`G-14`, `G-16`); the request input `msip` is already in the interface summary (§6) |
| `mhartid` with `NUM_HARTS > 1` | hart identification (`D-03`) | 2 | absent (`G-16`); read-only at 0xF14 in the map of `FR-50` |
| Debug mode and the core debug registers (`dcsr`, `dpc`, `dscratch0`, `dscratch1`) | halting a hart, inspecting and changing its state, and resuming it where it stopped — the first thing an RTOS bring-up needs and the last thing a deployed part should allow (`FR-53`) | debug | absent: no debug port, no `dcsr`/`dpc`, and the system opcode group is a no-operation (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:348-353`); `FR-53` is `deferred` |
| Debug transport: external Debug Module behind a JTAG DTM | one DM serving several harts and no transport logic in the core (`C-13`, `D-10`) | debug | absent by construction: the core exposes the hart side only and the DMI stays between the DTM and the DM (`Q-36`, `Q-37`) |
| Abstract commands (register, CSR and memory access) | reading and writing a halted hart without executing code (`FR-55`) | debug | absent (`G-13`); `FR-55` is `deferred` |
| Program buffer | running small sequences in debug mode, which is where a debugger has to go for anything an abstract command cannot do (`FR-56`) | debug | absent; `FR-56` is `deferred` |
| `Sdtrig` triggers (`mcontrol6`) | breakpoints and watchpoints on an instruction address, a load or store address, or store data (`FR-57`, `NUM_TRIGGERS`) | debug | absent: `tselect`..`tinfo` are not decoded (`G-13`); `FR-57` is `deferred` |
| Single step | stepping one instruction at a time, which is also how a debugger steps over a breakpoint (`FR-58`) | debug | absent; `FR-58` is `deferred` |
| `dbg_auth` strap | a deployed part that cannot be halted by an attached probe, gated at the hart beside the DM's own authentication (`C-14`, `FR-59`) | debug | absent: the hart has no `dbg_auth` input yet (`G-23`); `FR-59` is `deferred` |
| Non-maskable interrupt (`Smrnmi`) | a watchdog that must preempt a masked machine interrupt; not required in this revision (`Q-41`) | 2 | absent and not specified here; the machine interrupt set stays the three maskable causes of `FR-48` |
| Trace (`N-Trace`, `E-Trace`) | instruction-trace reconstruction for profiling and post-mortem analysis; it needs a trace encoder and a trace port this core does not have | — | **not targeted in this revision**; §1 records it as a non-goal |
| `Zihpm`, `S`/`U` mode, Sv32 MMU, `F`, `D`, `V` | outside the product scope: the core is machine-mode only, the ABI is `ilp32`, and task isolation is not a requirement (`A-19`, `Q-34`, `C-15`); no requirement asks for a programmable hardware counter, so `Zihpm` is out too | — | not targeted (`A-19`), and the condition is recorded there: a requirement for task isolation would bring back `S`/`U` mode plus physical-memory protection, not an MMU. `F`, `D` and `V` read zero in `misa` (`FR-37`) |

- Forward index: the `Traces to` column of §4 and §5 (requirement to block and to test).
- Reverse index: [verification.md](verification.md).

  `T-11`; `FR-08` to `T-12` and the branch images `T-10`/`T-19`..`T-22`/`T-38`; `FR-11` to `T-13`, `T-14`,
  `T-15` and `T-51`; `FR-17` to `T-16`; `FR-06` to `T-18`; `FR-01` to `T-10`, `T-29`..`T-36` and `T-43`; `FR-03` to
  `T-26`, `T-27` and `T-40`; `FR-04` to `T-41`; `FR-05` to `T-42`; `FR-38` to `T-53`; `FR-42` to `T-52`;
  `FR-23`, `FR-25`, `FR-29`, `FR-32`, `FR-37`, `FR-47`, `FR-49`, `FR-51` and `FR-52` to `T-03`.
  **Twenty-five rows** — v0.8.7 added `FR-38` and `FR-42`, and gave `FR-11` and `FR-12` a second observation each
  (`T-51`, the odd halfword offset their earlier evidence did not present); v0.8.8 added nine CSR/trap rows to
  `T-03` (`riscv_small_csr_test`, wp 14, green in a 50/50 regression at RTL `sha256:2e91301d…` / TB
  `sha256:0091e260…`) and took `FR-32` there too, from phase 2 — while `FR-50`, which the same test also
  exercises, is `partial` on `G-39` and is not in this list. `T-47`..`T-49`, the Spike
  commit-trace comparison, corroborate `FR-09`, `FR-10`, `FR-11` and `FR-12` against an external model but are
  **not** what makes them `verified`: each of the four names its own image or directed test above.
- Orphans (`implemented` or `partial`, no observing test): `FR-13` to `FR-15`, `FR-19` to `FR-22` and
  `FR-41` — six of them to `G-04` (`FR-21` is one of the six and is `partial` on `G-35` as well), `FR-22` to
  `G-37` (its RTL is built and its own arm — the `exception[3:0]` request — is what no test drives) and `FR-41`
  to `G-21` (its reset-vector arm is observed by
  `T-50` in a run at the default value, which does not take the row out of this list: the release half stays with
  `G-21` and the two-build arm of its acceptance is unobserved, `G-33` being closed only for the half `T-50`
  observes). The single root cause for the
  `G-04` rows is that the reference model tracks the load/store subset (`LW`, `LB`, `LH`, `LBU`, `LHU`, `SW`)
  and the scoreboard compares store predictions only, which the `rv32ui` images do not depend on because each
  one self-checks. The external comparison WP-9 added narrows that root cause on 2026-11-18 without releasing a
  row: `T-47`..`T-49` match the core against Spike on `pc`, instruction, `rd` and written value for three images,
  and they carry no store address, no store data, no commit that writes `x0` and no bus protocol. `FR-07` is `partial` and **not** an orphan: `T-17`
  and `T-37` observe its mask clause, while its instruction-address-misaligned clause has no implementation
  (`G-13`), which is the gap that keeps it out of the `verified` list above.
- Unimplemented as-built requirements: `FR-26` (`G-06`);
  and `NFR-01`..`NFR-04` (`G-05`). `NFR-07` left this list on 2026-11-18: `make lint_fpga` measured
  **0 inferred latches and 0 combinational loops** on the synthesised netlist, so it is `verified` and it is the

  `FR-35`, `FR-36` (`G-16`) and `FR-43`, `FR-44`, `FR-46` (`G-06`). `FR-38` (`G-18`) and

  word-crossing split and `T-53`/`T-52` observed them; `FR-23`, `FR-25`, `FR-29`, `FR-32`, `FR-37`, `FR-47`,

- `T-02` is registered but adds no new stimulus, so nothing cites it (`G-11`).
- Every target row from `FR-27` to `FR-59` names its phase in the acceptance text, so a reader can tell a
  phase-1 gap from a phase-2 or a debug-phase commitment without leaving this document; the as-built
  `deferred` rows (`FR-23` to `FR-26`) inherit their phase from §7.1.
- Registered but not implemented: `T-03` (CSR and trap), `T-04` (timer and interrupt) and `T-05` (debug) are
  the tests `FR-22`, `FR-37`, `FR-48`..`FR-52` and the debug rows name, and [verification.md](verification.md)
  registers all three with status `deferred`. No test file exists under `tb/tests/` for any of them. Until the
  three are written, those rows stay `deferred` however complete the RTL becomes.

## 9. Assumptions, gaps, and open questions

### Assumptions

`A-01` to `A-07` were inferred in v0.1 from the RTL or the testbench. `A-08` to `A-16` were the proposed
defaults of the 2026-10-28 rounds, each naming what changes if it is wrong; `A-17` and `A-18` were answered by
the normative text, and `A-19` records the scope decision the round-7 answers depend on. Status is `open` while
the point is unconfirmed, `confirmed` once the designer has settled it, and `superseded` when a later answer
replaced it — a superseded row is kept, never renumbered, and must not be cited.

No row is `open`. Every row is `confirmed` — or `superseded` in the case of `A-02` (retired into `C-12`,
because `x0` is an ISA rule) and `A-03` (retired into `FR-27`/`FR-28`). No question from `Q-30` to `Q-42` is
open either: the round-7 answers of 2026-10-28 closed `Q-30`..`Q-35` into `FR-37`, `FR-41`, `FR-46`, `FR-52`,
`A-19` and the port rows of §6 (§0.8), and the debug set `Q-36`..`Q-42` into `C-13`, `C-14`, `D-10`, `D-11`,
`FR-49` and `FR-53`..`FR-59`. A confirmed row is a settled obligation, not a rubber stamp on the v0.1 wording:
rounds 4 and 5 rewrote several of them against the standard.

| ID | Statement | Owner | Method | Due | If false | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A-01 | The data memory asserts the grant in the cycle its read data is valid, and the core may stall indefinitely while the grant is withheld. | integrator | protocol review against `rtl/control.sv:295-300` | 2026-12-31 | `FR-21` is wrong and every load gains or loses a wait state (`G-09`) | confirmed 2026-10-28 for loads (answer 6); the store half moved to `FR-38`. **The second clause does not hold as built and the "if false" branch is the one that happened:** a read whose grant is withheld for two or more cycles is lost, because the freeze acts one cycle after the read was presented (`G-35`, `FR-21` `partial`, measured 2026-11-19 by WP-13) — the fix is the request/response form of the data port (`E8`), not a wider stall condition |
| A-02 | **Retired 2026-10-28, do not cite.** `x0` is not an assumption: volume I §1.1.1 makes it a rule, now recorded as constraint `C-12` (`Q-22`). | designer | retired by the normative citation; the rule lives in `C-12` | — | nothing is lost: `FR-14` keeps its acceptance and `C-12` adds the `rd = x0` side-effect obligation | superseded (retired into `C-12`) |
| A-03 | **Superseded 2026-10-28, do not cite.** The instruction memory presents data and ready in the same cycle as the address, so a fetch has zero latency. | integrator | replaced by the line-fill contract | 2026-12-31 | nothing is lost by retiring it: the replacement is `FR-27` (a line per fill) and `FR-28` (a real stall), and `G-09` became a prerequisite rather than a doubt | superseded |
| A-04 | After reset, the state the hardware defines is only what privileged volume II §2.1.4 lists: machine mode, `MIE` = 0, `MPRV` = 0, `MBE` = 0, `misa` maximal, no valid load reservation, `pc` at the implementation-defined reset vector, `mcause` holding the reset cause, and PMP entries with `A` = 0 and `L` = 0. Everything else is UNSPECIFIED — the same section says "All other hart state is UNSPECIFIED" — so no general-purpose register has a defined value at reset and no register-file reset value is a contract. Software shall initialise every register it reads before reading it; zeroing the file is permitted but nothing may rely on it. | designer | normative citation, RISC-V ISA v20260120 vol. II §2.1.4; answered 2026-10-28 (`Q-23`) | 2026-12-31 | a test that reads an uninitialised register is a testbench bug, not a core defect; a design that does rely on a zeroed file has an unstated requirement | confirmed 2026-10-28 |
| A-05 | This execution environment (EEI) **guarantees** that misaligned non-atomic loads and stores are fully supported, and raises no address-misaligned exception for them. The guarantee does not extend to atomics: `LR`, `SC` and `AMO` must be naturally aligned and otherwise take an address-misaligned or access-fault exception (`FR-35`, vol. I A). Volume I §1.1.6 requires that naturally aligned accesses never raise one and makes misaligned behaviour EEI-dependent, adding that an EEI "may guarantee that misaligned loads and stores are fully supported"; this row is that guarantee. How it is discharged: an access that crosses a word boundary is issued by the core as two accesses, each confined to one word — beat 1 carries the low bytes of the access at its byte address with its strobes within that word, beat 2 carries the high bytes at `(base & ~32'h3) + 4` with the remaining strobes — with the value reassembled on a load and the strobes recomputed on a store (`FR-42`, `Q-29`); such an access is **not atomic**, which the ISA states for misaligned accesses. Consequence for the integrator: the strobes of each beat lie within one word, so the data memory sees a byte address and a byte mask that never spans two words, while the first beat's address is the access's byte address and not a rounded one; lane selection stays in the core (§6, `FR-11`, `FR-12`, `FR-42`). | integrator | normative citation, RISC-V ISA v20260120 vol. I §1.1.6; answered 2026-10-28 (`Q-24`), discharged by `Q-29` | 2026-12-31 | withdrawing the guarantee would require a misaligned-access trap and a cause, `FR-13` and `FR-42` would change, and the memory model would need a misaligned port | confirmed 2026-10-28 |
| A-06 | The reset is **hybrid**, and that is a project decision because the ISA says nothing about reset style: `rst_n` low asserts asynchronously, de-assertion is synchronised inside the core through `RESET_SYNC_STAGES` flops (default 2), and a global macro selects the style so an FPGA build can use a synchronous reset. The reason is the project's own RTL guidance: an asynchronous reset arriving from an external pin needs a synchroniser for de-assertion, and the two styles are already partitioned by a compile-time macro (`.opencode/skills/systemverilog-rtl-verification/references/reset-and-fsm.md`, "Global reset selection" and "Reset synchronizer"). Requirement `FR-41`; the as-built gap is `G-21`. | designer | project decision `D-09`, answered 2026-10-28 (`Q-25`, revised from the v0.1 assumption) | 2026-12-31 | the as-built async-assert/async-release reset stands, and a de-assertion that lands close to a clock edge can corrupt the first cycle of every stage | confirmed 2026-10-28 (revised) |
| A-07 | A single hart has one outstanding instruction fetch and one outstanding data access, and no ordering between the two ports is promised. This is a microarchitecture assumption and not an ISA rule: the standard constrains *ordering* (RVWMO, volume I chapter 17; `FENCE`, §1.1.7; `FENCE.I`, Zifencei chapter 4) and never the number of transactions in flight. What it is worth: a blocking, single-outstanding port trivially satisfies the single-hart ordering rules, and the normative obligation that remains is `FENCE` for data (`FR-26`) and `FENCE.I` for fetch versus data (`FR-31`). | designer | answered 2026-10-28 (`Q-26`); RISC-V ISA v20260120 vol. I ch. 17 and §1.1.7, Zifencei ch. 4 | 2026-12-31 | a pipelined memory interface would need its own ordering argument, and `A-15`'s no-coherence reading would have to be revisited | confirmed 2026-10-28 |
| A-08 | No clock-frequency target and no target part are recorded; the default is "unmeasured", not a frequency. | A-15 | There is no data cache in any phase, so shared memory needs no coherence protocol, and inter-hart ordering is limited to what fences and atomics provide. | designer | answered 2026-10-28 (`Q-17`), confirming `D-04` at SoC level | 2026-12-31 | a coherence or TCDM protocol becomes a requirement and `FR-35` changes | confirmed |
