| A-17 | The causes the core must report are the standard's, not a subset chosen by this project: 11 for `ECALL` from M-mode and 3 for `EBREAK` (`FR-25`), 2 for an illegal instruction (`FR-24`), 0 for an instruction-address-misaligned fetch or target (`FR-06`..`FR-08`), 4 or 6 for an atomic that is misaligned (`FR-35`), and the interrupt causes 7, 11 and 3 for MSI, MTI and MEI with bit 31 set (`FR-36`, `FR-39`, `FR-40`, `FR-48`). That is the whole set the requirements name, and vol. II Table 7 is the authority for each. | designer | confirmed by the normative text on 2026-10-28 (`Q-04` closed) | — | a row would name a cause outside this list, which the standard does not assign to that event | confirmed |
| A-18 | The fetch side of ordering is discharged by `Zifencei`, which is in the phase-1 ISA surface and not deferred with the cache: `FENCE.I` is the only standard mechanism by which a store becomes visible to a later fetch on the same hart, so `FR-31` holds whatever the cache configuration, including `ICACHE_BYTES` = 0. No coherence protocol beyond it is required by these rows. | designer | confirmed by the normative text on 2026-10-28 (`Q-27` closed); Zifencei chapter, `FR-31` | — | the phase-2 ISA surface loses `Zifencei`, and a coherence requirement the specification does not have would be needed for the cache | confirmed |
| A-19 | This core targets a machine-mode-only MCU: `S`/`U`-mode, an MMU, `F`/`D` and `V` are not implemented, and task isolation is not a requirement. If isolation becomes a requirement, the minimum change is `S`/`U`-mode plus physical-memory protection, not an MMU. Two consequences follow from the standard rather than from preference: bits 18 (`S`) and 20 (`U`) read zero in `misa` because those modes are not implemented, which is exactly what `FR-37` requires; and `F` (bit 5), `D` (bit 3) and `V` (bit 21) read zero in `misa` while the ABI is `ilp32`, so a toolchain must not assume `ilp32d`. The constraint on the CSR and trap surface is `C-15`. | designer | answered 2026-10-28 (`Q-34`), with the scope decision recorded in the same round | 2026-12-31 | a task-isolation requirement reopens `S`/`U`-mode and physical-memory protection, retires `C-15`, and adds delegation registers, the lower-mode CSRs and the `mstatus` fields `FR-48` now reports as read-only zero; a profile claim (RVA20 or RVA22) would invalidate the row, because a profile obliges `Zicclsm` and, for RVA22, `S`/`U` | confirmed |

`A-09`'s outside-the-bar set, in the one form a machine can read: the gate compares the phase-1 rows
against this list instead of against every `FR`, and it reads the document rather than keeping a copy, so
this line — not the gate — is the source of truth. A table cell cannot hold a line of its own, which is why
it sits here, directly under the row it belongs to.

- Gate input — outside the bar: `FR-32 FR-33 FR-34 FR-35 FR-36 FR-53 FR-54 FR-55 FR-56 FR-57 FR-58 FR-59`

### Gaps

The canonical gap table, with owners and targets, is in `doc/verification.md`. Rows introduced or reframed in
**Round 1**: `G-01` and `G-02` now name `mtvec` and `mcause` as their targets; `G-09` changed from "a doubt
about the fetch handshake" to "the prerequisite for the instruction cache", because with a 2 kB cache a miss
is the normal case and the core must be able to hold its program counter. New rows opened in Round 1:
`G-13` (no CSR file), `G-14` (no interrupt subsystem), `G-15` (no cache and no line fetch), `G-16` (no
multi-hart support), `G-17` (no decision log), `G-18` (stores ignore the data grant).

Round 2 (2026-10-28) reframed two more. `G-07` (`JALR` leaves bit 0 of the target set) and `G-08` (a sub-word
access always uses the lowest lane) were made **release blockers**: `Q-06` fixed both deviations, so phase 1
could not sign off while either was open. Both are now closed — `G-08` on 2026-11-05 because `FR-12` took the
load half in v0.7.4 under `T-06` and `FR-11` the store half under `T-13`..`T-15`, and `G-07` the same day
because the target mask exists and `T-17`/`T-37` observe it — so `Q-06` has no deviation left to block on.
`G-06` also narrowed — from "unimplemented encodings are unspecified" to "the §4.1 conformance list is not
implemented", which is what `Q-05` fixed.

Rounds 3 and 4 (2026-10-28) closed the gap list. `Q-11` made `G-03`, `G-04`, `G-10` and `G-11` **release
blockers** alongside `G-07` and `G-08`; on 2026-10-30 WP-4 closed `G-10` and added `G-26`/`G-27`, on
2026-11-05 WP-6 closed `G-08` and WP-7 closed `G-07`, and on 2026-11-06 WP-7's coverage measurement closed `G-03`, so `G-04` and `G-11` are the two that remain — WP-9 narrowed `G-04` on 2026-11-18 without closing it, since the Spike comparison (`T-47`..`T-49`) sees a commit's `pc`, encoding, `rd` and value but not a store's address or data; `Q-12` fixed an owner and a 2026-12-31 target for every other row, so
the `Owner` and `Target` columns of `doc/verification.md` are commitments and not proposals — stated once here
and not repeated per row. `Q-15` narrowed `G-06` again, to the data side only: the `FENCE` obligation is now a
phase-1 requirement (`FR-26`) and the fetch side belongs to `FR-31`, which holds in every cache configuration because `Zifencei` is in the phase-1 surface (`Q-27` closed, `A-18`). `Q-25` introduced `G-21`, the
first gap this document has opened against a requirement it wrote itself, and round 5 added `G-22` the same
way: the data port of §6 promised strobes and a split that `rtl/` did not have. WP-6 built the strobes and the
lane on 2026-11-05, so what `G-22` recorded until it closed on 2026-11-19 was the split alone (`FR-42`).

The debug increment adds two gap rows. `G-23` records that the hart has no debug subsystem of any kind — no
debug signal in the port list, no debug mode, no `dcsr`/`dpc`/`dscratch` and no abstract-command or
program-buffer path — and `G-24` records that no trigger unit exists: `tselect`..`tinfo` are not decoded and no
address or data comparator is built. `FR-53`..`FR-59` are `deferred` because of those two rows: `FR-54`,
`FR-56`, `FR-58` and `FR-59` name `G-23` in their Evidence cell and `FR-57` names `G-24`, while `FR-53` and
`FR-55` cite `G-13` for the absent CSR file. Both rows carry an owner and a 2026-12-31 target in the canonical
gap table of `doc/verification.md`.

WP-4 (2026-10-30) closed `G-10` (no sanity flow) and `G-25` (the store address was `rs1 + rs2`) and opened
`G-26` (operand forwarding) and `G-27` (branch redirect). **WP-4c (2026-11-04) closed both of them, and `G-20`
with them**: `rv32ui-p-addi`, `-beq` and `-and` pass, the six branch images pass, and the negative controls
`forward-rs1`, `negative-branch` and `branch-redirect` each fail the test that cites the row they reintroduce,
so the closure is observed rather than inferred. `G-19` survived that run and was isolated on `lb`/`lbu`,
instead of being a note about a lost write-back; WP-6 closed it on 2026-11-05, as the paragraph below records.

The 2026-11-04 round-8 decision opened `G-28`, which is a verification-architecture gap and not a product gap:
the core had no retire/commit observation, so the only instruction-level visibility was a fetch, and
`if_id_flush` can discard a fetch the monitor has already accepted. It had two consequences. A functional-coverage
bin can be satisfied by an instruction the core never executed. And the reference model cannot be monitor-driven,
which is what `C-16` requires, so the same model cannot be reused in a passive agent and there is no seam for the
Spike commit-trace comparison. `E3` owned the fix and delivered it on 2026-11-07 (WP-8, below). `G-28` was **not a
release blocker in the sense of `Q-11`**, and its closure does not change the blocker list: it did not make a
product claim false, it made the verification weaker than it looked.

**WP-6 (2026-11-05) closed `G-08` and `G-19`, narrowed `G-22`, and added one row.** The store path now places the
value in the addressed lane, drives `data_wr_strb[3:0]` for exactly the bytes it writes and reports the width
decoded from `funct3`, which `T-13` (`rv32ui-p-sb`), `T-14` (`rv32ui-p-sh`) and `T-15` (`rv32ui-p-st_ld`)
observe, so `G-08` stops being a release blocker and `FR-11` becomes `verified`. The register file now takes the
global clock enable, so the write-back a load-use freeze could drop (`G-19`) survives, and `rv32ui-p-lb`/`-lbu`
went green. Of `G-22` the byte strobes and the lane exist and only the second beat does not, which is `FR-42`.
The new row is `G-30`: during a load-use freeze the branch is evaluated with the load result unresolved,
`branch_taken` pulses and `branch_taken_q` flushes a valid in-flight fetch, so `rv32ui-p-ld_st` fails `testnum=2`
(`tohost=5`). The verification lead owned it with the same 2026-12-31 target, and it is the row `FR-17` cited
in place of the closed `G-19`; WP-7 closed it the same day, as the paragraph below records.

**WP-7 (2026-11-05, its coverage measured 2026-11-06) closed `G-03`, `G-07` and `G-30`, opened and closed `G-31`, and re-attributed `G-15`.**
`G-03` was the bin half of the `A-09` bar and it is now full: `cp_inst` 37/37, `cp_width` 3/3 and `cg_mem`
13/13 over the 39-test manifest, 100 % with no uncovered bin (`build/cov_report/summary.json`). The row keeps
its caveat rather than its blocker: a bin is an accepted **fetch**, not a retire, so the bar is met as `A-09`
defines it while `G-28` — still open when that measurement was taken — said that the instrument could not tell an
executed instruction from a discarded one. `G-28` closed on 2026-11-07, but the caveat stays because coverage was
not migrated to the retire stream (WP-8, below). `G-07` closed because `rtl/jump_decision.sv:115-116` masks bit 0 of a `JALR` target, which `T-17` and
`T-37` observe — `T-37` being the project-local odd-target image, the only one that can see the mask, since
`rv32ui-p-jalr` jumps to aligned labels only. `G-30` closed because `rtl/control.sv:108` gates `branch_taken_q`
with `~branch_op_stall`, so the branch a frozen load feeds is no longer decided on an unresolved value;
`T-16` (`rv32ui-p-ld_st`) passes and fails the `branch-taken-stale` mutant. `G-31` was **found inside the work
package and closed in it**: a `JALR` target reads `rs1`, but the jump-compare forward mux was gated on
`cond_jump` alone, so a `JALR` immediately after its producer read a stale register. The mask alone left `T-17`
failing identically, which is what exposed the second defect; `rtl/control.sv:131-137` now opens the mux for
`cond_jump` or `jalr`, `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:289` exports `jalr`, and `T-17` passes and fails the
`jalr-no-forward` mutant. Because it never left the work package it never joined the release-blocker list, and
it is recorded here so the closure is not inferred from a green run. `G-15` is **re-attributed**: `rv32ui-p-fence_i`
passes, so its earlier timeout was a `G-31` symptom — `src/riscv-tests/isa/rv64ui/fence_i.S`, which
`src/riscv-tests/isa/rv32ui/fence_i.S` includes, does `la a5,2f` and one instruction later `jalr t1,a5,0`
(`:26`/`:27`, with the same pair at `:40`/`:41`), so the jump read a stale `rs1` — and not a defect of the
instruction-fetch path. The `G-07` bit-0 mask is a no-op in that image: the four `jalr`s at `:27`, `:41`, `:57`
and `:60` all target a word-aligned address. The gap itself stays open and unchanged: there is still no
instruction cache, no line fill and no `ICACHE_BYTES`, so `FR-27`, `FR-28`, `FR-31` and `FR-34` stay
`deferred`; what changed is that `G-15` no longer explains a failing image, so `NFR-05` is no longer narrowed
by it.

**WP-8 (2026-11-07) closed `G-28` and `G-32`, and `C-16` is met.** The core carries `valid`, its `pc` and its
instruction word to write-back, so an instruction that reaches WB is visible with everything a commit trace
needs; a `bind` probe — not a DUT port, the port list still has sixteen — gates that on `valid_wb && ma_wb_clk_en`
and publishes `pc`, `inst`, `rd` and the written data. The monitor republishes the event and the reference model
runs from it with no program feed: `program_port`, `rm_export` and `publish_program()` are gone, and the program
image is configuration through `uvm_config_db`. `T-44`'s straight-line program commits 72 instructions for 72
fetches, and `T-45` commits 10 for 12 — the taken branch discards the two words at `0x0c` and `0x10`, where 0
retires are observed — so a fetch-driven and a retire-driven model are distinguishable, and both tests fail on
the `retire-every-slot` mutant. In the same package the monitor began correlating request and response by
handshake, which is `G-32`: the address of an accepted request waits in a queue until its response is accepted,
`FETCH_PAIR` reports 0 mismatches on both tests, and that queue holds more than one entry when a cache or a wait
state puts more than one request in flight. The data port keeps its level-based sampling and its documented
limit, because it has no per-access completion pulse; that is `E8`'s `G-35`, the read half (its store half closed with `G-18` on 2026-11-19). One limit deliberately survives:
coverage still samples the fetch, so every `cp_inst` bin is an accepted fetch and not a retire.

**`NFR-07` (2026-11-18) is measured, and `G-05` is narrowed to the four rows it still holds.** The
FPGA/synthesis flow was generalised rather than rewritten — `FPGA_TOP`, `FPGA_PROJECT_NAME` and `FPGA_PART` are
cache variables, the source list is the `srclist/<top>.srclist` expansion, and board files are optional — so
`make lint_fpga` now lints and synthesises *this* core instead of the SoC the flow was borrowed from. At RTL
`sha256:b914f2e1…`, Vivado 2024.1 on `xczu3eg-sbva484-1-i`, out-of-context at default synthesis settings: 10
sources, **4069 cells**, **1405 flip-flops** and **0 inferred latches**, and the DRC rule `LUTLP-1` reports **0**
combinational loops; the linter itself reports 28 infos, 9 warnings, 0 critical warnings and 0 errors. `NFR-07`
therefore moves `deferred` → `verified`: its target is a number this document already fixed at zero, both clauses
measured zero, and the arms nothing observed are carried as limits rather than as doubt — **one part**, because this
document records no target part (`A-08`), and **the only configuration that can be built**, because the three named
configurations of §5.2 are spec target and cannot be elaborated (`G-13`, `G-15`, `G-16`), so each one that becomes
buildable needs its own run of the command before it inherits the zero. The netlist pass's single critical warning,
`[Synth 8-5799] Converted tricell instance 'u_controli_2086' to logic`, is the out-of-context mode converting a
boundary tricell and not a defect, and the measurement file says so rather than dropping it. `G-05` is **narrowed,
not closed**: its statement is the absence of timing, area and power evidence, and the four rows it still blocks
(`NFR-01`..`NFR-04`) are untouched by a latch count. The five linter messages — `INFER-2` at
`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:264`, `RESET-2` on `data_rd_en_ex_reg`, two `ASSIGN-5` partial assignments and one
`ASSIGN-6` unread signal — are recorded in `doc/measurements/NFR-07-lint.md`; synthesis inferred **no** latch on the
`INFER-2` case, because its signals are assigned before the case, so the message is the linter's latch-risk signal
and not a latch, and triage of the other four belongs to the designer. Two registers the netlist pass removes,
`sepc` and `scause` in `rtl/control.sv:334-345`, are the synthesis view of `G-01`/`G-02` and not a new gap. The
canonical gap row is the one in `doc/verification.md`; the narrowing is recorded here and in the measurement file.

**WP-13 (2026-11-19) closed `G-18`, `G-22` and `G-34`, and opened `G-35` — the one row it found and did not fix.** The two long-standing data-port rows go
first: `G-18` because a store is now held in MA while the grant is withheld (`rtl/control.sv:295-301`, `FR-38`,
`T-53`, mutant `store-grant-ignored`), and `G-22` because a crossing access is now issued as two beats, each confined to one word — beat 1 carrying the low bytes of the access, beat 2 the high bytes — by `rtl/misaligned_split.sv` instead of one unaligned one (`FR-42`, `T-52`, mutant
`no-misaligned-split`), which is the second beat `G-22` had been narrowed to since WP-6. `G-34` is different in
kind: it was **found inside the work package** and is recorded because the red is the proof it existed — the
halfword lane was halfword-granular, so `LH`/`LHU`/`SH` at byte offset 1 took bytes 0-1, `T-51` measured that
red first — an `LH` at offset 1 returned the bytes at 0-1 — and the `halfword-lane-ignore-bit0` mutant restores the
defect and is detected by check 11. It never left the work package, so it was never a release blocker and no
shipped revision carries it, exactly as `G-31` was recorded. **`G-35` is the finding that matters:** a read whose
grant is withheld for two or more cycles is **lost**, because the load is presented for one cycle and the stall
condition acts one cycle later (`mux_sel_rd_wb` is the write-back stage's select, so it qualifies the access after
MA has stopped driving it); `verif-author` measured it as a deadlock against a memory that withheld a read grant.
`FR-21` is therefore `partial` rather than `implemented`, and its acceptance keeps the two latencies that work —
granted immediately and granted one cycle late. The fix is the request/response form of the data port (`E8`), the
epic `G-18` belonged to; with `G-18` and `G-22` closed, `G-35` is the only member of it left. `G-05` is unchanged:
timing, area and power are still owed. The gate results behind all four rows are regress **49/49**, negative-control
**47/47**, sanity **43/43** and coverage **52 of 52** bins with **40 of 40** on the `A-09` bar, at RTL
`sha256:f84ea86b…` and TB `sha256:7860d1b0…`.

| Gap | Statement | Impact on this document |
|---|---|---|
| `G-03` | **Closed 2026-11-06 by WP-7.** The functional-coverage half of `A-09` was declared but not hit: WP-5 added the 37 instruction bins and WP-6 the store width and lane, and 19 instruction bins stayed open. | closed: `make coverage` at RTL `sha256:3541dfb8…` reports `cp_inst` 37/37, `cp_width` 3/3 and `cg_mem` 13/13 — 50 of 50 report bins, 100 %, `uncovered` empty — so `NFR-06`'s bin target is met. The caveat is kept and is not a blocker: a bin is an accepted fetch, not a retire, and coverage was not migrated to the retire stream WP-8 added (`G-28`, closed 2026-11-07) |
| `G-04` | **Statement narrowed 2026-11-07 by WP-8 and extended 2026-11-18 by WP-9; the row stays open.** Only the load/store subset is model-checked by the scoreboard: the reference model executes `LW`, `LB`, `LH`, `LBU`, `LHU` and `SW` and publishes a prediction only for `SW`, which is the only thing the scoreboard compares, while every other instruction has no architectural effect the model tracks, so an `rv32ui` image is judged by its own `tohost` self-check and not by the model. **Since 2026-11-18 a second and independent model exists beside it:** the monitor dumps a canonical commit line per retired instruction — `pc`, `rd`, the written value and the instruction word, with a dash where there is no register write — under `+RETIRE_LOG`, Spike logs the same image with `--log-commits`, and `T-47` (`rv32ui-p-add`, 459 commits), `T-48` (`rv32ui-p-beq`, 285) and `T-49` (`rv32ui-p-st_ld`, 477) compare the two by commit index on all four fields, so for those three images the instruction stream — every commit's address, encoding, destination register and written value — is checked against an implementation this project did not write. | the six as-built rows it still holds — `FR-13`, `FR-14`, `FR-15`, `FR-19`, `FR-20` and `FR-21` — cannot become `verified`, and the residual is exact. The canonical line drops Spike's `mem` suffix on purpose, so a store's **address and data are invisible**: `store-width-cast` still produces an identical 477 of 477 trace on `st_ld`. The dump **suppresses `rd = x0` by construction**, so `FR-14`'s `x0` arm is never a commit. The comparison is architectural state, so the bus properties of `FR-20` (one fetch address per cycle, request asserted with it) and `FR-21` (a load completes only on the ready) are **outside it entirely**. And it spans **3 of the 43 images**, so `FR-13`'s `-2048`/`+2047` offsets and the 0/1/2-distance matrices of `FR-15` and `FR-19` are still unobserved. `FR-42`'s retirement arm joins them: no named test counts the instructions that retire for a two-beat split pair — `T-52` judges the four data-port cases, and the retire-count tests `T-44`..`T-46` run straight-line, branch and skip programs, none of which contains a crossing access. A **release blocker** since `Q-11`, and still one. The `ADD` encoding of `FR-01` used to be exercised but never read back; since 2026-11-06 `T-43` runs `rv32ui-p-add` and observes its result, so no clause of `FR-01` rests on this row any more |
| `G-06` | **Narrowed 2026-11-21 by WP-14.** An unimplemented encoding no longer executes as a no-operation: the decode's legality table (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:271-361`) traps on an opcode outside §4.1 and on a reserved `funct3`/`funct7` of an implemented instruction. What stays open is the **capability**, not the policing: `M` and `C` are not executed (`FR-43`, `FR-44`), `FENCE` orders nothing (`FR-26`) and `fence.i` is a no-operation (`FR-31`), while `MRET` and `WFI` are legal no-operations by decision (`FR-45`, `FR-46`). | `FR-26`, `FR-43`, `FR-44`, `FR-45` and `FR-46` stay `deferred`; `Q-05` decided that each extension stops trapping for its own encodings when its requirement lands, so this row closes when `FR-43`/`FR-44` execute, `FR-26` orders and `FR-31` invalidates; §7 marks RV32I partial for the `M`/`C` bits and `FENCE` only |
| `G-07` | **Closed 2026-11-05 by WP-7.** `JALR` did not clear bit 0 of the target address, so a target computed as `(rs1 + imm) & ~1` continued at an odd address; `rtl/jump_decision.sv:115-116` now masks bit 0 when `base_addr_sel == RS1`, which is the `JALR` case only, so `JAL` and the branch targets keep every address bit. | closed: `T-17` (`rv32ui-p-jalr`) and `T-37` (`rv32ui-p-jalr_unaligned`, project-local) pass, and `T-37` fails on the `jalr-keep-bit0` mutant, where the fetch address is odd and the image fails `testnum=3`; the row leaves the release-blocker list of `Q-06` and `FR-07`'s mask clause is observed, though `FR-07` itself stays `partial` on its instruction-address-misaligned clause (`G-13`) |
| `G-08` | **Closed 2026-11-05 by WP-6.** Sub-word **stores** used to drive the same lane whatever `data_addr[1:0]` said, with no byte strobes and a width output that carried `funct3[0]`; the store path now places the value in the addressed lane, asserts `data_wr_strb[3:0]` for exactly the bytes it drives and reports the width decoded from `funct3`. The load half closed in v0.7.4 under `T-06`. | closed: `T-13` (`rv32ui-p-sb`), `T-14` (`rv32ui-p-sh`) and `T-15` (`rv32ui-p-st_ld`) pass and the mutants `store-lane-offset` and `store-strb-all` are each detected by check 11, so `FR-11` is `verified` and the row leaves the release-blocker list of `Q-06` |
| `G-09` | No fetch stall exists; the program counter advances while the grant is withheld. | prerequisite for `G-15`: a cache cannot stall a core that cannot stall; `FR-28` is the requirement that closes it |
| `G-13` | **Narrowed 2026-11-21 by WP-14: the CSR half is built, and what remains is the instruction-address-misaligned cause.** The CSR file, the six `Zicsr` instructions and the trap entry exist (`rtl/csr_file.sv`, `FR-47`, `FR-50`), so `FR-23`, `FR-29`, `FR-32`, `FR-37`, `FR-47`, `FR-49`, `FR-50`, `FR-51` and `FR-52` are no longer blocked by this row. Still absent is a cause-0 path: a `JALR`, jump or branch target whose bit 1 is set while `IALIGN=32` is fetched rather than trapped, because the causes this build raises are 2, 3 and 11 only (`rtl/riscv_definitions_pkg.sv:291-298`). | the instruction-address-misaligned clause of `FR-07` keeps that row `partial` (`T-17`/`T-37` observe the mask half only); `FR-44` (`C`, `IALIGN=16`) retires the clause rather than implementing it, and the rest of epic `E6` is discharged |
| `G-14` | No machine-mode interrupt subsystem: no timer, no external controller, no software interrupt. | `FR-39`, `FR-40`, `FR-36`, and the vectored mode of `FR-30`, stay `deferred`; `FR-48` and the `mtip`/`meip`/`msip` inputs of §6 are the target |
| `G-15` | **Re-attributed 2026-11-05 by WP-7; attribution corrected 2026-11-06.** No instruction cache and no line-based fetch; `ICACHE_BYTES` and `ICACHE_LINE` do not exist. The gap no longer explains a failing image: `rv32ui-p-fence_i` passes, and its earlier timeout was a `G-31` symptom, because `src/riscv-tests/isa/rv64ui/fence_i.S` — the file `src/riscv-tests/isa/rv32ui/fence_i.S` includes — does `la a5,2f` at `:26` and `jalr t1,a5,0` at `:27` (and `la a5,3f` at `:40` with `jalr t1,a5,0` at `:41`), so the jump read a stale `rs1`; the `G-07` mask is a no-op there, since the targets of all four `jalr`s (`:27`, `:41`, `:57`, `:60`) are word-aligned. | `FR-27`, `FR-28`, `FR-31` and `FR-34` stay `deferred`, §5.2 stays a target and not a description, and the cache is still `F-10`'s target; `NFR-05` is no longer narrowed by this row, and the I-cache itself is not a release blocker |
| `G-16` | No multi-hart support: no hart-identity input, no `NUM_HARTS`, no `A` extension. | `FR-32`, `FR-33`, `FR-35`, `FR-36` stay `deferred`; `MCU_SMP` cannot be built |
| `G-17` | No decision log: `doc/decisions.md` does not exist, so `D-01` to `D-11` live in §0.1. | decisions are not indexed beside the requirements they justify |
| `G-18` | **Closed 2026-11-19 by WP-13.** Stores did not wait for the data grant: the stall condition needed a load's destination, so a store drove its strobe for one cycle whatever `data_ready` said and a memory that withheld the grant lost it. | closed: `rtl/control.sv:295-301` extends the pipeline freeze to `data_wr_en_ma`, so the store is held in MA — the stage that drives the address, the data and the strobes — until the grant arrives, and `:325` keeps the flush from firing into the frozen instruction. `T-53` (`riscv_small_store_grant_test`, wp 13) withholds the grant for two cycles and passes at RTL `sha256:f84ea86b…` and TB `sha256:7860d1b0…`, failing on the `store-grant-ignored` mutant, so `FR-38` is `verified`. It was a dated commitment and not a release blocker |
| `G-19` | **Closed 2026-11-05 by WP-6.** A load-use freeze also disabled the register-file write, because the ID stage's load-use insertion cleared a clock enable the register file shared with it, so the write-back of a younger instruction could be lost (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:44`, `:211`, `rtl/riscv_small.sv:166`, `rtl/reg_file.sv:99`); the register file now takes the global `clk_en`. | closed: `rv32ui-p-lb` and `rv32ui-p-lbu` went green in the 38/42 measurement of 2026-11-05 and `T-13`/`T-14`/`T-15` pass; `FR-17` left `partial` in v0.8.0, when `G-30` closed under `T-16` |
| `G-20` | **Closed 2026-11-04 by WP-4c.** The jump/branch target used to add `imm[20:0]` as an unsigned slice, so a backward control transfer landed at `base + (imm mod 2^21)`; `rtl/jump_decision.sv:111` now adds the whole sign-extended immediate. | closed: `rv32ui-p-jal` and the six branch images pass, `FR-08` is `verified` by `T-12` and the branch images, and `FR-06` is `verified` by `T-18`; `FR-07`'s mask clause reached `T-17`/`T-37` once WP-7 closed `G-07`, and the row itself stays `partial` on the instruction-address-misaligned clause of `G-13` |
| `G-21` | No reset synchroniser and no reset-style macro: every sequential block hardcodes `always_ff @(posedge clk or negedge rst_n)` (`rtl/instruction_fetch.sv:83`, `rtl/instruction_fetch.sv:108`, `rtl/control.sv:334`, and four more modules), so assertion and release are both asynchronous. | `FR-41` is `partial`: half the requirement is as-built and half is absent; the fix is the synchroniser and the style macro of `D-09` |
| `G-22` | **Narrowed 2026-11-05 by WP-6 and closed 2026-11-19 by WP-13.** WP-6 built the byte strobes and the addressed lane and left the second beat; WP-13 built the second beat. A crossing access is now two beats, each confined to one word — beat 1 carries the low bytes of the access, beat 2 the high bytes — issued by `rtl/misaligned_split.sv`: `split_needed` derived from lane plus width (`:108`), the second beat at `(addr & ~3) + 4` (`:117`) with the strobes of those high bytes (`:113`), the store operand shifted to byte 0 (`:119`) and the load's two words merged before extension (`:139-143`), replacing the single unaligned access at `alu_ma`. | closed: `FR-42` is `verified` under `T-52` (`sanity:rv32ui-p-ma_data`), which passes at RTL `sha256:f84ea86b…` and TB `sha256:7860d1b0…` and fails on the `no-misaligned-split` mutant, and `rv32ui-p-ma_data` is the last `rv32ui` image to go green (`NFR-05` 42/42 bundled). It was a dated commitment and not a release blocker |
| `G-23` | No debug subsystem: the DUT has no debug port, no debug mode, no `dcsr`/`dpc`/`dscratch` and no abstract-command or program-buffer path (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:45-62`, `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:351-353`). | `FR-53` to `FR-56`, `FR-58` and `FR-59` stay `deferred`, and `T-05` cannot exist until it closes |
| `G-24` | No trigger unit: `tselect`/`tdata1`..`tdata3`/`tinfo` are not decoded and no address or data comparator exists (`rtl/instruction_decode.sv:351-353`). | `FR-57` stays `deferred`; breakpoints and watchpoints cannot be observed in any configuration |
| `G-26` | **Closed 2026-11-04 by WP-4c.** Branch-compare forwarding: the comparison in ID read the register file whatever the producer's stage, because the `.jmp_src1`/`.jmp_src2` forward selects were unconnected (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:177-178`) and the EX forward source `alu_ex` was undriven (`rtl/execution.sv:259`), so the EX selection sourced `X`; the ALU forward itself was functional. The visible effect was that the `li` expansion (`lui` + `addi`) that every `rv32ui` image uses for its operand setup was misread at the branch (`rv32ui-p-addi` case 3 read 1 instead of 2). | closed: `T-11` fails on the `forward-rs1` control and passes on the RTL, `FR-16` and `FR-18` are `verified`, `FR-19` is `implemented`, and `NFR-05` moved from 1/42 to 34/42 in the same step |
| `G-27` | **Closed 2026-11-04 by WP-4c.** Branch redirect: the first instruction at a taken branch target was not fetched or executed, so the `RVTEST_FAIL` path of every image lost `slli gp,gp,1` and reported a corrupted `testnum` (`rv32ui-p-and` wrote 3 where 5 was expected). | closed: the target address now appears in the fetch history, `T-10` fails on the `branch-redirect` control and passes on the RTL, and the six branch images pass, so `FR-08` is `verified` by `T-12` |
| `G-28` | **Closed 2026-11-07 by WP-8.** No retire/commit observation existed: the only instruction-level visibility on the DUT ports was the fetch (`tb/env/agents/riscv_small_agent/riscv_small_monitor.sv:38-47`), and an accepted fetch can be discarded by `if_id_flush` (`rtl/control.sv:267-287`), so fetch != retire. The core now pipelines `valid`, `pc` and the instruction word to write-back (`rtl/instruction_fetch.sv`, `rtl/instruction_decode.sv`, `rtl/execution.sv`, `rtl/memory_access.sv`, `rtl/riscv_small.sv`), and a `bind` probe inside the core — `riscv_small_retire_if` (`tb/riscv_small_interface.sv:133-157`), not a DUT port, so the port list stays at sixteen — publishes `valid_wb && ma_wb_clk_en` with `pc`, `inst`, `rd` and the written data. | closed: the monitor publishes the retire event and the reference model consumes only the monitor, with no program feed — `program_port`, `rm_export` and `publish_program()` are deleted and `grep` finds none in `tb/` or `scripts/` — so `C-16` is met. `T-44` commits 72 instructions for 72 fetches; `T-45` commits 10 for 12 fetches, the two missing words being exactly the instructions the taken branch discards at `0x0c` and `0x10`, at which 0 retires occur; both fail on the `retire-every-slot` mutant. **`T-46` is what proved the invariant is about *which* `pc` was fetched, not how many:** a taken branch lets exactly two fetch slots go whatever the jump distance, so `T-45`, whose branch skips two words, made `fetch − commit == \|skip_pc\|` true by coincidence; `T-46`'s branch skips three (12 words placed, 9 executed, 11 fetched), so the difference is 2 against a skip set of 3, and the checker now asserts claim 6 as a per-`pc` set — every fetched `pc` is an executed `pc` or a `pc` the control flow skips — with claim 7 gated to the runs where the count is still a constant. The limit is kept where it now lives: coverage was not migrated to the retire stream, so a `cp_inst` bin is still an accepted **fetch**, not a retire |
| `G-30` | **Closed 2026-11-05 by WP-7.** A load-use freeze evaluated a branch with the load result unresolved: the decision was made while the load it consumed was still in flight, so `branch_taken` pulsed on the unresolved value and `branch_taken_q` then flushed a valid in-flight fetch (`rtl/control.sv:267-287`). `rtl/control.sv:108` now gates that register with `~branch_op_stall`, so the echo waits for the operand the branch needs. | closed: `T-16` runs `rv32ui-p-ld_st` — the image that used to fail `testnum=2` (`tohost=5`) — and passes, and the `branch-taken-stale` mutant reintroduces the defect and is detected by check 11; `FR-17` is `verified`. Found on 2026-11-05 by WP-6 once `G-29` stopped masking it |
| `G-31` | **Opened and closed 2026-11-05 by WP-7.** `JALR`'s `rs1` was never forwarded: the jump-compare forward mux was gated on `cond_jump` alone (`rtl/control.sv:137`), so a `JALR` decoded immediately after its producer read the stale register even though the branch path forwarded correctly. | closed: `rtl/control.sv:131-137` opens the mux for `cond_jump` and `jalr` and `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:289` exports `jalr`; `T-17` passes and fails the `jalr-no-forward` mutant. The row records why it exists rather than what it blocks: the mask fix alone left `T-17` failing identically, which is the evidence that separates this defect from `G-07`. It never left the work package, so it was never a release blocker and no shipped revision carries it |
| `G-32` | **Opened 2026-11-06 and closed 2026-11-07 by WP-8.** The monitor published the fetch pair from two different cycles: it sampled `inst_addr` and `inst_data` in the same `@(posedge clk)`, while the driver assigns `inst_data` two time units after that edge and the core captures `pc_id <= pc_fetch` with `inst_id <= inst_data`, so the published word belonged to the previous address. A `pc -> word` table built on it would execute the wrong instruction, and the rule could not survive a cache or a wait state. | closed: the monitor now correlates request and response **by handshake** — the address of an accepted request enters an outstanding-request queue and leaves it when a response is accepted, so the published `(pc, word)` pair is exact — and `riscv_small_fetch_pair_checker` reports `FETCH_PAIR` green, 0 mismatches over 72 pairs in `T-44` and 12 in `T-45`. That queue is the mechanism that survives a cache or a wait state: it holds as many entries as there are requests in flight, one with `LINE_WORDS = 1`, so `F-10`/`FR-27` do not need the monitor rewritten again. The **data-port half is documented, not fixed**: the flat port has no per-access completion pulse, so the monitor still samples it by level, and fixing that needs the req/rsp form of the data port (`E8`, `G-18`) |
| `G-33` | **Opened 2026-11-18 and closed the same day by WP-11 P3a.** The fix is the parameter and the reset that consumes it: `rtl/riscv_small.sv:43` declares `RESET_VECTOR` as a `dataBus_t` defaulting to `32'h8000_0000`, `:139` passes it to the fetch stage, `rtl/instruction_fetch.sv:47` carries it, and `rtl/instruction_fetch.sv:110` reads `pc <= RESET_VECTOR;` where it used to write `pc <= 'b0;`, so the first fetch after reset is the parameter and no longer `0x0`. | closed for exactly what the evidence observes, and no more: **the parameter exists and the reset consumes it.** `T-50` (`riscv_small_reset_vector_test`, wp 11) captures the first fetch address after reset at the instruction port and compares it with the elaborated parameter, and the `reset-vector-zero` mutant (`scripts/verify/mutants.tsv`, the same line reverted to `pc <= 'b0;`) fails that test under check 11, so the assignment is load-bearing and not incidental; both run at RTL `sha256:b914f2e1…` and TB `sha256:066dce85…`, where `make regress` is 46/46, `make negative-control` 44/44 and `make spike-trace` 3/3. **Parameter configurability is not demonstrated, which is why `FR-41` does not move:** `T-50` runs with the default value, so `pc <= RESET_VECTOR` and `pc <= 32'h8000_0000` are indistinguishable, no mutant can separate them, and the "two different values of the parameter in two builds" arm of `FR-41`'s acceptance therefore still has no observation — the second elaboration was deferred by the designer on 2026-11-18, with what it would and would not have proved recorded in `.opencode/plan/wp-11-reset-vector.md` §9.1. The harness that used to hide the divergence now runs the spec's address: `scripts/verify/sanity/link.ld` links at `0x80000000`, `scripts/verify/sanity.cmake` rebases the hex with `objcopy --change-addresses=-0x80000000` so `tohost` needs no mask, and Spike runs `--isa=rv32i --log=… --log-commits` only, the `-m0:0x40000`, `--no-dm` and `--disable-dtb` flags having disappeared with the low base that collided with the debug module `[0,0x1000)` and the device-tree ROM `[0x1000,0x2000)`. Not a release blocker: like `G-21` it was a dated commitment under `A-09`, so the blocker list stays `G-04` and `G-11` |
| `G-34` | **Opened and closed 2026-11-19 by WP-13.** The halfword lane ignored `addr[0]`: the lane a halfword access selected was halfword-granular, so `LH`/`LHU` at byte offset 1 returned bytes 0-1 and `SH` at the same offset wrote bytes 0-1 instead of 1-2. It is the sub-word lane defect of `G-08` surviving in the halfword path, and it survived because the tests that closed `G-08` presented even offsets only. | closed: the lane is byte-granular — `rtl/memory_access.sv:96` (`(ld_lane == 2'd3) ? 2'd2 : ld_lane`, from `alu_wb.u_data[1:0]` at `:84`) for the load and `rtl/execution.sv:224` (`4'b0011 << alu_result.u_data[1:0]`) for the store — and `T-51` (`riscv_small_halfword_lane_test`, wp 13) observes offset 1 in both directions at RTL `sha256:f84ea86b…` and TB `sha256:7860d1b0…`, failing on the `halfword-lane-ignore-bit0` mutant, whose two catalogue rows restore the granular lane in both files. **The red is the proof it existed:** the defect was measured first (`LH` at offset 1 gave bytes 0-1), then fixed, and the red run that opened the row is stored at `build/red/T-51/results.json` (record `red`, RTL `sha256:a88a3060…`, `status=fail`, 4 scoreboard comparisons failing and `uvm_errors=5`); the work package had to open the row before it could close it. Recorded the way `G-31` was: found and fixed inside one package, so it never was a release blocker and no shipped revision carries it. `FR-11` and `FR-12` were overclaimed before this row — both cited `T-13`..`T-15`/`T-06`, which never present an odd halfword offset — and `T-51` is what observes them now |
| `G-35` | **Opened 2026-11-19 by WP-13, from `FR-21`. Open.** A read whose grant is withheld for two or more cycles is lost: the load is presented on the data port for one cycle, the freeze that should hold it uses `mux_sel_rd_wb` (`rtl/riscv_small.sv:309`) — the write-back stage's memory select — and acts one cycle later, when the request is already off the bus and is never re-presented. Measured by `verif-author` on 2026-11-19 as a deadlock against a testbench memory that withheld the grant on reads. The store half of the same contract was fixed in the same work package and is not part of this row (`FR-38`, `G-18` closed). | the load's completion must be able to land later than one cycle after presentation, which a flat port that drives the request for a single cycle cannot express: the fix is the request/response form of the data port, epic `E8`. `FR-21` moves `implemented` → `partial` on this row — its clause ("a load is complete only when the memory reports the data ready") is exactly what fails on the two-or-more-cycle case — while its acceptance names only the two latencies that work. Owner designer, target 2026-12-31 (`Q-12`); with `G-18` and `G-22` closed, this row is the only member of `E8` left, and it is not a release blocker (it was a dated commitment under `A-09`, and `NFR-05`/`NFR-06` do not depend on it) |
| `G-36` | **Opened 2026-11-19 by the WP-13 signoff audit. Open.** The scoreboard does not require every observed data write to be compared. `run_phase` takes one monitor publication and then blocks on one model prediction (`tb/env/top/riscv_small_scoreboard.sv:93-101`), and the model publishes one prediction per retired store, so a split store's **two** publications face one prediction: the pairing shifts by one for the rest of the run — or, when it is the run's last store, the second publication never gets a partner and the loop is killed at the end of the test. `check_phase` raises `RM_NO_PREDICTION` only when `pred_count == 0` (`:144`), and `report_phase` **prints** `obs_count` against `pass_count + fail_count` without failing when they differ (`:156-164`), so a run can end green with a publication never compared. **Latent:** no test reaches it — every model-mode test stores at a word-aligned address (`tb/tests/sequence_lib/riscv_small_basic_load_store_seq.sv:52,97`, both address generators `* 4`, and `T-06` stays inside a word) and the crossing images are `sanity:` rows with no model — but WP-13 made a crossing store two publications by construction. | the desynchronisation is a scoreboard defect and not an RTL one, and the split is judged elsewhere: `FR-42` **does not rest on this row** — its `verified` stands on the four data-port cases of `T-52` (`sanity:rv32ui-p-ma_data`, whose image's own `tohost` is the verdict) and on the beat rules of `riscv_small_strb_checker`, neither of which pairs a publication with a prediction. What the row costs is the next directed crossing store, which would desynchronise the pairing rather than be reported as a mismatch. Owner verification lead; target the next WP — skip beat 2 in the scoreboard or publish per beat from the model, plus a non-vacuity guard that `obs_count == pass_count + fail_count` |
| `G-37` | **Opened 2026-11-21 by WP-14. Open.** The arms of the CSR/trap work package that no test exercises: `FR-22`'s external `exception[3:0]` request, which is **not merely unexercised but undrivable without a testbench change**: `tb/riscv_small_tb.sv:32` ties the DUT port to a local constant (`logic [3:0] exception = '0;`) and `:79` connects that constant, so the driver never drives `intf.exception` and no sequence can raise the request; `FR-23`'s `mepc` WARL write and read-back, `mepc[1]` under `IALIGN=32` included; `FR-29`'s second `mtvec` value and its `MODE` ≥ 2 legalisation; `FR-32`'s 0/1/3 identifier sweep and the multi-hart set; `FR-37`'s other configurations, because a build that sets `ENABLE_M`, `ENABLE_C` or `ENABLE_A` advertises an extension this RTL does not execute; `FR-49`'s "`instret` counts retired instructions without counting `ECALL`/`EBREAK`", observed only as "the trapping instruction never retires"; `FR-50`'s `rv32mi-p-mcsr`, which **did not run** — there is no `rv32mi` environment (no `riscv_test_mi.h`, plan §4.3) — and its `NUM_TRIGGERS` > 0 window; `FR-52`'s read after a request on the external bus and its handler-fetches-the-encoding arm; and `D12`, a trap raised while `split_stall` is high. | the rows stay `verified` on their shall and on the main clause a named test does observe, with the arm named here; nothing on this list releases a phase-1 obligation, and `FR-50`'s own row states that the image did not run. Owner verification lead; target the next WP — one directed test per arm, plus the `rv32mi` environment that `NFR-05`'s `rv32mi` leg waits on |
| `G-38` | **Opened 2026-11-21 by WP-14. Open.** A reserved configuration of an implemented instruction retires instead of trapping: `ECALL` and `EBREAK` with a non-zero `rd`/`rs1` should raise cause 2 (`FR-24`) but decode as a legal trap with cause 11 and 3, because `rtl/instruction_decode.sv:334-336` keys on the CSR field alone and never checks `rd` or `rs1`. The reference model mirrors the RTL, so no test can see the divergence. | `FR-24` is `partial` on this row: the trapping families it names are built and observed by `T-03`, the reserved-encoding family is not policed. Owner designer/rtl; target the next WP — a decode check on `rd`/`rs1` in the SYSTEM group, and the matching model change in the same package so a test can see it |
| `G-39` | **Opened 2026-11-21 by the WP-14 documentation pass. Open.** `minstret` and `instret` are one architectural counter — `instret` is a read-only alias of `minstret` (vol. II §2.1.1.10) — and they read **different values**: `rtl/csr_file.sv:147`/`:149` return the raw `minstret_q`, while `:152`/`:155` return `instret_view = minstret_q + valid_ma + valid_wb` (`:125-127`). `mcycle`/`cycle` do not have the problem because both read `mcycle_view`. The module's own comment and the reference model assume the aliasing, and no test reads `minstret`, so nothing caught it. | a read of 0xB02 and a read of 0xC02 at the same point disagree by the instructions in flight, so `FR-50`'s "read-only views" clause is not met as built and the row is `partial` on this one. `FR-49` is untouched: its text is about `instret` counting retirements, which the view does correctly. Owner verification lead; target 2026-12-31 — one sampling point for both addresses (make `minstret` read the same view, or define the retire point consistently), plus a test that reads 0xB02 and 0xC02 at the same point |

### Open questions

None. The interview ledger is [spec-questions.md](spec-questions.md): 43 questions asked, 43 answered, nothing
open. The last six (`Q-30`..`Q-35`) were closed by the round-7 answers of 2026-10-28, and the debug set
(`Q-36`..`Q-42`) by the same round and the checkpoint that followed it; the last entry is `Q-43` of 2026-11-04,
the reference-model decision recorded as `C-16` — met 2026-11-07 by WP-8 — and `G-28`, closed the same day. This
revision applies each of them rather than asking
again. The table records where each answer landed, so a reader can check that no clause re-decides one.

| Question | The answer it produced | Where it lands in this document |
|---|---|---|
| `Q-30` | `misa` is read-only: it reports the build, a write is ignored and does not trap, and `IALIGN` is therefore fixed by the configuration | `FR-37`, `FR-50`, §0.8 |
| `Q-31` | `WFI` is a no-operation: it retires immediately and a later interrupt leaves `mepc` at the following instruction | `FR-46`, §0.8 |
| `Q-32` | `mtval` is read-only zero; a handler that needs the encoding reads it from `mepc` | `FR-52`, `FR-24`, §0.8 |
| `Q-33` | the reset vector is the `RESET_VECTOR` build parameter, default `0x8000_0000`, and not a CSR | `FR-41`, §5.2, §0.8 |
| `Q-34` | no profile is claimed, and the product is machine-mode only: `S`/`U`, the MMU, `F`/`D` and `V` are out | `A-19`, `C-15`, §7.1, §0.8 |
| `Q-35` | a synchronous request carries its cause on `exception[3:0]`, and `mtip`, `meip` and `msip` are separate level-sensitive inputs | `FR-22`, `FR-39`, `FR-40`, §6, §0.8 |
| `Q-36` | debug reaches the hart through an external Debug Module behind a JTAG DTM, and the core keeps only the hart side | `C-13`, `D-10`, `FR-54`, `FR-55`, §6, §0.8 |
| `Q-37` | the hart side is the full interface the DM needs; the DMI is the DTM-to-DM link and not a core port | `C-13`, `FR-54`..`FR-56`, §6, §0.8 |
| `Q-38` | `NUM_TRIGGERS` of 0, 2 or 4 with default 2, and `mcontrol6` is the only trigger type | `FR-57`, §5.2, §0.8 |
| `Q-39` | a `dbg_auth` strap gates debug at the hart, driven by the platform and enabled by default, as an addition to the DM's authentication | `C-14`, `D-11`, `FR-59`, §6, §0.8 |
| `Q-40` | `resethaltreq` is supported, so a hart can be halted at the reset vector | `FR-54`, §0.8 |
| `Q-41` | no non-maskable interrupt in this revision; a watchdog NMI is phase 2 | §7.1, §0.8 |
| `Q-42` | `time`/`timeh` are read-only views of a dedicated 64-bit `mtime` input | `FR-49`, §6, §0.8 |

`Q-36`..`Q-42` are the debug increment's set: `Q-36`..`Q-41` were approved in round 7 and `Q-42` at the
checkpoint that followed, and each one is cited where the table above says it lands. Nothing in §4 or §5
re-decides any of them.

Answered in the conformance review on 2026-10-28, against the normative text: `Q-04` (the cause set is the
standard's, not a subset — `A-17`, `FR-23`, `FR-25`) and `Q-27` (`Zifencei` stays in the phase-1 surface, so the
fetch side is discharged without a coherence protocol — `A-18`, `FR-31`).

Answered in round 3 on 2026-10-28: `Q-08` (measure, then set the frequency — `NFR-01`, `A-08`), `Q-09`
(measure a named kernel — `NFR-02`), `Q-10` (report resources and power — `NFR-03`, `NFR-04`), `Q-11` (the
`A-09` signoff bar — `NFR-05`, `NFR-06`, release blockers), `Q-12` (gap ownership — `A-09`, §2), `Q-15`
(`fence` is full ordering — `FR-26`, `A-13`) and `Q-21` (a short burst is a protocol error — `FR-27`, §6).

Answered in round 4 on 2026-10-28, against the normative text: `Q-22` (`x0` — volume I §1.1.1, now `C-12`),
`Q-23` (reset state — volume II §2.1.4, now `A-04`), `Q-24` (misaligned EEI guarantee — volume I §1.1.6, now
`A-05` and the §6 lane contract), `Q-25` (reset style, a project decision — `D-09`, `FR-41`, `G-21`) and `Q-26`
(ordering, not transaction count — `A-07`).

Answered in round 5 on 2026-10-28, against maintained open cores: `Q-28` (byte strobes on the data port, with
the core placing the value — §6, `FR-11`, `G-22`) and `Q-29` (splitting a word-crossing access into two beats, each confined to one word — `FR-42`, `A-05`, `G-22`), both of those gap rows **closed 2026-11-19 by WP-13**.

Answered in round 2 on 2026-10-28 and no longer open here: `Q-05` (unimplemented encodings trap — `FR-24`,
`FR-25`, §4.1), `Q-06` (both ISA deviations fixed — `FR-07`, `FR-11`, `FR-12`, `G-07`, `G-08`), `Q-13` (burst
shape — `FR-27`, §6), `Q-14` (32-byte line — `C-09`, §5.2), `Q-16` (one binary per configuration — `D-05`,
`C-11`), `Q-17` (CLINT plus PLIC — `A-14`, `A-15`), `Q-18` (same reset address for every hart — `A-16`) and
`Q-20` (`ENABLE_A` = 0 for `MCU_RTOS` — `C-07`, §5.2, §7.1). Round 1 and the closed items are `Q-01`, `Q-02`,
`Q-03`, `Q-07` and `Q-19`.

## 10. References

- ISA: *The RISC-V Instruction Set Manual, Volume I: Unprivileged ISA*, **v20260120** (RV32I, `Zicsr`,
  `Zifencei`, `Zicntr`, `M`, `C`, `A`) — the normative edition for this revision (`D-08`); §1.1.1 (`x0`),
  §1.1.6 (misaligned accesses and `rd = x0`), §1.1.7 (`FENCE`) and chapter 17 (RVWMO) are cited above.
  Drafts are advisory only.
- Privileged: *The RISC-V Instruction Set Manual, Volume II: Privileged Architecture*, **v20260120**: the CSR
  address map (§1.1.1), `mtvec`, `mepc`, `mcause`, `mstatus`, `mstatush`, `mie`, `mip`, `mtval`, `mscratch`,
  `mret`, `mhartid`, `misa`, the machine counters and their read-only views, the identification registers, and
  §2.1.4 for the state defined at reset.
- Microarchitecture: [uarch.md](uarch.md) — blocks `BLK-IF`, `BLK-ID`, `BLK-EX`, `BLK-MA`, `BLK-CTL`, `BLK-REG`, `BLK-ALU`, `BLK-JMP`.
- Debug: *RISC-V Debug Specification*, **v1.0** (ratified 2025-02-21) — chapter 3, the Debug Module (§3.1.1 the
  Debug Module Interface, §3.1.5 run control, §3.1.7 abstract commands with §3.1.7.1 for the listing, §3.1.8
  the program buffer, §3.1.12 security, §3.1.14 the Debug Module registers); chapter 4, the `Sdext` extension
  (§4.1.1 debug mode, §4.1.5 single step, §4.1.9 the core debug registers, §4.1.10 the virtual debug registers
  and `dcsr.prv`); chapter 5, the `Sdtrig` extension (§5.1.7 the trigger module registers); and appendix A,
  Table 1, the suggested DMI between one DTM and one DM. This document cites it by chapter and never mixes its
  numbering with the ISA's (§0.7).
- Reset guidance: `.opencode/skills/systemverilog-rtl-verification/references/reset-and-fsm.md` — "Global reset
  selection" and "Reset synchronizer", the source of `D-09`.
- The exact section of each normative claim is tabulated in §0.7; `M` (vol. I §11.1), `C` (vol. I §27.1),
  `Zicntr` (vol. I §6.1), `Zifencei`, `Zicsr` and `A` are the chapters the conformance review and this revision
  draw on, and every machine-mode address and bit position comes from volume II §2.1.1 and the subsections
  §0.7 names, each next to the register it defines.
- Verification: [verification.md](verification.md) — tests `T-01` and `T-02`, the harness tests `T-06` and
  `T-08`..`T-53`, with `T-03` (CSR and trap),
  `T-04` (timer and interrupt) and `T-05` (debug) registered there as `deferred` and owed for the machine-mode
  and debug rows of §4; gaps `G-01` to `G-39`.
- Interview ledger: [spec-questions.md](spec-questions.md).
- Conflict resolution: when this document and the RTL disagree, open a gap; do not silently edit either.
